264 — Bundler / Transpiler (Webpack/Vite/esbuild-like)

Goal: Implement, from scratch in Rust, a production-grade JavaScript/TypeScript bundler and transpiler — a JS/TS/JSX lexer and recursive-descent + Pratt parser producing an ESTree-shaped AST, Node CommonJS + ESM module resolution (exports/imports conditions, tsconfig paths), a module dependency graph with live-binding semantics, a transform pipeline (TypeScript type-stripping, JSX, syntax lowering to a target, define replacement), tree-shaking, scope-hoisting / module concatenation, code splitting and deterministic content-hashed chunking, multi-format code generation (ESM / CJS / IIFE) with an injected module runtime, scope-aware minification, a Vite-style ESM dev server with Hot Module Replacement, and a content-addressed incremental cache. Source maps are produced through the project #265 codec. It bundles and transpiles; it is not a type checker (that is #42) and not a JavaScript engine (that is #126).

Project Number: 264

Project Name: Bundler / Transpiler (Webpack/Vite/esbuild-like)

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a deterministic JS/TS bundler + transpiler — bndl — that lexes/parses ES2022+TS+JSX, resolves Node CJS/ESM specifiers, builds a module graph, tree-shakes, scope-hoists, code-splits into content-hashed chunks, emits ESM/CJS/IIFE with an injected runtime, minifies, serves a Vite-style HMR dev server, and caches incrementally — as an embeddable crate set behind one CLI.

1.1 What is built (precise definition)

bndl = a 12-crate Rust workspace producing one binary (bndl) + an embeddable library API. Pipeline, end to end:

# Capability Crate Normative reference
1 Lex JS/TS/JSX per ECMAScript lexical grammar bndl-lexer TC39 lexical grammar
2 Parse ES2022+TS+JSX → ESTree-shaped, span-tagged AST (recursive descent + Pratt) bndl-parser ESTree, TS/JSX grammars
3 Resolve specifiers (Node CJS + ESM, exports/imports/main/module/browser, tsconfig paths/baseUrl, ext/index probing, symlink realpath) bndl-resolver Node module resolution specs
4 Build module graph (live bindings, re-exports, export *, dynamic import(), CJS interop, cycles) bndl-graph Rollup, esbuild
5 Transform (TS type-strip, JSX→jsx-runtime/createElement, Target lowering, define replacement) bndl-transform SWC, OXC, Babel
6 Tree-shake (symbol-level reachability + side-effect analysis) bndl-treeshake Rollup
7 Scope analysis + scope hoisting (module concatenation) w/ wrapped fallback bndl-codegen Rollup, esbuild
8 Code-split into deterministically content-hashed chunks (entry/dynamic/shared) bndl-chunk esbuild module groups
9 Emit ESM/CJS/IIFE w/ injected module runtime + interop helpers bndl-codegen esbuild, Webpack
10 Minify (scope-aware mangle + DCE + constant fold) bndl-codegen esbuild, Terser-class
11 Vite-style ESM dev server + HMR bndl-server Vite
12 Content-addressed incremental cache bndl-cache Turbopack/Rolldown

Source maps composed across every stage via the #265 codec — bndl never hand-rolls VLQ.

Inputs: entry file(s) + a config (+ optional Plugins). Outputs: OutputChunk{ code, map } set + a manifest. Build determinism is a hard invariant: identical inputs+options → byte-identical output (stable hashing, rename order, module ordering) — required for caching + long-term CDN caching.

1.2 System boundaries — explicit NON-goals

NON-goal Why out of scope Owner
Type checking TS types are parsed and discarded, never checked — no inference, no diagnostics, no type errors #42 Type Checker
Executing JavaScript / a JS engine bndl emits code; it does not interpret/JIT it (dev server serves to a browser, does not run JS itself) #126 JS Engine
Package install / registry No download, no lockfile, no node_modules population; consumes an existing tree #135 Package Manager
Source-map FORMAT VLQ encode/decode + map merge live in the codec; bndl only feeds it position deltas #265 Source-Map Codec
CSS parse/layout CSS treated as opaque asset module (string + dependency edges), no AST/cascade (asset boundary)

Non-goals are stated at the relevant phase, not silently assumed. Crossing a boundary (e.g. emitting a type error) is a spec violation.

1.3 Deployment model

Three consumption surfaces over one codebase:

  • Build tool — bndl build produces optimized chunks+maps+manifest for production (CI, CDN deploy).
  • Dev server — bndl serve: ESM-native on-demand transform + HMR over websocket; dependency pre-bundling for node_modules.
  • Programmatic transform API — embeddable crates (parse_program, TransformPass, emit_chunk, Resolver, ModuleGraph) called in-process by app build pipelines, test runners (transform-on-import), monorepo tooling, and framework meta-frameworks.

bndl-cli subcommands: build, serve, transform (single-file), analyze (size/treeshake report), graph (dependency graph dump). All wrap the same library — the CLI is a thin host + Plugin (resolve/load/transform/render_chunk).

1.4 Target users

  • Frontend build-tool engineers — embed/extend the bundler; need a fast, correct, hackable core.
  • Framework authors (meta-frameworks, SSR) — need the transform API + dev server + plugin hooks.
  • Monorepo platform teams — need deterministic, content-addressed incremental builds at scale.

1.5 Phase 0 deliverable — the twelve bndl-* crates

A Cargo workspace (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78), 12 members, each a compiling shell with type stubs + CI green:

  1. bndl-lexer — tokenizer: Token, TokenKind, Lexer, code-point classifier, template/regex disambiguation, ASI newline tracking, Span, SourceId.
  2. bndl-ast — ESTree AST + spans + NodeId + Visit/VisitMut traits + scope-id placeholders (Program, ModuleItem, Stmt, Expr, Pat, Ident). No parsing.
  3. bndl-parser — recursive-descent + Pratt parser; ES2022+TS+JSX; ParseError/ParseErrorKind; Syntax{typescript,jsx}; error recovery.
  4. bndl-resolver — Node CJS+ESM resolution; Resolver, ResolveOptions, Specifier, ResolveResult, ResolveError, Condition; tsconfig paths/baseUrl; caches.
  5. bndl-graph — module graph: ModuleId, Module, ModuleKind, ImportRecord, ExportRecord, SymbolId/Symbol, Binding, ModuleGraph; cycle detection; live bindings + export *.
  6. bndl-transform — TransformPass, TransformContext, Target, DefineMap; passes TypeStrip, JsxTransform, Lowering, Define; VisitMut driver + pass ordering.
  7. bndl-treeshake — SymbolGraph, Reachability, SideEffect, TreeShaker; reachable set from entry; sideEffects + pure-annotation analysis; dead elimination.
  8. bndl-chunk — Chunk, ChunkId, ChunkGraph, ChunkingAlgo, OutputFormat; module-group assignment; deterministic naming + content hashing; cross-chunk imports.
  9. bndl-codegen — Emitter/Printer, injected runtime (__require/__esm/__commonJS/__toESM/__export), format wrappers, ScopeTree, Renamer, scope hoisting, Minifier, OutputChunk; maps via #265.
  10. bndl-cache — CacheKey (BLAKE3), Cache, Artifact; memoize parse/transform/codegen; on-disk store; change-driven invalidation.
  11. bndl-server — DevServer, HmrEngine, HmrBoundary, Watcher, ModuleRunner; ESM on-demand transform; HMR boundary + propagation; websocket protocol + client runtime; full-reload fallback.
  12. bndl-cli — the bndl binary: build/serve/transform/analyze/graph; config loader + Plugin host.

Phase 0 also ships: ParseError/ResolveError shells, CLI scaffold, CI (fmt/clippy/build/test), Makefile/Dockerfile, the smoke test ("bundle a.js that imports b.js into one ESM file"), and the differential-oracle scaffold (esbuild/Rollup behind a dev-dependency/feature for output comparison).

1.6 What later phases unlock

Phases Unlocks
1–3 (## 13–## 15) Full JS/TS/JSX front end: lexer → parser → TS+JSX gating; correct AST + spans
4–5 (## 16–## 17) Resolution + module graph: real node_modules/tsconfig resolution, live bindings, cycles, parallel load
6–7 (## 18–## 19) Transform pipeline + tree-shaking: type-strip, JSX, lowering, define; symbol-level dead-code removal
8–9 (## 20–## 21) Scope hoisting + code splitting: module concatenation, shared/dynamic chunks, content hashing, manifest
10–11 (## 22–## 23) Codegen + minification: ESM/CJS/IIFE output, interop, source maps, mangle/DCE/fold
12 (## 24) Dev server + HMR: fast iterative dev loop
13 (## 25) Incremental cache + the conformance/perf done gate (Test262 parse, differential vs esbuild/Rollup, fuzzing, criterion benches)

1.7 Deferred features — PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section X")

Gated behind an explicit marker; absent in v1, no silent partial impl:

Deferred feature Gate

CSS Modules (scoped class hashing) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §17")
Non-JS asset pipelines (image/font transforms, inlining) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §17")

1.8 Estimated timeline

  • Phase 0: 6–14 weeks (workspace, 12 shells, type stubs, smoke test, differential-oracle scaffold, CI).
  • Full project (Phases 1–13): several months for a single experienced engineer; the front end (1–3) and the graph+treeshake+chunk+codegen core (5–11) are the dominant cost. The done gate (13) gates ship.

1.9 Recommended language — Rust (rationale)

Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78; mostly std (fs/threads/watch); lexer/parser/AST core is allocation-aware (arena/bump).

  • Memory safety on an untrusted hot path — the parser consumes arbitrary source; a bug must not be a memory-safety exploit. Non-negotiable.
  • Sum types + exhaustive match — model the ESTree AST (Expr/Stmt/Pat) and the transform fold/visitor with zero-cost abstractions; the compiler proves every node variant is handled.
  • Bump/arena alloc + Send+Sync immutable Modules — cheap allocation in the per-file hot loop; fearless parallel module processing across a thread pool.
  • Reference fidelity — the fast real-world references (SWC, OXC, Rolldown, Turbopack) are all Rust → reference reading maps 1:1.

Rejected: Go/esbuild (GC pauses on huge graphs, weaker sum types for the AST); TypeScript (self-hosting irony, no allocation/parallelism control, slow); C/C++ (memory-unsafe on the parsing hot path).

1.10 Required prerequisites

  • ECMAScript lexical grammar + module grammar (imports/exports, dynamic import(), ASI rules).
  • Recursive-descent + Pratt expression parsing (binding powers, regex-vs-divide context).
  • Node CJS/ESM resolution (PACKAGE_RESOLVE, PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE, ESM_FILE_FORMAT, LOAD_AS_FILE/LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY/LOAD_NODE_MODULES).
  • Dataflow / reachability analysis (worklist algorithms for tree-shaking).
  • Scope analysis (binding resolution, scope trees, collision-free renaming).
  • Content-addressed caching (BLAKE3 hashing, memoization, invalidation).
  • Thread-pool parallelism (work distribution over immutable shared state).

2. Why Build This

The build tool is the compiler of the modern web. Every shipped JS/TS app passes through a bundler: resolve thousands of modules, strip types, lower syntax, prune dead code, split chunks, mangle names, emit maps. bndl reconstructs that pipeline from scratch — a whole-program compiler over a module graph, not a file-at-a-time transpiler. This is the single most architecturally rich frontend-tooling project in the portfolio: it touches lexing, recursive-descent + Pratt parsing, name resolution, dataflow, dead-code analysis, register-allocation-flavored renaming, deterministic output, and an incremental + parallel + live-reload runtime — all under the constraint that the input is untrusted and the output must be byte-identical across runs.

2.1 Unique Systems Knowledge

Each capability teaches a transferable compiler/systems skill not isolated elsewhere in the portfolio:

Capability Systems lesson Sound only when…
Whole-program dataflow over a module graph Global reachability across compilation units; the graph (not the file) is the unit of analysis Edges resolved before analysis; cycles recorded, not infinite-looped
Live-binding semantics (ESM) An import is a read-only view of an exported cell, not a value copy; mutation visibility + identity must survive renaming/concat Concatenation/renaming preserve binding identity, not just value
Tree-shaking soundness Symbol-level reachability + side-effect proof; removal is a correctness decision, not an optimization Effect proven absent (sideEffects:false, /*#__PURE__*/, no TLA effect) — over-removal = silent bug
Scope hoisting + collision-free renaming Scope-correct α-renaming across merged scopes; graph-coloring-style short-name assignment Module needs no wrapper (no eval, no top-level CJS, not in lazy-init cycle)
Module-group code splitting Chunk = function of the set of reaching entries (static+dynamic); shared code factored to common chunk Reachable-entry-set computed per module; dynamic import() = hard boundary
Deterministic content-hashed output Reproducible builds; fixed-point hashing when chunks reference each other's hashes Stable module order + rename order + hash-of-final-content
CJS/ESM interop Bridging value-snapshot semantics (module.exports at require time) to live bindings via interop helpers __toESM/__commonJS/__esm model namespace + default + circular init correctly
Incremental + parallel compilation Content-addressed memoization (BLAKE3) + Send+Sync immutable modules across a thread pool Cache key = content + options; arena-owned ASTs shared read-only
HMR boundary propagation Reverse-dependency graph walk to the nearest accepting boundary; full-reload fallback Boundary found before module root; otherwise reload

Cross-cutting invariants the project forces a builder to internalize:

  • Source-map composition: every stage (lex → parse → transform → print → minify) emits a position delta; the final map is the composition of per-stage deltas via the #265 codec. Never hand-roll VLQ. A renamer/minifier that forgets to compose silently breaks debuggability.
  • Determinism as a contract: identical input + options ⇒ byte-identical bytes. Underwrites caching, CDN long-term caching, and the differential oracle. Any iteration-order leak (HashMap, fs readdir, thread completion order) is a defect.
  • Untrusted input on the hot path: the lexer/parser must never panic — recover via Invalid tokens / ParseError. Memory safety is non-negotiable, which is why Rust (arena/bump-allocated, exhaustive match over the AST enums).

2.2 Why Bundlers Matter — Historical + Architectural

Browsers had no module system until ES2015, and ESM shipped slowly; meanwhile npm produced CommonJS graphs of tens of thousands of files. The bundler emerged to (1) make those graphs loadable, (2) shrink them for the wire, and (3) compile not-yet-supported syntax down to deployable targets. It is, structurally, an optimizing compiler with a filesystem-aware resolver front end and a chunk-graph back end.

Canonical prior art bndl is modeled on (cite consistently throughout the blueprint):

Tool The idea it pioneered What bndl borrows
Webpack Loaders/plugins, the chunk graph, an injected runtime; "everything is a module" Plugin hook model (resolve/load/transform/render_chunk), chunk graph, module runtime
Rollup ESM-first, symbol-level tree-shaking, scope hoisting (flat bundle), the chunk graph Tree-shaking soundness, module concatenation, ESM-first output
esbuild Go-speed parallel bundling, module-group code splitting, fast scope hoisting Parallel module processing, module-group splitting algorithm, interop-helper runtime
Vite ESM-native unbundled dev server, dependency pre-bundling, HMR bndl-server dev mode, pre-bundling, HMR boundary protocol
SWC / OXC Rust JS/TS parser + transform (visitor/fold) at native speed bndl-parser/bndl-transform architecture, Visit/VisitMut, arena AST
Babel The transform-pass + plugin paradigm TransformPass ordering, type-strip/JSX/lower/define passes

Normative specs (treated as ground truth, not approximations): the TC39 ECMAScript spec (lexical grammar + modules), the TypeScript and JSX grammars, and the Node.js CJS (LOAD_AS_FILE/LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY/LOAD_NODE_MODULES) + ESM (PACKAGE_RESOLVE, PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE, ESM_FILE_FORMAT) resolution algorithms.

The architectural thesis: a bundler is the place where parsing, name resolution, dataflow, codegen, and caching all meet over a graph too large to hold naively — so it is the best single vehicle for learning how those phases compose under real performance and correctness pressure.

2.3 Engineering Muscles Built

  • Hand-written lexer with ASI newline tracking + regex-vs-divide disambiguation (lexer needs parser-fed context — a textbook lexer/parser coupling problem).
  • Recursive descent for statements/declarations + Pratt binding-power parsing for expressions; principled error recovery, not error abort.
  • Graph algorithms in anger: cycle detection, reachability worklists, reverse-dependency walks (HMR), reaching-set computation (chunking), fixed-point iteration (hash stabilization).
  • Scope analysis + α-renaming with collision-free short-name allocation (the minifier's renamer is graph-coloring-adjacent).
  • Designing for determinism and incrementality from day one (content addressing, stable orderings) rather than retrofitting.
  • Fearless parallelism: immutable Send+Sync modules + a content-addressed cache, exercising Rust's ownership model on a real concurrent workload.
  • Spec archaeology: turning prose algorithms (Node resolution, ESM module linking) into executable, test-conformant code.

2.4 Cross-Links to Portfolio Projects

bndl sits at a hub of the language-tooling portfolio; it consumes some projects and is the natural sibling of others:

Project Relationship
#265 Source Map Generator Dependency. bndl-codegen emits maps through the #265 codec; this blueprint never re-specifies the source-map format or VLQ.
#42 Type Checker Non-goal boundary. bndl parses and discards TS types (type-stripping); it never type-checks. Checking is #42.
#126 JS Engine Non-goal boundary. bndl emits JS; it never executes it. Evaluation is #126.
#135 Package Manager Non-goal boundary. bndl reads node_modules/package.json; it never installs. Installation is #135.
#259 JS Parser Sibling/shared lineage of bndl-lexer+bndl-parser; same ESTree/Pratt foundations.
#127 Tree-Sitter Incremental Parser Conceptual cousin to bndl-cache incremental rebuild + the dev-server re-parse path.
#62 Lexer / #63 Parser Combinators Foundational — the lexing/parsing primitives bndl-lexer/bndl-parser apply at scale.
#143 SSA Optimizer Conceptual cousin to tree-shaking/DCE/constant-folding (reachability + effect analysis as optimization).
#136 Build System Orchestration cousin — content-addressed caching + dependency-driven incremental rebuilds, here specialized to JS.

2.5 Calibration Value

This project calibrates a staff/principal-level ability to build an optimizing, parallel, incremental, reproducible compiler over a large dependency graph from primitive parts — and to draw hard scope boundaries (no checking, no execution, no install, no map format) so the system stays buildable. Success is not "it bundles a.js"; success is the Phase 13 done-gate: Test262 parser conformance, a differential oracle agreeing with esbuild/Rollup semantics, byte-identical reproducible output, fuzz-clean lex/parse/roundtrip, and criterion benchmarks meeting the §4 targets. Clearing that bar demonstrates command of every phase a production frontend toolchain depends on.

What Mastery Looks Like

A builder who has completed all 13 phases and cleared the Phase 13 done-gate demonstrates the following concrete competencies:

  • Phase 1 (§13) — Lexer mastery: Can explain why regex-vs-divide disambiguation requires parser-fed context and why a stateless DFA cannot express this; can trace an ASI insertion from had_newline_before flag to the token stream decision; can describe the never-panic Invalid-token recovery contract and its effect on downstream error messages.
  • Phase 2–3 (§14–§15) — Parser mastery: Can implement a new Pratt precedence level from a TC39 proposal without touching the statement parser; can add an error-recovery resync point that catches a common mistake while still parsing the rest of the file; can explain why TypeScript annotations are parsed-and-discarded at this layer and what would break if they were propagated.
  • Phase 4 (§16) — Resolution mastery: Can walk through PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE with a conditional-export pattern including a *-glob match; can explain the difference between tsconfig paths resolution (before node_modules) and Node's bare-specifier algorithm; can diagnose a silent symlink-realpath cache collision.
  • Phase 5 (§17) — Module graph mastery: Can distinguish a Tarjan SCC-detected cycle from an acyclic import and explain which modules fall back to wrapped lazy-init; can explain why Module must be Send+Sync and how the arena allocation makes that trivial; can trace a export * from "x" binding through bindings.rs to its resolved symbol.
  • Phase 6–7 (§18–§19) — Transform + tree-shake mastery: Can explain why over-removal in tree-shaking is a correctness bug and not an optimization; can trace a /*#__PURE__*/ annotation from source text through SideEffect::Pure in SymbolGraph to a removed call expression; can describe the pass-ordering constraint that requires TypeStrip before Lowering.
  • Phase 8–11 (§20–§23) — Codegen mastery: Can explain what "scope hoisting" does to the namespace of two merged ESM modules and exactly how Renamer prevents collision; can trace a source position through all four stage maps (lex→parse→transform→print) to a final VLQ segment in the output .map; can explain why the minifier must run after renaming, not before.
  • Phase 12–13 (§24–§25) — Cache + HMR mastery: Can explain why content-addressing is correct across branch switches where mtime caching is not; can trace an HMR boundary propagation from a changed module through the importer walk to the accepting HmrBoundary and describe the full-reload fallback condition; can derive the incremental rebuild cost model (O(changed subgraph), not O(M)) from the cache key design.

3. Architectural Overview

bndl is a 12-crate Cargo workspace. Data flows one direction on the build path (entries → resolve+load → graph → shake → chunk → emit); the dev path (bndl-server) re-enters the same pipeline on-demand per request and per fs event. The content-addressed cache (bndl-cache) wraps the three expensive memoizable stages (parse, transform, codegen). Three boundaries — trust, concurrency, persistence — partition the system and are referenced by every later phase.

3.1 Component Diagram

                          bndl (CLI) · config · Plugin host (resolve/load/transform/render)
              build                         serve (dev)                  transform · analyze · graph
                |                               |                                 |
                v                               v                                 v
        +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |  Orchestrator: entries -> resolve+load -> module graph -> shake -> chunk -> emit   |
        +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
            |  resolve specifier                                   |  watch + HMR
            v                                                      v
        bndl-resolver  --abs paths-->  bndl-graph  <----------  bndl-server (on-demand ESM, HMR)
            ^  package.json/tsconfig        |  Module{ast,imports,exports,symbols}
            |                               v
        bndl-parser  <--Token stream--  bndl-lexer            bndl-transform (TypeStrip·JSX·Lower·Define)
            |  ESTree AST + spans                                   |  rewritten AST
            v                                                       v
        bndl-treeshake (reachable symbols) --> bndl-chunk (module-group split) --> bndl-codegen
                                                                                   (Renamer·hoist·
                                                                                    Minifier·print)
                                                                                        |
                                                                                        v
                                                                  OutputChunk{code, .map(->#265)} + manifest
        bndl-cache (BLAKE3 content-addressed) wraps parse / transform / codegen artifacts

  == TRUST ==  untrusted source text · untrusted node_modules (package.json, .js/.ts) · config + plugins
               cross at: bndl-lexer/parser (source), bndl-resolver (package.json), bndl-cli (config/plugins)
  == CONCURRENCY == thread pool (rayon). Per-module parse+transform parallel; Module is Send+Sync &
               immutable post-build; per-chunk codegen parallel; resolver/graph mutate behind locks only.
  == PERSISTENCE == bndl-cache on-disk CAS (BLAKE3) + in-mem layer · output OutputChunk + manifest to disk.

3.2 Boundaries

Boundary Where it sits Rule enforced Phase ref
Trust bndl-lexer/bndl-parser (source text); bndl-resolver (package.json/tsconfig from node_modules); bndl-cli (config + plugin code) Lexer/parser never panic on any byte sequence → emit Invalid token / ParseError + recover. Resolver treats every package.json field as adversarial (InvalidExports, BadTsPaths). All untrusted input is bounded (max nesting, max size). 1, 2, 3, 4; Threat Model
Concurrency thread pool over module set; per-chunk codegen Module is immutable + Send+Sync once built → fearless parallel reads. Mutation (graph edges, resolver caches) only behind Mutex/DashMap during the build phase, never during shake/chunk/emit. Determinism independent of thread schedule (§3.15). 5, 9, 10
Persistence bndl-cache CAS; output dir CacheKey = BLAKE3(content ‖ options) is the only identity; artifacts are pure functions of their key → safe to share/evict. Output writes are atomic (temp + rename). 13

The pipeline is pull-based on the dev path, push-based on build: build materializes the whole graph then shakes/chunks/emits; serve resolves+transforms exactly the modules the browser requests, deferring shake/chunk (ESM-native, no bundling) and using HMR for incremental updates.

3.3 bndl-lexer

ECMAScript/TS/JSX tokenizer. In: &str source + SourceId. Out: Token stream (TokenKind, Span, had_newline_before). Owns the code-point classifier (ID_Start/ID_Continue, whitespace, line terminators), template-literal state stack, regex-vs-divide disambiguation (resolved with parser-fed context flag), numeric/bigint literal scanning, JSX text/ident modes. Invariant: total — every byte sequence yields a finite token run ending in Eof; lexical errors surface as Invalid tokens, never panics. had_newline_before is the sole ASI signal the parser consumes. Referenced by Phase 1 (impl), Phase 2 (parser pulls tokens), Phase 3 (JSX/TS token shapes).

3.4 bndl-parser

Recursive-descent (statements/decls) + Pratt (expressions, binding powers) parser. In: token stream via parse_program(src, id, syntax) -> (Program, Vec<ParseError>). Out: ESTree-shaped Program with Span+NodeId on every node, plus a Vec<ParseError> (ParseErrorKind). Syntax{typescript, jsx} gates grammar extensions. Invariant: always returns a Program (error-recovery panic mode re-syncs at statement boundaries) so downstream stages and the IDE/dev server get a best -effort tree; spans are byte-accurate (source-map + diagnostics correctness). Reads bndl-lexer + bndl-ast. Referenced by Phase 2 (core grammar), Phase 3 (TS/JSX), and the differential parser-conformance gate (Phase 13, Test262).

3.5 bndl-ast

The ESTree-shaped AST + Visit/VisitMut visitor traits. In/Out: pure data — Program, ModuleItem, Stmt, Expr, Pat, Ident{sym:Atom}, NodeId, Span; no parsing logic. Identifiers carry interned Atoms (cheap eq/hash, dedup). Holds scope-id placeholders filled later by bndl-codegen's ScopeTree. Invariant: the single shared node vocabulary — lexer/parser produce it, transform/treeshake/codegen consume and rewrite it; NodeId is stable across passes for source-map provenance and cache keying. VisitMut is the only sanctioned in-place rewrite path (Phase 6 driver builds on it).

3.6 bndl-resolver

Module resolution implementing both Node algorithms: ESM (PACKAGE_RESOLVE, PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE with conditional + pattern matching, ESM_FILE_FORMAT) and CJS (LOAD_AS_FILE/LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY/LOAD_NODE_MODULES). In: Specifier (Relative/Absolute/Bare{pkg,sub}) + importer path + ResolveOptions. Out: ResolveResult{path, kind, side_effects, from_node_modules} or ResolveError. Matches Conditions against exports/imports; honors main/module/browser main-fields; resolves tsconfig paths/baseUrl before node_modules; does extension + index probing; canonicalizes via symlink realpath. Caches fs stats, parsed package.json, and realpath. Invariant: resolution is a deterministic pure function of (specifier, importer, options, fs snapshot) — same inputs → same ResolveResult. Feeds absolute paths to bndl-graph; Phase 4 owns it.

3.7 bndl-graph

Builds the module graph. In: entry paths + the resolver. Out: ModuleGraph (Vec<Module>, entries, edges, recorded cycles). Each Module holds ast, ImportRecords (Static/Dynamic/Require/ExportStar), ExportRecords (local symbol or reexport:(ModuleId,Atom)), Symbols, and has_side_effects. Classifies ModuleKind (Esm/Cjs/Json/Css/Asset/Builtin/External); scans ESM import/export + CJS require/module.exports; detects cycles; models live bindings (an import is a read-only view of the exported binding — identity preserved), re-exports, and export-star expansion. Loads modules in parallel on the thread pool. Invariant: every resolved edge points to a real ModuleId; Module is immutable + Send+Sync after construction. Reads bndl-parser + bndl-resolver. Consumed by treeshake (Phase 7), chunk (Phase 9), codegen, and the dev server.

3.8 bndl-transform

AST rewrite pipeline. In: &mut Program + TransformContext{target, defines, syntax, jsx_runtime}. Out: rewritten Program (same NodeId lineage for maps). Passes, ordered: TypeStrip (erase TS annotations/interfaces/enums/decorators — no type checking, that is #42) → JsxTransform (Classic createElement or Automatic jsx-runtime) → Lowering (optional chaining, nullish, class fields, logical-assignment, async per Target) → Define (constant replacement from DefineMap, enabling later DCE). Driven by a VisitMut driver. Invariant: transforms are semantics-preserving and target-correct; ordering is fixed (e.g. type-strip before JSX before lowering). Implements TransformPass. Reads bndl-ast; Phase 6 owns it; the dev server runs it per-module on-demand.

3.9 bndl-treeshake

Symbol-level reachability. In: &mut ModuleGraph + entry ModuleIds via shake(graph, entries) -> ReachableSet. Out: the set of live (module, symbol), with dead statements/exports/imports eliminated and re-exports pruned. Builds a SymbolGraph (per-symbol dependency edges + side-effect markers); worklist reachability from entry exports; side-effect analysis honoring sideEffects:false and /*#__PURE__*/ (SideEffect::Pure/Impure/Unknown). Invariant — soundness: never remove code with observable side effects unless proven pure; top-level effectful statements pin their module; over-removal is a correctness bug, not an optimization. Reads bndl-graph; output drives bndl-chunk. Phase 7.

3.10 bndl-chunk

Code splitting via esbuild/Rollup module groups. In: split(graph, reachable, fmt) -> ChunkGraph. Out: ChunkGraph (Vec<Chunk>) + manifest. A module's chunk is fixed by the set of entry points (static + dynamic) that can reach it: single-entry → that entry's chunk; multi-entry → a shared chunk. import() creates a chunk boundary (is_dynamic). Wires cross-chunk imports (Chunk.imports); names chunks deterministically; content-hashes (Chunk.hash) with a fixed-point pass so cross-chunk hash references converge. Invariant — determinism: identical (graph, reachable, options) → byte-identical chunk set, stable order + names + hashes (long-term CDN caching). Reads bndl-graph + bndl-treeshake. Phase 9; consumed per-chunk by codegen.

3.11 bndl-codegen

Emits final code; owns four subsystems. In: emit_chunk(chunk, graph, fmt, opts) -> OutputChunk{file_name, code, map}. (1) ScopeTree + Renamer — scope analysis binds every reference; the cross-module Renamer produces collision-free names (also short names for minify). (2) Scope hoisting / module concatenation — hoist ESM modules into one shared scope (Rollup/esbuild-style); wrapped lazy-init fallback (a runtime function with first-call memoization) for CJS, cyclic, or eval-bearing modules. (3) Module runtime + OutputFormat wrappers — inject __require/__esm/ __commonJS/__toESM/__export interop helpers; wrap as Esm/Cjs/Iife; rewrite imports/exports incl. CJS↔ESM interop (CJS = value snapshot at require-time, ESM = live binding). (4) Minifier — scope-aware mangle + DCE + constant fold + dead-branch removal (Phase 11). Source maps composed across all stages via the #265 codec (never hand-rolls VLQ). Invariant: output is semantics-preserving and deterministic; live- binding identity + mutation-visibility survive renaming/hoisting. Reads bndl-ast + bndl-chunk. Phases 8, 10, 11.

3.12 bndl-cache

Content-addressed incremental store. In: content + options → CacheKey([u8;32]) = BLAKE3(content ‖ options). Out: memoized Artifact (Parsed/Transformed/ Emitted). In-memory layer over a persistent on-disk CAS; change-driven invalidation (a changed source → new key → miss → recompute; unchanged deps hit). Invariant: an artifact is a pure function of its key → cache hits are observationally identical to recompute; eviction is always safe. Wraps the three expensive stages (parse/transform/ codegen) and underpins incremental rebuild + the dev server (Phase 13).

3.13 bndl-server

Vite-style ESM dev server + HMR. In: entries + config; HTTP/WS clients; fs events via Watcher. Out: per-request transformed ESM modules; WS HMR updates. Owns DevServer (http + ws + transform pipeline + cache + watcher), HmrEngine (boundary computation + accepted-modules set), HmrBoundary{module, accepts}, ModuleRunner. Serves modules on-demand (ESM-native, no bundling in dev) with dependency pre-bundling of node_modules; on change, computes the HMR boundary, propagates up the graph to the nearest accepting module, and falls back to full reload when no boundary accepts. Invariant: dev transform output is byte-consistent with the build path's per-module transform (same bndl-transform + cache). Reads bndl-graph + bndl-transform + bndl-codegen + bndl-cache. Phase 12.

3.14 bndl-cli

The bndl binary + plugin host. In: run(args: CliArgs) -> ExitCode — subcommands build, serve, transform, analyze, graph. Loads config; hosts the Plugin trait (resolve/load/transform/render_chunk hooks) — plugins extend each pipeline stage. Out: orchestration of the whole pipeline; written OutputChunks + manifest (build), the dev server (serve), a single transformed file (transform), graph/size reports (analyze/graph). Invariant: the orchestration layer — the only crate depending on all others; the plugin host is the single extension surface and runs inside the trust boundary (plugin code is treated as untrusted config). Phase 0 scaffolds it; every later phase wires its stage in.

3.15 Determinism Contract

A cross-cutting invariant verified in Phase 13: identical inputs + options ⇒ byte-identical output, independent of thread schedule. Enforced by (1) stable module ordering (by resolution order, not completion order), (2) stable identifier-rename order (Renamer keyed on NodeId/scope position), (3) stable chunk hashing with a fixed-point pass over cross-chunk hash references, (4) deterministic chunk naming. Required for cache correctness and long-term CDN caching; any nondeterminism is a release-blocking bug.

4. System Constraints

Binding non-functional contract. Prime directive: a malformed or malicious input file MUST NEVER panic, hang, or OOM bndl. Every parse/resolve/transform path returns Result or recovers (Invalid token / ParseError); no unwrap/expect/panic!/unreachable! on the input-driven hot path (bndl-lexer, bndl-parser, bndl-resolver, bndl-graph, bndl-transform). Unbounded recursion → iterative or depth-capped. All limits below are caps, not assertions: hitting one is a typed error, never a crash.

4.1 Performance Targets

Measured on 8 physical cores (16 threads), NVMe SSD, warm OS page cache unless "cold". "LOC" = non-blank source lines. Targets are p50 unless a percentile is named.

# Metric Target Method
P1 Lex+parse throughput > 1M LOC/s/core single-thread criterion, real TS corpus (TypeScript repo)
P2 Cold full build, 10k-module graph < 5 s on 8 cores end-to-end bndl build, empty cache
P3 Incremental rebuild, 1 changed module < 50 ms edit-rebuild loop, warm bndl-cache
P4 Dev-server on-demand transform, 1 module < 15 ms p95 bndl-server request→response, warm
P5 Cold CLI start → first FS hit < 50 ms arg parse + config load, before any module read
P6 HMR update propagation (boundary→ws push) < 30 ms p95 fs event → client accept message
P7 Tree-shake + chunk, 10k modules < 800 ms shake+split, post-graph
P8 Minify throughput > 500k LOC/s/core Minifier mangle+DCE+fold pass
P9 Resolution (cached) < 1 µs per specifier Resolver warm fs/realpath cache
P10 Resolution (cold, w/ package.json read) < 200 µs first resolve into a node_modules pkg

Scaling: cold build wall-clock scales ~linearly to cores−1 (one core reserved for the orchestrator). Per-stage budget of the 10k-module cold build: resolve+load+parse ~60%, transform ~15%, shake+chunk ~16%, codegen+minify+map ~9%.

4.2 Memory Ceilings

Resource Ceiling Why
Resident graph (AST+Module+symbols) < 6× total source bytes bound peak RSS; enables 10k-module builds on dev laptops
Per-module AST arena/bump-allocated, freed as one block post-codegen no per-node Box churn; Atom interner dedups identifiers
Atom interner global, dedup; ≤ source-bytes order, never per-occurrence identifier-heavy code stays sublinear
Single-file parse peak ≤ ~3× that file's bytes arena reset between files
bndl-cache in-memory LRU configurable, default 512 MB; spills to on-disk store bound RSS; disk is source of truth
Source-map builder streaming via #265 codec; mappings not all resident large bundles don't blow RSS

Modules are immutable + Send+Sync once built → shared by & across the thread pool, zero clone. Arena (bump) for parse means allocation is pointer-bump; whole-AST drop is O(1) region free.

4.3 Determinism

Identical (inputs, options, bndl version) → byte-identical output chunks + manifest + maps, across runs, machines, OSes, and core counts. Enforced by:

  • Stable module ordering: by resolved absolute path, not fs-enumeration / thread-completion order.
  • Stable identifier-rename order in Renamer: scope-tree pre-order, deterministic short-name sequence.
  • Stable chunk hashing: BLAKE3 of final chunk content; cross-chunk hash references resolved via a fixed-point pass (hash → rewrite import URLs → re-hash until stable, cap 8 iterations → else NonConvergentHash error).
  • No wall-clock, no PID, no RNG, no HashMap iteration order in output paths (use sorted keys / IndexMap).
  • Parallelism affects only speed, never bytes: results merged in deterministic order regardless of completion order.

Determinism is required for bndl-cache correctness and long-term CDN caching (content-hashed filenames).

4.4 Concurrency Assumptions

  • Thread pool sized cores−1; orchestrator coordinates, workers do resolve/parse/transform per module.
  • Parallel unit = one module; bndl-graph load is a fan-out of independent parse+scan jobs joined at edge-wiring.
  • Shared state is read-mostly: Resolver caches behind concurrent maps; Cache behind a sharded lock; Atom interner lock-free read / locked insert.
  • No data races by construction: immutable Module, no &mut aliasing across threads (Rust borrow checker is the enforcement).
  • bndl-server HMR + watcher run off the request path; invalidation is message-passed, not shared-mutex on the graph.

4.5 Input Assumptions, Hard Caps, and Rationale

Defaults; some overridable via config but ALL have a hard ceiling (DoS + memory bound). Exceeding → typed error, clean abort, no panic.

Dimension Assumed (typical) Hard cap Error / Rationale
Single source file size ≤ 5 MB 64 MB FileTooLarge — bound parse arena
Module count / graph ≤ 10k 1,000,000 (ModuleId = u32) GraphTooLarge — bound RSS + u32 id space
Parser nesting depth (expr/stmt/paren/JSX) ≤ 200 1,000 ParseError(NestingTooDeep) — stack-overflow DoS via ((((…
Import/resolution depth ≤ 100 2,048 ResolveError — pathological dep chains
Template/JSX nesting ≤ 64 512 bound lexer template-stack
Chunk count — 65,536 TooManyChunks — bound manifest + fixed-point hash cost
Symbols / module — u32 (SymbolId) id-space bound
Cyclic-import handling any detected + recorded, never infinite-loops worklist with visited-set
Hash fixed-point iterations 1–3 8 NonConvergentHash — bound rehash cost

Parser recursion is explicitly depth-counted (not relying on native stack); deep input yields NestingTooDeep, never SIGSEGV. Lexer always terminates: every code path advances the byte cursor or emits Eof/Invalid (no zero-progress loop).

4.6 Cache & Incremental Targets

  • Warm incremental rebuild cache hit rate > 95% (unchanged modules served from bndl-cache by CacheKey = BLAKE3(content + options)).
  • Invalidation is content-driven + transitive: a changed module invalidates its Artifacts and dependents whose resolved graph shape changed; pure leaf edits invalidate only that module + downstream chunks.
  • On-disk store survives process restart; cold start with populated store ≈ warm (re-validate by hash, skip recompute).

4.7 I/O, FD, and Network Budget

  • No network at runtime. Resolution is registry-less: filesystem only (no installs/fetches; package install is #135). External/builtin specifiers are marked external, not fetched.
  • Dev server: localhost HTTP + WebSocket only (HMR). No outbound connections.
  • Watcher FD budget: native recursive fs-watch where available (FSEvents/inotify/ReadDirectoryChangesW); fall back to a polling watcher above an open-handle cap. Hard cap ≤ 8,192 watched paths/handles → beyond it, watch parent dirs coarsely (WatchBudgetExceeded downgrade, not abort).
  • File reads go through Resolver/Cache fs layer (stat+realpath cached) → bound syscall count; symlink realpath cached to avoid repeat lstat walks.

4.8 Robustness Invariants (cross-cutting)

  • No panic on any input — fuzzed (lex/parse/roundtrip, Phase 13); fuzz target = "process N MB of arbitrary bytes, assert no panic/hang/leak."
  • Bounded time + space for any single input ≤ caps above; no input drives super-linear blowup without hitting a cap first.
  • Graceful degradation: unparseable module → ParseError collected, module marked errored, build reports + continues (or fails fast per config) — never takes down the process.
  • Type stripping is not type checking (#42): malformed TS types are discarded/recovered, never block bundling of valid runtime code.

5. Required Reading

Curated, load-bearing corpus. Read order: Books §5.1 (theory) -> Specs §5.4 (normative ground truth) -> Reference Impls §5.3 (how the fast tools actually do it) -> Papers/Articles §5.2 (design rationale gluing them). Each entry: source, exact locus, why it matters here, what to extract. Skip anything not tied to a phase below.

5.1 Books

# Book / chapters Maps to phase Extract
B1 Nystrom, Crafting Interpreters — Ch. 4 Scanning; Ch. 6 Parsing Expressions (Pratt); Ch. 16–17 Compiling Expressions / Pratt table Ph1 bndl-lexer, Ph2 bndl-parser Pratt binding-power table -> our Pratt Expr parser; scanner state machine -> Lexer code-point classifier; error-token recovery shape -> Invalid/ParseError. Take the Pratt mental model; ignore the bytecode VM (no JS execution — #126).
B2 Aho/Lam/Sethi/Ullman, Compilers: Principles, Techniques & Tools (Dragon Book) — §3.1–3.4, 3.6–3.9 lexical analysis + DFA/maximal-munch; §4.4 recursive-descent / predictive; §9.2, 9.4 dataflow & liveness; §9.5 partial-redundancy / dead-code Ph1, Ph2, Ph7 bndl-treeshake, Ph11 Minifier Maximal-munch + longest-match -> regex-vs-divide & template lexing; liveness/reachability lattice -> symbol-level Reachability worklist + DCE. Read dataflow as the formal frame for tree-shaking soundness; the codegen chapters are register-alloc, skip.
B3 Cooper & Torczon, Engineering a Compiler, 2e — Ch. 5 IR; Ch. 8 §8.1–8.6 scalar opt (constant prop/fold, DCE, useless-code elim); Ch. 9 §9.3 dominators/SSA; Ch. 13 reg-alloc (skim only) Ph7, Ph8 scope hoisting, Ph11 Constant-folding + useless-code algorithms -> Minifier fold/DCE; SSA-style def-use -> ScopeTree reference binding & Renamer collision-freedom; dominance intuition -> dead-branch removal. Don't build full SSA — we rewrite the AST in place, not a CFG.
B4 Appel, Modern Compiler Implementation in ML/C — Ch. 1–3 lexing/parsing/AST; Ch. 5 semantic analysis / symbol tables & scopes; Ch. 15 functional-style intermediate forms (skim) Ph2, Ph8 Nested-scope symbol-table discipline -> ScopeTree build + binding resolution + shadowing; AST-as-tree visitor shape -> Visit/VisitMut. The scope/environment chapter is the keystone for hoisting correctness.
B5 Grune et al., Parsing Techniques: A Practical Guide, 2e — Ch. 2 grammars; Ch. 8 deterministic top-down (LL, recursive descent); §16.x error recovery & resynchronization Ph2, Ph3 TS/JSX Panic-mode + follow-set resynchronization -> ParseError recovery that never panics on untrusted input; ambiguity handling -> regex-vs-divide, < JSX-vs-less-than, arrow-vs-paren cover-grammar.
B6 Crockford, JavaScript: The Good Parts — App. A/B (ASI, the grammar's sharp edges) Ph1, Ph2 ASI restricted-production hazards (return\n, ++, [/( continuation) -> had_newline_before driven ASI; precise hazard list as parser test oracle. Short, normative-adjacent.
B7 Muchnick, Advanced Compiler Design and Implementation — Ch. 12 constant folding/prop; Ch. 18 control/data-flow analysis (reference, not cover-to-cover) Ph7, Ph11 Backup/depth reference for fold soundness and effect analysis when the Dragon Book is too terse; consult per-pass, do not read linearly.

5.2 Papers / Articles

# Source (author / venue / year) Core contribution Relevance here
P1 esbuild architecture — Evan Wallace, esbuild docs Architecture.md + "FAQ: Why is esbuild fast" (2020–) Single-pass, parallel-per-file, GC-light bundler design; pass fusion Blueprint for the whole orchestrator: parse/transform per module in parallel, fuse passes. We adopt the architecture, reject Go's GC model (Rust arenas instead).
P2 esbuild "module groups" code splitting — Wallace, GitHub issue #16 + CHANGELOG splitting notes Chunk = determined by set of entry points (static+dynamic) that reach a module; shared chunk for multi-entry modules The exact ChunkingAlgo for split() — reachable-entry-set partition; dynamic import() = chunk boundary. This is the §3-locked splitting model.
P3 Rollup tree-shaking — Rich Harris, "Tree-shaking versus dead code elimination" (2015) + Rollup docs tree-shaking.md ESM static structure enables symbol-level live-code inclusion (not statement-level DCE); sideEffects contract Justifies bndl-treeshake working at SymbolId granularity from entry exports; sideEffects:false + /*#__PURE__*/ as explicit contracts.
P4 Rollup scope hoisting / module concatenation — Harris, "Rollup 0.20 / module concatenation" write-ups + docs Hoist many ESM modules into one shared scope; rename to avoid collisions; wrap only when necessary Direct source for Ph8: when hoisting is sound vs. wrapped lazy-init fallback (cycles, CJS, eval).
P5 Vite design — Yuxi You, Vite docs "Why Vite" + "Dep Pre-Bundling" + "Features/HMR" (2021–) Unbundled native-ESM dev server; on-demand transform; esbuild-based dependency pre-bundling; fine-grained HMR Architecture for bndl-server: serve source as ESM, transform on request, pre-bundle node_modules.
P6 Vite/HMR boundary semantics — Vite HMR API docs (import.meta.hot.accept) + ESM-HMR proposal accept/dispose boundaries; propagation up the importer graph; full-reload fallback Defines HmrEngine boundary computation + HmrBoundary{accepts} + propagation in Ph12.
P7 SWC architecture — DongYoon Kang, "Speeding up the JavaScript ecosystem / SWC" + SWC design blog Rust visitor/fold transform model; interned Atom; high-throughput parser Pattern for TransformPass/VisitMut driver, Atom string interning, parser perf. Study the fold ordering.
P8 OXC architecture — Boshen et al., "OXC — The JavaScript Oxidation Compiler" blog series Allocation-aware (bump-arena) parser, span-tagged ESTree-ish AST, resolver design Closest 1:1 reference: arena AST, Span/NodeId layout, oxc_resolver algorithm shape.
P9 Webpack chunk graph & runtime — webpack docs "Under The Hood" + "Module Federation"/runtime notes + Tobias Koppers talks ChunkGraph abstraction; injected module runtime (__webpack_require__, chunk loading) Contrast model for ChunkGraph + the injected module runtime (__require/__esm/__commonJS); take the runtime concept, not webpack's heavier graph.
P10 V8 / spec ESM "live bindings" — V8 blog "JavaScript modules" + Lin Clark "ES modules: A cartoon deep-dive" (Mozilla Hacks, 2018) Module linking phases (construction/instantiation/evaluation); imports are live read-only views of exports Normative mental model for Binding/live-binding preservation through shake + rename + concatenation; export-star + cyclic-module eval order.
P11 Babel transform/plugin model — Babel docs "Plugin Handbook" (visitor, paths, bindings) Visitor-based AST mutation; binding/scope tracking; pass pipeline Reference for TransformPass ergonomics + scope tracking; we use enum/match folds, not a path object, but the pass semantics transfer.
P12 CommonJS ⇄ ESM interop — Node.js "Interoperability with CommonJS" guide + esbuild __toESM/__commonJS CHANGELOG notes Default-export interop, named-export detection, __esModule marker, value-snapshot vs live view Specifies the interop helpers (__toESM/__commonJS/__export) emitted in Ph10.
P13 Determinism / content hashing — Rollup output.hashCharacters/chunkFileNames docs + esbuild "entryNames/hash" notes Stable content hashing with a fixed-point pass for cross-chunk hash references Drives byte-identical output + stable Chunk.hash; the cross-chunk-hash fixed-point loop in Ph9.

5.3 Reference Implementations

# Repo — exact paths Study (architectural lesson) Do NOT copy
R1 esbuild — internal/js_lexer, internal/js_parser, internal/bundler, internal/linker, internal/js_printer Single-pass parse+bind; linker = symbol resolution + module-group splitting + runtime injection; printer w/ inline sourcemap. Mirrors our lexer→parser→graph→chunk→codegen. Go GC + slice-of-everything memory model; manual ast.Ref index scheme — use Rust SymbolId/arena instead. Not type-checked (correct — types discarded, #42).
R2 Rollup — src/Graph.ts, src/Chunk.ts, src/Module.ts, src/ast/nodes/*, src/utils/chunkAssignment.ts Graph→ModuleGraph; chunkAssignment = canonical module-group code splitting; per-AST-node hasEffects/include = symbol-level tree-shaking + scope-hoisting render. JS perf shortcuts; AST node class hierarchy (we use enums + match); Acorn coupling.
R3 Vite — packages/vite/src/node/server/* (moduleGraph, hmr, ws), src/node/optimizer (pre-bundle), src/node/plugins Dev ModuleGraph + invalidation; hmr.ts boundary propagation + ws protocol -> HmrEngine; optimizer = dependency pre-bundling. Rollup/esbuild delegation glue; the huge plugin surface — keep Plugin to resolve/load/transform/render_chunk.
R4 SWC — crates/swc_ecma_parser, crates/swc_ecma_ast, crates/swc_ecma_transforms_*, crates/swc_ecma_minifier Visitor/Fold transform driver + pass ordering -> bndl-transform; minifier mangle/DCE/fold -> Minifier; Atom interning. Its source-map handling (we use #265); minifier's sheer breadth — implement a sound subset, don't clone every pass.
R5 OXC — crates/oxc_parser, crates/oxc_ast, crates/oxc_resolver, crates/oxc_span Bump-arena AST + Span/NodeId layout -> bndl-ast; oxc_resolver = the Node CJS+ESM + tsconfig-paths algorithm -> bndl-resolver 1:1. Arena lifetime gymnastics if they fight our Send+Sync immutable-module model; keep ownership pragmatic.
R6 Babel — packages/babel-parser/src (tokenizer, expression, statement, plugins/{jsx,typescript}), babel-traverse/src/scope TS + JSX plugin parsing tables -> Ph3; scope binding/reference tracking -> ScopeTree. The transform runtime + helpers-as-deps model; @babel/traverse path mutation API (we fold over enums).
R7 Acorn — acorn/src/{tokenize,expression,statement}.js, acorn-jsx Minimal, spec-faithful ESTree producer; regex-vs-divide context tracking; ASI handling. Performance is not its goal; do not adopt its plugin-monkeypatch extension style.

5.4 Specifications / RFCs (normative)

# Spec Normative for Notes
S1 ECMA-262 (TC39 ECMAScript) sec. 11 (Source Text / Unicode), sec. 12 Lexical Grammar (incl. sec. 12.9 ASI), sec. 12.9.6 RegExp-vs-Div via goal symbols Ph1 bndl-lexer, Ph2 ASI Maximal-munch tokens, template-literal goal symbols, ASI restricted productions. Ground truth for token shapes.
S2 ECMA-262 sec. 16 Modules — Module Records, ImportEntry/ExportEntry, ResolveExport, GetExportedNames, Cyclic Module Record eval Ph5 bndl-graph, Ph8, Ph10 Live-binding semantics, export-star resolution, cyclic eval order. Defines ImportRecord/ExportRecord/Binding behavior.
S3 ECMA-262 sec. 13–15 expressions/statements/functions/classes; sec. 11.1.6 reserved words Ph2 grammar Pratt precedence, cover grammars (arrow params, async), reserved-word ParseErrorKind.
S4 TypeScript grammar — TypeScript/doc/spec-ARCHIVED.md + current parser; types, interfaces, enums, generics, decorators, import type Ph3 Parse-and-discard surface only (no checking — #42). Defines TrailingTsType recovery + .d.ts handling.
S5 JSX specification (facebook/jsx) — elements, fragments, {expr} children, namespaced/member names Ph3, Ph6 JsxTransform Grammar gated by Syntax{jsx}; drives classic createElement vs automatic jsx-runtime lowering.
S6 Node.js ESM resolution — PACKAGE_RESOLVE, PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE (conditional + pattern *), PACKAGE_IMPORTS_RESOLVE, ESM_FILE_FORMAT Ph4 bndl-resolver The exact ESM algorithm; Condition matching order; exports/imports maps.
S7 Node.js CJS resolution — require() algorithm: LOAD_AS_FILE, LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY, LOAD_NODE_MODULES, LOAD_INDEX Ph4 Extension + index probing, node_modules walk, main. tsconfig paths/baseUrl run before node_modules.
S8 package.json fields — exports, imports, main, module, browser, sideEffects, type (Node + the package-exports proposal) Ph4, Ph7 PackageJson model; main_fields order; sideEffects -> tree-shake contract; browser Condition.
S9 tsconfig.json — compilerOptions.paths, baseUrl, jsx, target, moduleResolution (TS handbook) Ph3, Ph4, Ph6 TsConfig path mapping (glob *), jsx runtime selection, target -> Target lowering hint.
S10 Source Map v3 source maps — see #265 Bundler consumes the #265 codec; never hand-rolls VLQ. Each stage composes position deltas; final map = composition. Do not re-specify the format.
S11 WHATWG WebSocket / RFC 6455 + Vite HMR message schema Ph12 ws HMR protocol Frame transport for the HMR protocol; message kinds (update/full-reload/prune) modeled on Vite's.
S12 Unicode UAX #31 (Identifier and Pattern Syntax) + ID_Start/ID_Continue tables Ph1 identifier lexing Code-point classifier for JS identifiers incl. $, _, ZWNJ/ZWJ, \u{} escapes.

6. Design Principles

Fourteen non-negotiable principles. Each is a global invariant that constrains every crate and phase; later phases cite them by bold name. Ordered: correctness foundations first, then performance, then operability.

6.1 Spec is the oracle

Statement. The TC39 ECMAScript grammar, the TypeScript/JSX grammars, and the Node CJS+ESM resolution specs are normative. When intuition and spec disagree, spec wins. Conformance is measured, not asserted: Test262 (parser subset) + a differential-oracle harness comparing bndl output semantics against esbuild/Rollup.

  • Tradeoff. Spec fidelity (ASI corner cases, __proto__ keying, exports condition order) costs throughput and code; we pay it. A 5% perf win that drops a spec case is rejected.
  • Operational. Phase 0 wires the differential oracle (esbuild/Rollup behind a dev-dep/feature); Phase 13 gates "done" on Test262 + fixture parity. Each unsupported construct is an explicit ParseErrorKind, never silent miscompile.
  • Violation breaks. Hand-tuned ASI guess that omits had_newline_before → a\n++b parses as a++; b vs a; ++b → emitted bundle changes runtime behavior; a "small lexer shortcut" silently corrupts every consumer's program.

6.2 Parsing never panics

Statement. bndl-lexer/bndl-parser consume untrusted source on the hot path. Malformed input → Invalid token + ParseError accumulated in Vec<ParseError>, never a Rust panic!/unwrap/unbounded recursion/OOM. parse_program always returns (Program, Vec<ParseError>).

  • Tradeoff. Error-recovery machinery (sync tokens, partial nodes) > a fail-fast parser; required for a long-lived dev server and for fuzzing.
  • Operational. #![deny(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::panic)] in lexer/parser/resolver. Phase 13 fuzzes lex/parse/roundtrip; zero panics is a CI gate. Recursion is depth-capped (see Bounded resource usage).
  • Violation breaks. One unwrap() on peek() at EOF → a truncated file in a watched dir crashes bndl-server, killing HMR for every connected client; a fuzzer finds it in seconds.

6.3 Determinism is a contract

Statement. Identical inputs + options ⇒ byte-identical output (chunks, filenames, maps, manifest). Determinism is required for the content-addressed cache, reproducible builds, and long-term CDN caching.

Source of nondeterminism Mitigation
HashMap iteration order sort by ModuleId/SymbolId/Atom; never iterate hash order for emit
identifier renaming stable rename order keyed on scope-entry + declaration order
module/chunk ordering topological + tiebreak on stable id
chunk hashing hash of final chunk content, fixed-point over cross-chunk hash refs
thread interleaving parallelism produces immutable results; merge order is sorted
time/RNG/abs-paths in output forbidden in emitted bytes
  • Tradeoff. Sorting + fixed-point hashing add passes vs emit-as-you-go; mandatory.
  • Operational. Phase 9 computes content hashes via the fixed-point loop. Phase 13 asserts build(x)==build(x) byte-for-byte across runs/threads/platforms as a gate.
  • Violation breaks. Hashing chunk content before rewriting cross-chunk hash refs → every rebuild flips hashes → cache always misses, every CDN edge re-downloads, incremental rebuild is a full rebuild.

6.4 Tree-shaking must be sound

Statement. Never remove code with observable side effects unless proven pure. Over-removal is a correctness bug, not a missed optimization. Explicit purity contracts: sideEffects:false (package.json) and /*#__PURE__*/. SideEffect is Pure | Impure | Unknown; Unknown is treated as Impure (conservative).

  • Tradeoff. Conservative Unknown→keep leaves dead bytes; acceptable. Aggressive removal that drops a polyfill/import "./side" is not.
  • Operational. Phase 7 TreeShaker worklist marks reachable symbols from entry exports; top-level effectful statements pin their module. Differential fixtures (Phase 13) assert effect-bearing imports survive.
  • Violation breaks. Treating a bare import "./register-globals.js" as unreachable (no exports used) → polyfill dropped → Object.entries undefined at runtime in the user's app, with no error from bndl.

6.5 Live bindings are sacred

Statement. An ESM import is a live, read-only view of an exported binding; mutating the export must be visible at every import site, and identity (===) must be preserved. Transform (TypeStrip/JSX/Lower), tree-shaking, renaming, and scope hoisting must all preserve live-binding semantics. CJS exports is a value snapshot at require time; the two are bridged by named interop helpers (__toESM/__commonJS), never by ad-hoc copying.

  • Tradeoff. Concatenation must rename a binding consistently across modules and route mutations through the same slot (not a copied local), constraining the Renamer.
  • Operational. Phase 5 records ExportRecord/ImportRecord + re-export edges; Phase 8 hoist preserves a single canonical slot per exported binding; circular ESM bindings stay observable.
  • Violation breaks. Hoisting export let count into a per-importer const copy → counter.increment() mutates the source slot but importers read a stale copy → counter "doesn't update"; a classic Rollup/esbuild correctness regression.

6.6 Scope correctness before optimization

Statement. No rename, no hoist, no mangle without a correct ScopeTree in which every reference is bound to its declaration. Scope analysis precedes every optimization that touches identifiers.

  • Tradeoff. Building + validating the ScopeTree (TDZ, hoisting, var vs let/const, shadowing, with, eval taint, labels) is upfront cost before any minify win.
  • Operational. Phase 8 builds ScopeTree; Phase 8 hoist and Phase 11 Minifier mangle are consumers of it and refuse to run on an unresolved reference. with/direct-eval scopes are marked tainted → mangling disabled inside them.
  • Violation breaks. Mangling a free identifier assumed local → renames a closed-over outer variable or a global → process becomes t → ReferenceError at runtime; or two distinct bindings collapse to one name and silently alias.

6.7 Interop is explicit

Statement. CJS↔ESM bridging happens only through the named module-runtime helpers — __require, __esm, __commonJS, __toESM, __export — with documented contracts. No implicit, per-site interop guessing.

Helper Contract
__commonJS wrap a CJS module body; memoize module.exports on first __require
__esm wrap an ESM module needing lazy init (cycle/wrapper); first-call memoized
__require resolve a wrapped module id → its exports (CJS view)
__toESM adapt a CJS namespace to ESM shape (synthesize default, mark __esModule)
__export define live getters on an ESM namespace object for re-exports
  • Tradeoff. Helpers add runtime bytes vs inlining; the named contract is what keeps interop auditable + correct across formats.
  • Operational. Phase 10 injects exactly these; __esModule + default-interop follow the esbuild/TS convention. Same helper names across ESM/CJS/IIFE output.
  • Violation breaks. Inlining require('x').default when x is a CJS module without __esModule → default is undefined → import dep from 'x' yields undefined; React-style default imports of CJS deps break.

6.8 Incremental by construction

Statement. Every expensive stage (parse, transform, codegen) is a pure function of its inputs, keyed by a CacheKey = BLAKE3(content + options). bndl-cache memoizes Artifacts; unchanged inputs ⇒ cache hit, never recompute. Incrementality is designed in, not bolted on.

  • Tradeoff. Demands purity discipline (no hidden global/clock/fs state in a stage) and stable key derivation; this is also what makes parallelism + determinism free.
  • Operational. Phase 13 store is in-memory + on-disk; the Watcher (Phase 12) drives change-based invalidation by content hash, not mtime alone. Determinism (6.3) is the precondition for cache hits.
  • Violation breaks. A transform pass reads Date.now()/an ambient env not folded into the key → cache returns a stale artifact for "changed" inputs, or never hits → either wrong output or no incremental speedup.

6.9 Parallel by default, immutable modules

Statement. Module-level work (load → lex → parse → transform) runs across a thread pool. A Module is Send + Sync and effectively immutable once built; parallel stages share &Module and produce new immutable results. Bump/arena allocation gives cheap per-module memory.

  • Tradeoff. Immutability can mean copy-on-rewrite vs in-place mutation; bought back by fearless parallelism + arena cheapness, and it underpins determinism (merge order sorted, 6.3).
  • Operational. Phase 5 loads modules in parallel; the graph is assembled by id. Cross-module mutation (rename/hoist) is confined to the single-threaded codegen merge step operating on the frozen graph.
  • Violation breaks. Sharing a &mut parse arena across worker threads → data race / RefCell borrow panic under load; or interleaving-dependent module order → nondeterministic chunk hashes (violates 6.3) and flaky CI.

6.10 Source maps compose, never hand-rolled

Statement. Every stage (lex→parse→transform→print→minify) emits position deltas; the final map is the composition of per-stage deltas. The bundler never hand-writes VLQ or the map format — it emits through the project #265 codec.

  • Tradeoff. Threading spans through every pass + composing maps is more bookkeeping than dropping maps; required so debuggers point at original TS/JSX.
  • Operational. Span{start,end} rides on every AST node from Phase 1; Emitter (Phase 10) holds the #265 sourcemap-builder handle; Minifier (Phase 11) updates mappings, never invalidates them. Map format is out of scope here (NON-GOAL, see #265).
  • Violation breaks. A pass that rewrites nodes but drops their Span → mappings point at the wrong line, or a hand-rolled VLQ off-by-one shifts every column → stack traces are unusable, and we'd be re-implementing #265's contract incorrectly.

6.11 Bounded resource usage

Statement. All recursive/unbounded structures have hard caps: parser nesting depth, JSON/object nesting, chunk count, cyclic-resolution depth, dynamic-import graph fanout. Exceeding a cap → a clean diagnostic, never stack overflow / runaway memory.

Resource Cap (default) On exceed
parser recursion depth 1000 ParseError(UnexpectedToken) + recover
module graph size configurable, default 100k hard error with count
resolution symlink/realpath hops 40 ResolveError::NotFound
chunk fixed-point hash iterations bounded (e.g. 10) error if non-convergent
  • Tradeoff. Caps can reject pathological-but-legal input; mitigated by generous, configurable limits. Predictable failure beats a crashed long-lived server.
  • Operational. Caps are explicit constants; Phase 2 enforces depth, Phase 9 bounds the hash fixed-point, Phase 4 bounds realpath chains. Ties to 6.2 (never panic) — caps are how recursion stays panic-free.
  • Violation breaks. No parser depth cap → a fuzzer-generated ((((((… 200k-deep expression blows the native stack → SIGSEGV in bndl-server, no recoverable error.

6.12 Plugins are pure hooks

Statement. The Plugin trait exposes exactly four hooks — resolve, load, transform, render_chunk — each a documented, side-effect-disciplined function of its inputs returning Option<_> (None = "not mine, defer"). Plugins extend the pipeline; they do not reach into internal graph state.

  • Tradeoff. A constrained hook surface rejects some power-user patterns; bought back by composability, determinism (6.3), and cacheability (6.8) — a transform plugin's output must be hashable into the CacheKey.
  • Operational. Phase 0/Phase 13: the CLI plugin host runs hooks in deterministic order; first Some wins for resolve/load. Plugin outputs feed the same bndl-cache. No hook may mutate the ModuleGraph directly.
  • Violation breaks. A load plugin that reads an unkeyed external file or random state → breaks determinism + caching (stale/wrong cache hits), exactly the failure 6.3/6.8 forbid; or a plugin mutating shared graph state mid-parallel-load → race (violates 6.9).

6.13 Observable pipeline

Statement. The bundler is introspectable end-to-end. bndl graph dumps the module graph; bndl analyze reports chunk sizes / reachability / tree-shaking decisions; bndl transform shows post-transform output for one file. Trace hooks expose timing + decisions per stage. No black-box "it just bundled".

  • Tradeoff. Maintaining stable introspection output + trace instrumentation is ongoing cost; essential for debugging miscompiles, perf regressions, and "why is X in my bundle?".
  • Operational. analyze/graph are first-class CLI subcommands (Phase 0 scaffold, fleshed alongside graph/chunk phases). Trace output is deterministic + diffable. Backs the differential oracle (6.1) — divergences are localized via per-stage dumps.
  • Violation breaks. A tree-shake that silently drops a module with no analyze visibility → user can't tell whether removal was intended → debugging a missing feature requires reading bundler internals; soundness regressions (6.4) become undiagnosable.

6.14 Errors are diagnostics, not surprises

Statement. Every failure across bndl-* is a typed, span-tagged diagnostic — ParseErrorKind, ResolveError, etc. — with the offending Span/path, surfaced to the user; never a silent miscompile and never a process abort (cf. 6.2). Recoverable errors accumulate; fatal ones stop the build with a clear message.

  • Tradeoff. Rich typed errors + span plumbing add boilerplate vs Result<_, String>; required for actionable messages and for the dev server to keep running.
  • Operational. ResolveError::{NotFound,InvalidExports,NotExported,BadTsPaths} and ParseErrorKind::* are exhaustive enums; Phase 12 renders them in-browser without crashing HMR. CI greps for unwrap/expect/panic in library crates.
  • Violation breaks. Returning a stringly-typed error or swallowing a ResolveError::NotExported as None → import { x } from 'pkg' where x isn't exported produces undefined instead of a build error → the bug ships to production silently.

Cross-references. 6.3 (determinism) ⇐ depends on ⇒ 6.8 (cache) and 6.9 (immutable parallel merge). 6.4 (sound shaking) + 6.5 (live bindings) + 6.6 (scope correctness) jointly gate every identifier-touching optimization in Phases 7–8, 11. 6.2 + 6.11 + 6.14 define the failure contract enforced by Phase 13 fuzzing/conformance. 6.10 defers all map mechanics to #265. 6.12 + 6.13 keep the extended/observed pipeline within the determinism + cacheability envelope.

7. System Invariants

The invariants below are hard correctness contracts: bndl must never violate them on any input or config. Each row is a load-bearing rule whose breach is a correctness bug (silent miscompilation), not a perf regression. Numbered INV-1..INV-12; each has a stable id used in test names (inv1_*), assertion messages, and trace tags. Enforcement spans compile-time types, debug_assert!, runtime checks gated by --strict-invariants, and the Phase 13 differential + fuzz gates.

7.0 Classification

Tier Meaning Enforcement posture
A — Soundness Breach = wrong output ships (INV-4, INV-5, INV-6, INV-7, INV-10) always-on debug_assert + differential oracle + fuzz; release: structural checks kept
B — Determinism Breach = non-reproducible build / cache poison (INV-2, INV-8, INV-9, INV-11) byte-equality harness (N runs, 2 hosts), hash fixed-point assert
C — Liveness/Safety Breach = crash / hang / OOM on untrusted input (INV-1, INV-3, INV-12) total functions, recursion+memory caps, fuzz with sanitizers

Conventions: "detection" = automated signal; "telemetry/trace" = tracing span fields under target bndl::inv; "recovery" = behavior when the guard fires; "debug method" = first move when it fails in the field.

7.1 INV-1 — Parse is total

  • Statement: parse_program(src, id, syntax) returns (Program, Vec<ParseError>) for every &str (any byte sequence that is valid UTF-8; invalid UTF-8 rejected upstream at load with a diagnostic, never reaches lexer). Never panics, never infinite-loops, never aborts.
  • Why: parser is the untrusted hot path (#264 identity). A panic = DoS; a hang = build wedge. Errors are data, not control flow.
  • Where enforced: bndl-lexer emits TokenKind::Invalid instead of unwrapping; bndl-parser error recovery (synchronize to ;/}/ModuleItem boundary) guarantees forward progress — every recovery step consumes ≥1 token or inserts a synthetic node and advances.
  • Detection: Phase 13 fuzz target fuzz_parse (libFuzzer/AFL, ASan+UBSan) over arbitrary bytes; harness asserts return within budget and zero panics. Progress guard: debug_assert!(self.cur_pos > entry_pos || produced_node) per parse loop iteration.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv1.parse{source_id, byte_len, errors_len, tokens, recovered}; counter bndl_parse_recoveries_total.
  • Recovery: worst case = Program{items:[]} + populated errors; downstream treats a module with parse errors as opaque (no transform/shake), surfaces diagnostics, fails the build (build mode) or serves an error overlay (dev).
  • Debug method: minimize crashing input via cargo fuzz tmin; bisect to the offending next_token/parse_* via the per-iteration progress assert; check the template-stack and regex-context flags (the two known infinite-loop hazards).

7.2 INV-2 — Resolution is deterministic + cache-coherent

  • Statement: for fixed (Specifier, importer dir, ResolveOptions, fs snapshot), Resolver::resolve yields an identical ResolveResult (or identical ResolveError) across calls, threads, and processes. The in-process fs/package.json/realpath caches are transparent — they never change the result, only latency.
  • Why: resolution feeds ModuleId assignment and chunk hashes; nondeterminism here cascades into INV-8/INV-9 failures and stale-cache hazards.
  • Where enforced: condition list ordering is fixed (ResolveOptions.conditions is an ordered priority vector, matched first-wins per the Node PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE order); directory reads sorted before probing; realpath canonicalizes symlinks so two specifiers to the same file → one ModuleId.
  • Detection: golden-resolution fixtures (a tree of fabricated node_modules/package.json/tsconfig); a "resolve twice, compare" property test; cache-bypass mode (--no-resolve-cache) must match cached results byte-for-byte.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv2.resolve{specifier, importer, conditions, result_path, from_node_modules, cache_hit}; on a cache/no-cache mismatch → error! + abort.
  • Recovery: mismatch is a hard error (poisoned cache); the resolver invalidates its caches and re-resolves cold. A ResolveError is deterministic and final for that input — no retry loop.
  • Debug method: dump the resolution trace (bndl analyze --trace-resolve <spec>): the ordered list of probed paths + matched condition + the package.json that won; compare against the Node algorithm step labels (LOAD_AS_FILE, PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE, …).

7.3 INV-3 — Build-order acyclicity OR explicit wrapped cycles

  • Statement: the ModuleGraph may contain import cycles (legal in ESM/CJS), but the execution/instantiation order used by codegen is a valid topological order of the condensation (SCCs collapsed). Every module in a multi-node SCC is flagged requires_wrapper = true and emitted with lazy-init memoization; no module is concatenated in a way that reads another module's binding before that module's top-level eval.
  • Why: scope hoisting a cycle into one flat scope reorders side effects and can read TDZ bindings → wrong runtime behavior. Cycles must be wrapped, not flattened (locked algorithm fact).
  • Where enforced: bndl-graph runs Tarjan SCC; SCCs of size >1 (and self-loops via require) mark members non-hoistable. bndl-codegen scope-hoisting pass refuses to merge a wrapped module into a peer's scope (compile-time path split: hoist vs wrap).
  • Detection: assert every emitted chunk's module order is topo-valid over non-wrapper edges; cycle fixtures (ESM↔ESM, CJS↔CJS, mixed) with known-correct execution traces in the differential oracle.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv3.scc{scc_id, members, wrapped}; counter bndl_cycles_total, bndl_wrapped_modules_total.
  • Recovery: when in doubt (unanalyzable cycle, dynamic membership), fall back to wrapper — wrapping is always sound, hoisting is the optimization. Never the reverse.
  • Debug method: bndl graph --scc prints the condensation + per-SCC wrap flags; diff the module eval order against esbuild/Rollup for the same cycle.

7.4 INV-4 — ESM live-binding identity preserved (Tier A)

  • Statement: an ESM import { x } is a live, read-only view of the exporter's binding. After type-strip, JSX transform, lowering, renaming, tree-shaking, and concatenation, a read of x must observe the exporter's current value (incl. post-import mutations), and x must remain non-assignable. Namespace imports (import * as ns) preserve the live property per key; re-exports and export-star forward identity, not a copy.
  • Why: the defining semantic difference between ESM and CJS. Snapshotting a live binding (e.g. copying x into a local at hoist time) silently breaks counters, lazy singletons, circular-init patterns.
  • Where enforced: bndl-graph records ExportRecord.local: SymbolId / reexport: (ModuleId, Atom); concatenation rewrites a cross-module reference to the canonical SymbolId's renamed identifier (shared mutable slot), never to a fresh local. Wrapped modules expose getters via __export({ x: () => x }) so reads stay live.
  • Detection: "mutate-after-import" fixtures (let n=0; export const inc=()=>n++; export {n} consumed elsewhere) compared against native Node ESM + the oracle; an assert that no concatenated reference resolves to a const copy of a let/var export.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv4.binding{export_module, symbol, kind:live|const, via:hoist|wrapper|getter}.
  • Recovery: if liveness cannot be guaranteed for a binding under hoisting, downgrade that module to wrapper + getter export. No silent snapshot.
  • Debug method: trace the SymbolId from ExportRecord → renamed identifier in ScopeTree; confirm all readers share one slot; check getter emission for wrapped exporters.

7.5 INV-5 — Tree-shaking never drops an observable side effect (Tier A)

  • Statement: a statement, declaration, import, or export is removable only if proven side-effect-free. The effect lattice is Pure ⊑ Unknown ⊑ Impure; tree-shaking removes only Pure. Unknown is treated as Impure (conservative over-approximation of effects). sideEffects:false (package.json) and /*#__PURE__*/ are explicit downgrades to Pure; absent them, any call/new/getter/member-with-possible-getter is Unknown→retained.
  • Why: over-removal = miscompilation (lost polyfills, lost registration side effects). Tree-shaking is sound only as an under-approximation of reachability and an over-approximation of effects. "Smaller but wrong" is never acceptable.
  • Where enforced: bndl-treeshake SideEffect classifier defaults unknown→Impure; TreeShaker worklist marks reachable from entry exports + retains every top-level Impure statement (pins its module). ImportRecord whose imported symbols are all unreachable AND whose target module is sideEffects:false may be dropped; otherwise the bare import is kept for effects.
  • Detection: differential vs Rollup/esbuild on the tree-shaking fixture corpus (must match or be more conservative, never less); a "retained-effects" oracle that executes pre/post-shake under #126's harness and diffs observable effects (console, globals, throws).
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv5.shake{module, removed_stmts, removed_exports, retained_impure, pure_annotations_honored}; counter bndl_treeshake_removed_total.
  • Recovery: classifier ambiguity → retain. A sideEffects glob that doesn't match → treat as true (has effects).
  • Debug method: bndl analyze --why-kept <module> shows the effect class of each top-level node and the retention reason (reachable export / impure stmt / sideEffects flag); compare a suspected over-removal against the oracle's executed-effects diff.

7.6 INV-6 — Renaming is scope-correct (Tier A)

  • Statement: the Renamer (collision avoidance during concatenation + minify mangling) produces a renaming where: (a) no two distinct bindings visible in the same scope map to the same name (no shadow collision); (b) no reference is captured by an unintended binding; (c) free/global references (window, process, runtime helpers __require…) are never renamed; (d) eval/with scopes disable mangling for their contents.
