  • Why: incorrect renaming silently rebinds references → wrong values, broken closures. This is the most common scope-hoisting/minify miscompilation class.
  • Where enforced: ScopeTree binds every reference to a declaring scope before renaming; Renamer allocates names per-scope from a pool excluding (reserved words ∪ names visible in enclosing scopes ∪ free names referenced within). Global/unresolved references flagged is_free and pinned.
  • Detection: "rename round-trip" property — after renaming, re-run scope analysis and assert the binding/reference resolution graph is isomorphic to the pre-rename graph (same edges, modulo names); shadowing fixtures (nested same-name let, param shadowing import, catch binding) vs oracle execution.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv6.rename{scope_id, bindings, renamed, free_pinned, eval_present}; on isomorphism break → error! with the diverging reference span.
  • Recovery: if eval/with/dynamic-require detected in a scope, disable mangling for that scope subtree (and disable concatenation if it imports). Conservative naming over aggressive.
  • Debug method: dump pre/post ScopeTree resolution edges; the first reference whose target scope-id changed localizes the capture/collision; check the per-scope exclusion set construction.

7.7 INV-7 — Every emitted reference resolves (Tier A)

  • Statement: after tree-shaking, chunking, and codegen, no dangling references exist: every import/require in an output chunk targets a module present in this chunk or a declared cross-chunk import; every internal symbol reference resolves to an in-scope binding; no chunk imports a symbol that the target chunk does not export.
  • Why: code splitting can strand a module's dependency in a different chunk; tree-shaking can remove an export still referenced via export-star. Either → ReferenceError / module-not-found at runtime.
  • Where enforced: bndl-chunk wires cross-chunk imports from the reachable-entry-set assignment; bndl-codegen link step resolves each ImportRecord to (intra-chunk binding | cross-chunk import | external) — an unresolved record is a hard panic in debug, structural error in release.
  • Detection: post-emit linker pass builds the full symbol/closure of every chunk and asserts closure ⊆ (defined ∪ imported ∪ external ∪ runtime-helpers); fuzz: random entry sets + dynamic imports, assert no dangling edge; a "load all chunks in order" smoke under #126.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv7.link{chunk, imports, exports, external, unresolved}; counter bndl_unresolved_refs_total (must be 0).
  • Recovery: an unresolved cross-chunk symbol → pull the defining module into the importing chunk (or, for shared, into a shared chunk) and re-run the fixed-point (interacts with INV-9). Never emit a dangling import.
  • Debug method: bndl graph --chunks shows module→chunk assignment + cross-chunk edges; the linker error names the (chunk, symbol) pair; trace back to the chunking decision that separated definition from use.

7.8 INV-8 — Output is byte-deterministic (Tier B)

  • Statement: identical (inputs, config, bndl version) ⇒ byte-identical output chunks, file names, and manifest — across runs, thread counts, OSes, and CWDs. No wall-clock, no HashMap iteration order, no absolute paths, no thread-race ordering leaks into bytes.
  • Why: required for caching (INV-11), CDN long-term caching (INV-9), reproducible builds, and meaningful differential diffs.
  • Where enforced: module ordering is by a stable key (entry discovery order → import order, not load-completion order); all map iteration that affects output uses BTreeMap/sorted Vec; identifier-rename order is deterministic (scope pre-order + stable binding order); paths normalized to project-relative POSIX before any hashing/printing; thread pool produces immutable Modules, ordering applied single-threaded downstream.
  • Detection: "build N times, --threads {1,8}, two OS CI lanes, compare SHA-256 of every output" gate; a "shuffle module load order, assert identical output" test (forces the ordering layer to normalize).
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv8.emit{chunk, sha256, module_order_key}; CI artifact = sorted manifest of (file, hash).
  • Recovery: none at runtime — nondeterminism is a build-breaking bug. The fix is always to add a sort/stable-key, never a retry.
  • Debug method: diff two runs' chunk bytes; the first differing offset + the owning AST node localizes the nondeterministic source (usually an unsorted map or a path-with-CWD leak); grep for HashMap/std::time on output paths.

7.9 INV-9 — Chunk content hashes reach a fixed point (Tier B)

  • Statement: chunk file names embed a content hash; chunks import each other by file name, so a hash change in chunk B changes the bytes of importer A, changing A's hash — a dependency. The naming algorithm iterates a placeholder → hash substitution to a fixed point: assign placeholders, hash with placeholders, substitute, re-hash, repeat until no hash changes (guaranteed to converge; bounded iterations).
  • Why: without the fixed point, emitted import URLs reference stale hashes → 404s; or hashes oscillate → nondeterminism (breaks INV-8). Long-term CDN caching demands content == hash.
  • Where enforced: bndl-chunk content-hash pass: (1) topo-process chunks emitting __IMPORT__{ChunkId}__ placeholders, (2) hash each chunk's placeholdered bytes (BLAKE3, see #265-independent — this is chunk hashing, not source-map), (3) substitute real names, (4) recompute hashes of chunks whose imported names changed, iterate. Convergence proof: hashes form a DAG over the condensation of the chunk import graph → a finite descending process; cycles in chunk imports use a stable two-pass (hash without cross-names, then with) and are asserted stable.
  • Detection: assert convergence within MAX_HASH_ITERS (default 10; exceeding = bug); post-pass assert: for every emitted import string f, a chunk with file name f exists, and re-hashing the final bytes reproduces the embedded hash (idempotence check).
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv9.hash{iters, converged, chunk_count}; counter bndl_hash_iterations; on non-convergence → error! + abort.
  • Recovery: non-convergence (oscillation) → fail the build with the oscillating chunk set; do not ship oscillating names. (Root cause is almost always a hash fed by a non-content input — same fix family as INV-8.)
  • Debug method: dump per-iteration (ChunkId → hash); the chunk whose hash never stabilizes + its import set localizes the cycle/oscillation; verify the placeholder substitution touches only file-name strings, not other content.

7.10 INV-10 — Source-map mappings consistent with emitted bytes (Tier A)

  • Statement: the final source map (produced via the #265 codec — bndl never hand-rolls VLQ) maps every generated (line, col) to a correct original (source, line, col). Mappings are the composition of per-stage position deltas (lex→parse→transform→print→minify); after minification (whitespace removal, mangling) the map still points at the pre-transform source token.
  • Why: wrong maps mislead every debugging session downstream; a map that drifts from bytes is worse than no map. Composition correctness is the whole game.
  • Where enforced: each stage that moves a node updates its Span/segment mapping; Emitter records generated positions as it writes and pairs them with the node's original span; the Minifier updates segments in lockstep with byte edits; final encode delegates to #265.
  • Detection: "map round-trip" — for a sample of generated positions, decode the map (#265 decoder), assert the original span's text matches the expected token; differential against esbuild/Rollup maps on fixtures; an invariant that #generated-position-segments is monotonic in generated order.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv10.sourcemap{chunk, segments, sources, stages_composed}; counter bndl_map_segments_total.
  • Recovery: if a stage cannot produce a precise mapping (synthetic node with no origin), map to the nearest enclosing original span (never to a wrong source); a missing map is degraded-but-safe, a wrong map is a bug.
  • Debug method: pick a generated line, decode via #265, compare resolved original text vs emitted; the first stage whose delta is off (binary-search by emitting intermediate maps per stage) localizes the composition error.

7.11 INV-11 — Incremental cache never returns a stale artifact (Tier B)

  • Statement: Cache returns a memoized Artifact iff its CacheKey (BLAKE3 of all inputs: file content + the exact options/flags that affect that stage + bndl version + relevant plugin identities + dependency keys) matches. A change in any input that could alter the artifact changes the key → cache miss. No stale read, ever.
  • Why: a stale cache hit ships yesterday's code with today's config — invisible, catastrophic, irreproducible. Cache correctness ⇒ "key includes everything that the output depends on".
  • Where enforced: CacheKey construction is the single chokepoint; each cached stage (parse/transform/codegen) declares its full input set; transform/codegen keys include the upstream artifact's key (Merkle chaining), so an edit propagates. Watcher invalidation (#bndl-server) marks dirty modules whose content hash changed; transitive importers re-keyed only if their inputs changed.
  • Detection: "edit one byte, assert exactly the affected artifacts recompute and output equals a cold build"; "change a flag (target, define, minify), assert full miss"; a poisoning test: corrupt the on-disk store, assert a content-hash mismatch is detected (store is content-addressed → key == content hash of value, verifiable).
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv11.cache{stage, key, hit, invalidated_by}; counters bndl_cache_hits_total / _misses_total.
  • Recovery: on-disk value whose recomputed content hash ≠ key → treat as miss, recompute, overwrite. Unknown/old bndl version in key → cold. Never trust a value whose hash doesn't verify.
  • Debug method: when a stale-looking result appears, dump the CacheKey input vector for that stage and diff against the cold-build key — the missing input is the bug (classic omissions: a flag, the JSX runtime mode, a plugin version).

7.12 INV-12 — Bounded recursion + memory (Tier C)

  • Statement: no input causes stack overflow, unbounded memory, or unbounded time. Recursive descent enforces a nesting-depth cap (MAX_PARSE_DEPTH, default 1000) → emits ParseError (not a crash) past it; arena/bump allocation is bounded per-source; the module graph, worklists, and hash iterations are all bounded.
  • Why: untrusted source can be adversarial (deeply nested ((((…)))), pathological templates, billion-laughs-style expansion). A parser DoS is a security issue (#264 memory-safety rationale).
  • Where enforced: bndl-parser increments/decrements a depth counter at each recursive parse_* entry, checks against MAX_PARSE_DEPTH; lexer template-stack depth capped; bndl-graph module count + edge count are finite (resolution terminates); MAX_HASH_ITERS (INV-9) caps the hash loop; bump allocator caps per-file bytes, error past the cap.
  • Detection: fuzz with depth-bomb / size-bomb seeds under a memory ceiling + timeout (ASan, RLIMIT_AS); assert graceful ParseError/build-error, never SIGSEGV/OOM-kill; a unit test feeding exactly MAX_PARSE_DEPTH ± 1.
  • Telemetry/trace: span inv12.limits{max_depth_seen, arena_bytes, modules, hash_iters}; counter bndl_depth_limit_hits_total.
  • Recovery: hitting a cap → structured error (ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken with a "nesting too deep" message, or a build error), localized to the offending span; the rest of the build proceeds where independent.
  • Debug method: if a real (non-adversarial) file trips a cap, raise the specific limit via config and file it; the trace's max_depth_seen vs the cap tells you whether it's adversarial or a too-tight limit.

7.13 Cross-cutting enforcement matrix

Invariant Compile-time debug_assert release check Differential Fuzz Determinism gate
INV-1 parse total — progress — — ● —
INV-2 resolve det. — ● — ● — ●
INV-3 cycles wrapped path-split ● structural ● ● —
INV-4 live bindings SymbolId ● — ● — —
INV-5 effects kept lattice ● — ● ● —
INV-6 rename scope ScopeTree iso-check — ● ● —
INV-7 refs resolve — panic structural ● ● —

7.14 Global invariant methodology

  • --strict-invariants flag: promotes every debug_assert invariant to an always-on runtime check (CI + paranoid builds); off by default in release for speed. A breach prints the invariant id, span(s), and the inv::* trace context, then aborts the build (never ships).
  • Invariant tracing: all spans live under target bndl::inv; RUST_LOG=bndl::inv=debug produces a per-build invariant audit log; counters exported for the bench/CI dashboard.
  • The oracle as the soundness backstop: Tier-A invariants (4,5,6,7,10) are ultimately validated behaviorally against esbuild/Rollup + the #126 execution harness — structural asserts catch the known classes, the differential catches the unknown ones. A divergence is triaged: oracle-correct → bndl bug; spec-ambiguous → documented intentional deviation in the conformance notes.
  • First-failure protocol: (1) capture the failing input + config; (2) cargo fuzz tmin / manual-minimize; (3) reproduce under --strict-invariants to get the exact invariant id + span; (4) bisect the stage via per-stage dumps (--trace-resolve, graph --scc/--chunks, intermediate source maps); (5) add the minimized case to the corpus before fixing.
  • No-silent-degradation rule: every recovery path above is either provably safe (wrapper over hoist, retain over remove, miss over stale, error over crash) or a hard abort. bndl never trades correctness for size/speed/latency.

8. Domain Model — JS/TS Modules, Grammar, and the Module Graph

The domain is four layered models, each a lowering of the prior: bytes → tokens → AST → module graph → reachable/chunk model. Every layer is span-tagged (Span{start,end} byte offsets into one SourceId) so positions compose into the final source map via the #265 codec. Identifiers are interned Atom. All Module records are immutable + Send+Sync once built, enabling fearless parallel processing.

8.1 Tokenization model (bndl-lexer)

The lexer maps ECMAScript code points → TokenKind. It never panics: any unclassifiable sequence yields TokenKind::Invalid (parser recovers). had_newline_before is set when ≥1 LineTerminator precedes a token → feeds ASI.

Code-point classes (per ECMAScript lexical grammar §12.1–12.9):

Class Members Produces
WhiteSpace TAB, VT, FF, SP, NBSP, ZWNBSP (U+FEFF), Zs skipped
LineTerminator LF, CR, LS (U+2028), PS (U+2029) sets had_newline_before
IdentStart $ _ UnicodeIDStart, \u-escape Ident / Keyword
IdentPart IdentStart + UnicodeIDContinue + ZWNJ/ZWJ continues ident
DecimalDigit / 0x/0o/0b numeric Num(f64) / BigInt (trailing n)
" ' string body, escapes, line-continuation Str
` template — stateful (see below) NoSubTemplate/TemplateHead/Middle/Tail
/ divide or regex (context-dependent) Punct(Slash..) / Regex
# private name (#x) PrivateName
punctuators per §12.8 maximal-munch Punct(Punct)

Token kinds (TokenKind): Ident, PrivateName, Keyword(Keyword), Num(f64), BigInt, Str, NoSubTemplate, TemplateHead, TemplateMiddle, TemplateTail, Regex, Punct(Punct), JsxText, JsxIdent, Eof, Invalid.

Keywords are lexed as Ident then reclassified Keyword(_); contextual keywords (async, let, of, as, from, get, set, yield, await, TS type/interface/enum/declare) stay Ident and are resolved by the parser via position (avoids mode-dependent lexing).

Template-literal state machine — a template-stack of brace depths tracks nested ${...}:

            `...`                 `...${              }...${            }...`
   Start ──NoSubTemplate──▶ Done   ──TemplateHead──▶ InExpr ──TemplateMiddle──▶ InExpr ──TemplateTail──▶ Done
                                        │  push depth        ▲ on `}` at depth==tpl-brace re-enter tpl
                                        ▼                    │
                                   (normal token stream; `{`/`}` adjust depth)

A } only closes a template substitution when brace-depth returns to the template's recorded depth; otherwise it is Punct(RBrace). Unterminated ` → Invalid + ParseErrorKind::UnterminatedTemplate.

Regex-vs-divide disambiguation — / is ambiguous: regex literal vs division. The lexer holds a regex-context flag fed by the parser's expected-token state. Rule: a / begins a Regex when an expression is not yet in operand position (preceded by (, ,, =, :, [, !, &, |, ?, {, ;, return, typeof, keyword operators, or line start). It is Slash when preceded by an Ident, ), ], Num, Str, template tail, or this/literal (operand just closed). Invalid regex body → Invalid + ParseErrorKind::InvalidRegex.

ASI inputs — the lexer supplies had_newline_before; the parser inserts a virtual ; per the three ASI rules: (1) offending token on a new line; (2) } or EOF reached; (3) restricted productions (return/throw/break/continue/yield/post-++/--/arrow) where a LineTerminator forbids continuation.

8.2 Load-bearing grammar (EBNF)

Recursive descent for statements/declarations + Pratt for expressions. Syntax{typescript,jsx} gates TS/JSX productions. Representative subset (terminals in ''):

Program        ::= ( ModuleItem )* EOF ;
ModuleItem     ::= ImportDeclaration | ExportDeclaration | Statement ;

ImportDeclaration
  ::= 'import' ImportClause 'from' ModuleSpecifier ';'
    | 'import' ModuleSpecifier ';'                          (* side-effect import *)
    | 'import' 'type' ImportClause 'from' ModuleSpecifier ';'   (* TS, erased *) ;
ImportClause   ::= DefaultBinding ( ',' NameSpaceImport | ',' NamedImports )?
                 | NameSpaceImport | NamedImports ;
NameSpaceImport::= '*' 'as' Ident ;
NamedImports   ::= '{' ( ImportSpec ( ',' ImportSpec )* ','? )? '}' ;
ImportSpec     ::= ( Ident | String ) ( 'as' Ident )? ;
ModuleSpecifier::= String ;

ExportDeclaration
  ::= 'export' '*' ( 'as' Ident )? 'from' ModuleSpecifier ';'   (* export-star / export-as-namespace *)
    | 'export' NamedExports ( 'from' ModuleSpecifier )? ';'
    | 'export' 'default' ( AssignmentExpression | FuncDecl | ClassDecl ) ';'
    | 'export' Declaration ;
NamedExports   ::= '{' ( ExportSpec ( ',' ExportSpec )* ','? )? '}' ;
ExportSpec     ::= ( Ident | String ) ( 'as' ( Ident | String ) )? ;

ImportExpression ::= 'import' '(' AssignmentExpression ( ',' AssignmentExpression )? ')'  (* dynamic *)
                   | 'import' '.' 'meta' ;
ClassDeclaration ::= 'abstract'? 'class' Ident TypeParams? Heredity? '{' ClassElement* '}' ;  (* 'abstract','implements',TypeParams: TS, erased *)

(* Pratt expression core — driven by binding powers, not nested BNF *)
Expression       ::= AssignmentExpression ( ',' AssignmentExpression )* ;
AssignmentExpression ::= ArrowFunction | YieldExpression
                       | ConditionalExpression
                       | LeftHandSide AssignmentOperator AssignmentExpression ;
PrattExpr(rbp)   ::= NUD ( while lbp(peek) > rbp : LED )* ;   (* see binding-power table 8.2.1 *)

8.2.1 Pratt binding-power table (lbp; higher binds tighter)

Operator(s) lbp Assoc LED form
, (sequence)¹ 1 L Bin/Seq
= += … &&= ` = ??=` 2
? : 3 R Cond
?? 4 L² Bin
` ` 5
&& 6 L Bin
` ^ &` 7–9 L
== != === !== 10 L Bin
< > <= >= instanceof in³ 11 L Bin
<< >> >>> 12 L Bin
+ - 13 L Bin
* / % 14 L Bin
** 15 R Bin
prefix ! ~ + - typeof void delete await (unary NUD) 16 — Unary/Await
postfix ++ -- 17 — Update (ASI-restricted)
new 18 — New
. ?. [] () tag`...` 19 L Member/OptChain/Call/Template

¹ sequence lbp suppressed inside argument/array/declarator parsing. ² ?? cannot mix with &&/|| without parens (parse error). ³ in disabled while parsing for(...;;) init header.

8.3 AST mapping (production → AST variant)

Program{items: Vec<ModuleItem>, source_type} where source_type ∈ {Module, Script} (CJS files = Script).

Grammar production AST node (bndl-ast)
ImportDeclaration ModuleItem::Import(ImportDecl{specifiers, source: Atom, span})
export { … } [from] ModuleItem::Export(ExportDecl::Named{specifiers, source: Option<Atom>})
export default E ExportDecl::Default(Box<Expr>)
export * [as ns] from ExportDecl::All{source: Atom, alias: Option<Atom>}
export <decl> ExportDecl::Decl(Box<Stmt>)
ExpressionStatement Stmt::Expr(Box<Expr>)
var/let/const Stmt::Var(VarDecl)
function decl Stmt::Func(FuncDecl)
class decl Stmt::Class(ClassDecl)
{ … } Stmt::Block(Vec<Stmt>)
BinaryExpr / logical Expr::Bin(BinExpr)
a.b / a[b] Expr::Member(MemberExpr)
a?.b chain Expr::Optional(OptChain)
f(x) Expr::Call(CallExpr)
import(x) dynamic Expr::Import(ImportExpr)
arrow => Expr::Arrow(ArrowExpr)
`…${}…` Expr::Template(..)
identifier reference Expr::Ident(Ident{sym: Atom, span, node_id})
TS type annot/interface/enum/as/satisfies/declare/decorators erased in Phase-6 TypeStrip (parsed, never emitted)
JSX element/fragment Expr::Call after JsxTransform (createElement or jsx)

Every node carries node_id: NodeId (assigned monotonically at parse) + span. Scope-id placeholders are filled by Phase-8 ScopeTree.

8.4 Module-resolution decision model (bndl-resolver)

Specifier is classified first: Relative (./, ../), Absolute (/, drive), or Bare{pkg,sub}. ResolveResult{path, kind, side_effects, from_node_modules}. tsconfig paths/baseUrl run before node_modules.

resolve(spec, importer):
  ┌─ tsConfig.paths match? ──yes──▶ rewrite to candidates ──▶ probe ──hit─▶ ResolveResult
  │                          no
  ▼
  Relative|Absolute? ──yes──▶ join(importer_dir, spec) ──▶ LOAD_AS_FILE ─miss─▶ LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY ─miss─▶ ResolveError::NotFound
  │ no (Bare)
  ▼
  ESM importer (type:module / .mjs)?
     ├─ yes ▶ PACKAGE_RESOLVE → PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE (if "exports")  else legacy main/module
     └─ no  ▶ LOAD_NODE_MODULES (CJS)

CJS LOAD_NODE_MODULES — walk ancestor node_modules/:

Step Probe path Hit → Miss →
1 <ancestor>/node_modules/<pkg> (exact) realpath → ResolveResult next probe
2 <ancestor>/node_modules/<pkg>.{js,json,node,…extensions} realpath → ResolveResult next probe
3 <ancestor>/node_modules/<pkg>/package.json#main_fields (["browser"?,"module"?,"main"] in order) LOAD_AS_FILE(main_field_value) → ResolveResult probe index
4 <ancestor>/node_modules/<pkg>/index.{js,json,node} realpath → ResolveResult next ancestor dir
— no ancestor node_modules/ matched — ResolveError::PackageNotFound

All hits pass through realpath(symlink) → canonical path (symlink realpath cache); symlink cycles detected and capped.

ESM PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE — condition + pattern matching against package.json#exports:

exports == string                     → that target (only sub == ".")
exports == { ".": …, "./sub": … }     → exact key, else pattern key "./*" (capture * = sub-tail)
target == string                      → must start "./", forbid "../"/abs escapes → InvalidExports
target == { import|require|browser|node|development|production|default : … }
        → match Condition list LEFT→RIGHT in ResolveOptions.conditions (first match wins; recurse)
target == [ a, b ]                    → first that resolves
no key/condition matches              → ResolveError::NotExported
"imports" (#-prefixed)                → same algorithm, internal specifiers

Condition ordering (ResolveOptions.conditions, build default): ["import","module","browser"?,"node"?,"production"|"development","default"]. Condition::Custom(Atom) allows user keys. "browser" field/condition active when targeting browser; flips Node builtins → ModuleKind::External/polyfill.

ResolveError Trigger
NotFound relative path + no extension/index hit
PackageNotFound bare pkg absent from all ancestor node_modules
InvalidExports exports target escapes package root / not ./-prefixed
NotExported subpath not in exports map (encapsulation)
BadTsPaths tsconfig paths pattern malformed / no baseUrl

8.5 Module-graph + live-binding model (bndl-graph)

ModuleGraph{modules: Vec<Module>, entries, edges, cycles}. Each Module{id: ModuleId, path, kind: ModuleKind, ast, imports, exports, symbols, has_side_effects}. ModuleKind ∈ {Esm, Cjs, Json, Css, Asset, Builtin, External}.

Records:

ImportRecord { specifier: Atom, resolved: Option<ModuleId>, kind: ImportKind }   // Static|Dynamic|Require|ExportStar
ExportRecord { name: Atom, local: Option<SymbolId>, reexport: Option<(ModuleId, Atom)> }
Symbol       { name: Atom, kind: SymbolKind, local: bool }                       // SymbolId interned per module
Binding      // (scope-level link from a reference to its defining Symbol)

Live bindings — an ESM import is a live, read-only view of the exporter's binding, not a value copy. Invariant: rename/tree-shake/concatenate must preserve (a) binding identity (all importers + exporter alias the same storage) and (b) mutation visibility (an exporter reassigning export let x is observed by importers). After scope hoisting (Phase 8) the binding becomes one shared variable; in wrapped mode it is a getter on the namespace object. Default export = synthetic export named default.

export-star flattening — export * from 'm' (ExportDecl::All{alias:None}) re-exports all of m's named exports except default and any name explicitly bound by the importer (local exports + earlier stars shadow). Resolved by a fixed-point walk across transitive stars; an ambiguous name (two stars export same name) is silently excluded (per spec), not an error, unless directly imported → then BadImport/missing-export. export * as ns (alias:Some) instead binds a single namespace symbol.

Cycle handling — Tarjan SCC over static-import edges records cycles. ESM cycles are legal: bindings are hoisted/TDZ-checked; the graph keeps them and Phase-8 forces the wrapped lazy-init fallback for any module in a cycle that needs ordering. CJS require cycle → partial module.exports snapshot returned (the in-progress object), matching Node; not an error.

CJS↔ESM interop —

Direction Bridge Helper (Phase 10)
ESM imports CJS CJS module.exports value snapshot wrapped as ESM namespace; default = whole exports __toESM
CJS requires ESM ESM namespace materialized eagerly into a value object __commonJS wrapper + __esm
named import from CJS best-effort static keys; unknown → runtime getter __export

ImportKind::Require marks CJS edges (always wrapped, never concatenated). Dynamic marks import() edges → chunk boundary (Phase 9).

8.6 Worked example — 3 modules: tokens → AST → graph → reachable → chunks → ESM

Project: entry.ts (entry) imports local util.ts + bare pkg lib (conditional exports). Build target ESM, tree-shaking on.

entry.ts

// entry.ts — module surface (import declarations + reference notes; not runnable)
import { used } from "./util";   // referenced via call site used()
import { hello } from "lib";     // referenced as a value
// entry body references: used(), hello  → both symbols reachable from entry

util.ts

// util.ts — exported declarations (arrow bodies elided, shown as type)
export const used: () => number;   // body returns 41 + 1; referenced → KEPT
export const dead: () => string;   // body returns "tree-shaken"; unreferenced + Pure + sideEffects:false → ELIMINATED

lib/package.json: { "exports": { ".": { "import": "./esm/index.js", "require": "./cjs/index.js" } }, "sideEffects": false } lib/esm/index.js: export const hello = "hi";

(1) Tokens (entry.ts, abbreviated): Keyword(import) Punct({) Ident(used) Punct(}) Ident(from)* Str("./util") Punct(;) … (*from lexed as Ident, parser-reclassified). () => 41 + 1 in util.ts: Punct(() Punct()) Punct(=>) Num(41) Punct(+) Num(1).

(2) AST — entry: [Import(ImportDecl{spec:[used], source:"./util"}), Import(ImportDecl{spec:[hello], source:"lib"}), Stmt::Expr(Call(console.log, [Call(used), Ident(hello)]))]. util: two ExportDecl::Decl(Var(const …)), each a Expr::Arrow. TS : number / (): number erased by TypeStrip.

(3) Graph —

ModuleId(0)=entry.ts  imports: [Static→util(1), Static→lib(2)]   exports: []
ModuleId(1)=util.ts   exports: [used→S0, dead→S1]   has_side_effects:false
ModuleId(2)=lib       (exports.import → ./esm/index.js) kind:Esm  side_effects:false (pkg sideEffects:false)
ModuleId(3)=lib/esm/index.js  exports:[hello→S0]
entries=[0]   cycles=[]

Resolver picked lib via PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE with Condition::Import first → ./esm/index.js.

(4) Reachable symbols (TreeShaker from entries): entry uses util.used + lib.hello. used reachable; dead unreferenced + arrow is Pure + module sideEffects:false → eliminated. ReachableSet = {entry:*, util:[used], lib/index:[hello]}.

(5) Chunks (module groups): all modules reachable only from entry-0 → single entry chunk; no dynamic import → no async chunk; no shared chunk. Chunk{id:0, modules:[0,1,3], entry:Some(0), is_dynamic:false} (module 2 collapses to 3). Hash computed over final content.

(6) Emitted ESM (scope-hoisted, dead gone, identifiers kept—no minify):

// Emitted ESM — module-by-module result (declarations + reference notes, not runnable):
//   util.ts        → declares `used` (body returns 41 + 1)            [kept]
//   lib/esm/index  → declares `hello` (value "hi")                    [kept]
//   entry.ts       → references used(), hello                         [both reachable]
// One shared `const` per module via concatenation (no wrapper — pure ESM, acyclic).

Live binding used/hello became one shared const per module (concatenation, no wrapper — pure ESM, acyclic).

8.7 Malformed / edge examples → error

Input Stage Result
const t = `a${b (no closing) lexer Invalid token + ParseErrorKind::UnterminatedTemplate; parser recovers at ;/EOF
const s = "ab (no close quote) lexer ParseErrorKind::UnterminatedString
import x from 123; (specifier not string) parser ParseErrorKind::BadImport (ModuleSpecifier must be Str)
import { gone } from "./util"; (gone not exported) graph missing-export diagnostic; ImportSpec resolves to no ExportRecord → hard error (strict) / runtime undefined (loose CJS)
import "no-such-pkg"; (bare, absent) resolver ResolveError::PackageNotFound → fatal build error w/ importer span
CJS a.js: require('./b') ↔ b.js: require('./a') graph cycle recorded; require returns partial module.exports snapshot (Node semantics) — not an error
dynamic import('./a') where a dynamically imports ./b which imports ./a graph/chunk circular dynamic edge tolerated; each import() is a chunk boundary + async; runtime promise resolves with in-flight module record (no deadlock — memoized)
a as b in ??/&& mix without parens parser ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken (coalesce-mix rule)
TS let x: Foo with Syntax.typescript=false parser ParseErrorKind::TrailingTsType
<div/> with Syntax.jsx=false parser ParseErrorKind::JsxNotEnabled

8.8 Incremental rebuild after one file edit (sequence)

Edit to util.ts only. bndl-cache keys = CacheKey = BLAKE3(content + options); only changed-key artifacts recompute.

Watcher        Server/Orchestrator     Cache(BLAKE3)        Graph            Treeshake/Chunk/Codegen
   │ fs event(util.ts)  │                    │                 │                       │
   ├───────────────────▶│ invalidate(util)   │                 │                       │
   │                    ├───────────────────▶│ evict key(util) │                       │
   │                    │ reparse util ──────▶│ miss→Parsed     │                       │
   │                    │ retransform util ──▶│ miss→Transformed│                       │
   │                    │ rescan imports/exports ───────────────▶ update Module(1)      │
   │                    │  (entry, lib AST: cache HIT — reused, not reparsed)           │
   │                    │ exports of util changed? ─────────────▶ if export SET same →  │
   │                    │                    │                 │  importers unaffected  │
   │                    │ reshake (incremental from dirty set) ─────────────────────────▶ ReachableSet Δ
   │                    │ rechunk (only chunks touching util) ──────────────────────────▶ re-emit chunk(s)
   │                    │ stable hash recompute → new file_name only if content changed  │
   │                    │ (dev: send HMR update over ws to ModuleRunner; HMR boundary)   │

Invariant: identical inputs+options → byte-identical output (stable module order, rename order, fixed-point chunk hashing). If util.ts's export set / signature is unchanged, importer artifacts stay cache-hits; only util's own parse→transform→emit recomputes, plus any chunk physically containing it (rehash). HMR (Phase 12) finds the nearest HmrBoundary{module, accepts}; if none accepts, full-reload fallback.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

Every load-bearing decision, locked. Status: Accepted unless noted. Rejected alternatives carry the reason rejected, not just the name — re-litigation must clear that bar. ADRs are immutable once shipped in a release; supersede, never edit.

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 Go (esbuild): GC pauses on 10k+-module graphs, weak sum types for AST. TypeScript: self-hosting irony, no alloc/parallelism control, slow. C/C++: memory-unsafe on an untrusted-source hot path. Untrusted source on hot path → memory safety non-negotiable; enums + exhaustive match model AST/passes at zero cost; bump-arena + Send+Sync immutable Module → fearless parallel processing; refs (SWC/OXC/Rolldown/Turbopack) are Rust → 1:1 reading. Go wins when the primary concern is dev-team velocity over a small/trusted corpus (≤1k modules, GC pauses tolerable) and a large existing Go codebase must host the tool — esbuild's production regime. TypeScript wins for a bundler shipped as a Node plugin where FFI cost exceeds any Rust speed advantage and the graph is small enough to fit in V8 with acceptable GC.
ADR-02 Repo structure Single Cargo workspace, 12 bndl-* members One monolithic crate; many micro-crates. Crate = compile/test boundary + enforced layering (bndl-graph deps bndl-parser+bndl-resolver, never inverse); 12 = the natural pipeline stages; avoids both god-crate rebuild cost and micro-crate version churn. One monolithic crate wins when the project is a standalone binary with no external consumers needing partial compilation (prototype/research tools). Many micro-crates win when each pipeline stage ships as an independently versioned library consumed by separate downstream projects (e.g., a parser crate vendored by a type checker).
ADR-03 Tokenizer Hand-written Lexer (bndl-lexer), byte-at-a-time code-point classifier logos/regex-DFA generator; lexer-generator (project #63). Need parser-fed regex-vs-divide context, template-literal nesting stack, had_newline_before for ASI, never-panic Invalid-token recovery — none expressible in a stateless DFA; also >1M LOC/s/core (P1). logos/DFA generator wins for a language with a stateless, context-free lexer grammar (e.g., JSON, TOML, simple DSLs) where throughput matters but no parser-fed disambiguation or multi-mode state is required, and where rapid extension by contributors unfamiliar with lexer internals is a priority.
ADR-04 Parser strategy Hand-written recursive-descent (stmts/decls) + Pratt (expr binding powers) Parser generator / LALR; parser-combinators (project #63). Full control over ES2022+TS+JSX ambiguities, error recovery, span+NodeId tagging, and speed; Pratt handles operator precedence cleanly; generators give worse errors and no recovery. LALR/parser-generator wins for a well-specified, unambiguous grammar that changes rarely and where grammar-as-documentation and automated follow-set analysis outweigh recovery control (e.g., a config-file or protocol grammar shipped in a safety-critical system with formal verification requirements). Parser-combinators win for exploratory DSL prototyping where composability and testability of small rules matter more than peak throughput.
ADR-05 AST shape ESTree-shaped, span-tagged, NodeId-tagged (bndl-ast) Bespoke IR; untyped JSON-ish tree. ESTree → maps 1:1 to SWC/OXC/Babel references and tooling expectations; spans drive source maps; typed enums give exhaustive transform passes. Bespoke IR wins when the target language diverges significantly from JavaScript (e.g., a Rust MIR-like IR for a new language), the reference ecosystem is absent, and a tighter IR enables optimizations not possible in an ESTree-shaped tree. Untyped JSON-ish tree wins for rapid prototyping where the tree shape is still being discovered and static typing would require constant churn.
ADR-06 Symbol/string storage Interned Atom (global dedup interner) String per node; Rc<str>. Identifier-heavy code → interning keeps memory ≤ source-bytes order (not per-occurrence); Atom equality = u32 compare → fast scope/symbol lookups. String per node wins in a tool processing tiny files or when strings are mostly unique (e.g., a log-line parser where each value is a UUID) and interning overhead exceeds its memory benefit. Rc<str> wins when strings must be shared across unrelated lifetimes without a global interner and the interning corpus is too small to amortize interner overhead.
ADR-07 AST allocation Arena / bump per module, freed as one block post-codegen Per-node Box; refcounted Rc. Kills per-node alloc/free churn; whole-module free is O(1); bound RSS < 6× source bytes; immutable post-build module is trivially Send+Sync. Per-node Box wins when nodes must be individually live after their compilation unit is discarded (e.g., a language server that keeps partial ASTs alive for completion queries independently of file lifetime). Rc wins when nodes are shared across multiple owners with non-deterministic lifetimes and an arena's all-or-nothing free would hold too much live data.
ADR-08 AST traversal Visit (read) / VisitMut (in-place rewrite) trait pair Babel-style immutable fold returning new nodes. In-place rewrite avoids reallocating the tree each pass; VisitMut driver runs the ordered TransformPass list once; Visit powers scan/treeshake without mutation. Immutable fold wins when transform passes must be composed functionally without shared mutable state, when the old and new trees must coexist simultaneously for diffing (e.g., incremental IDE error-recovery), or when persistent/versioned AST snapshots are required.
ADR-09 Module resolution From-scratch Node CJS + ESM algorithms (bndl-resolver): PACKAGE_RESOLVE / PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE / ESM_FILE_FORMAT + LOAD_AS_FILE/DIRECTORY/NODE_MODULES oxc_resolver/node-resolve crate; shell out to Node. Need exact control over Condition matching, tsconfig paths-before-node_modules, browser-field remap, symlink realpath, and a warm cache hitting < 1 µs (P9); external dep would dictate semantics + caching. oxc_resolver wins if the project accepts the external crate's semantics and maintenance burden, prioritizes contribution from engineers unfamiliar with the resolution spec, and < 1 µs per-call hot-cache isn't a hard requirement. Shell-out to Node wins for a pure dev-tool prototype where correctness must match Node exactly and engineering bandwidth for spec-faithful reimplementation is absent.
ADR-10 CJS↔ESM interop ModuleGraph live-binding model: ESM import = live read-only view; CJS exports = require-time snapshot; bridged by runtime helpers Treat all modules as CJS; treat all as ESM. Live bindings are observable (mutation visibility, TDZ) and must survive shaking/renaming/concatenation; collapsing either way is a correctness bug. All-CJS wins for a bundler targeting only Node.js CJS consumers where live-binding semantics are never observable by downstream code and the full interop helper set bloats every output. All-ESM wins for a future where the dependency graph is 100% ESM (no CJS), all platforms support native ESM, and the interop overhead is purely technical debt.
ADR-11 Tree-shaking granularity Symbol-level reachability (SymbolGraph/TreeShaker) from entry exports Module-level (Webpack usedExports flag); none. Symbol granularity drops unused named exports + their transitive symbols within a kept module; honors sideEffects:false + /*#__PURE__*/; over-removal is a correctness bug, never an optimization. Module-level shaking wins when the dependency graph is dominated by CJS modules (shaking requires ESM static analysis), when compile time is more constrained than output size, or when the build target is a Node.js CLI where the size difference between symbol- and module-level shaking is negligible against startup latency.
ADR-12 Module merge strategy Scope hoisting / module concatenation for safe ESM + wrapped lazy-init fallback Always-wrap every module (Webpack classic); always-hoist. Hoisting (Rollup/esbuild) → one scope, fewer closures, smaller+faster output; unsafe for CJS/cyclic/eval → wrap with first-call memoization; Renamer keeps hoisting scope-correct. Always-wrap wins when the module graph is dominated by CJS or cycles (most modules would fall back anyway), when eval usage is pervasive in the target codebase, or when debugging a hoisted bundle is so difficult that the runtime overhead of per-module closures is an acceptable trade for debuggability.
ADR-13 Code splitting Module-group (esbuild/Rollup): chunk = set of entry points (static+dynamic) that reach a module; import() → chunk boundary (bndl-chunk) Webpack SplitChunks size/count heuristics; one chunk per module; no splitting. Reachable-entry-set is deterministic + minimal-duplication; heuristics are tunable knobs that defeat reproducibility; matches our determinism contract (ADR-14). SplitChunks heuristics win when the entry-set explosion problem (many entries × many dynamic imports = exponential shared chunks) dominates and a few empirically-tuned size/count knobs are more practical than a purely deterministic but chunk-explosion-prone algorithm. No splitting wins for a library bundler (single entry, single output) or a CLI tool where HTTP/2 push is unavailable and one monolithic chunk minimizes round trips.
ADR-14 Output determinism / hashing BLAKE3 content hash of final chunk content; fixed-point pass for cross-chunk hash refs; stable module + rename order SipHash/xxhash/SHA-256; mtime/counter naming. Byte-identical output for identical inputs+opts → enables bndl-cache reuse + long-term CDN caching; BLAKE3 = fast + collision-resistant; fixed-point resolves the hash-references-hash cycle. xxhash wins for an internal tool where collision resistance is unnecessary and the absolute maximum hashing throughput is the only metric. mtime/counter naming wins for a local dev build where determinism is irrelevant (never deployed to CDN, never distributed) and the fixed-point convergence cost exceeds its benefit.
ADR-15 Module runtime Injected helpers: __require/__esm/__commonJS/__toESM/__export (bndl-codegen) Per-bundle bespoke glue; Webpack __webpack_require__ runtime verbatim. Minimal, tree-shakeable, esbuild-style helper set; only the helpers a bundle uses are emitted; __toESM/__commonJS implement the ADR-10 interop. Webpack __webpack_require__ verbatim wins when the deployment target is an existing Webpack runtime host (Webpack Module Federation) and the bundle must register modules into an existing Webpack registry without re-implementing its module-graph semantics. Per-bundle bespoke glue wins for a single-module IIFE output where the only runtime needed is a trivial self-executing wrapper.
ADR-16 Output formats ESM / CJS / IIFE via OutputFormat wrappers ESM-only; AMD/UMD/SystemJS. Covers modern (ESM), Node (CJS), browser <script> (IIFE) targets; AMD/UMD are legacy and out of scope; one Emitter + format-specific wrapper. AMD/UMD wins when the deployment environment is a legacy enterprise SPA on RequireJS that cannot be migrated to native ESM or a bundled entrypoint, and the cost of maintaining a loader outweighs the cost of emitting AMD wrappers. SystemJS wins when targeting older browsers through the SystemJS polyfill layer that predates widespread ESM support.
ADR-17 Minifier In-house Minifier: scope-aware mangle + DCE + constant fold (bndl-codegen) Shell out to terser/esbuild minify; swc_ecma_minifier crate. Operates directly on our renamed/hoisted AST + ScopeTree → no re-parse; preserves source-map composition; > 500k LOC/s/core (P8); external tool breaks determinism + map chaining. terser wins for a build pipeline that needs aggressive micro-optimizations (e.g., property mangling, arguments object removal, advanced constant propagation across function boundaries) that a from-scratch minifier would take months to implement and where source-map fidelity is not a hard requirement. swc_ecma_minifier wins if the project is already in the SWC ecosystem and can accept the SWC AST as the common representation.
ADR-18 Source maps Project #265 codec for all VLQ/compose Hand-roll VLQ + JSON map; sourcemap crate. Every stage (lex→parse→transform→print→minify) composes deltas; bndl never hand-rolls VLQ — #265 owns the format; this blueprint references, never re-specifies it. sourcemap crate wins for a standalone tool with no upstream map composition requirements (single-stage transform) and when the portfolio does not include project #265. Hand-rolled VLQ wins only for an embedded environment with no crate dependencies allowed and a single-stage emit with no composition.
ADR-19 Incremental cache Content-addressed Cache keyed by CacheKey = BLAKE3(content+options); in-mem + on-disk; change-driven invalidation mtime/timestamp cache; no cache. Content addressing → correct across branch switches/clock skew where mtime lies; memoizes parse/transform/codegen Artifacts → < 50 ms incremental rebuild (P3). mtime cache wins for a single-machine interactive tool where clock skew is impossible, the cost of hashing every file on every rebuild (I/O-bound BLAKE3 over many small files) exceeds the mtime-check cost, and correctness across branch switches is not required. No cache wins for a CI-only pipeline where every build starts from scratch and cache invalidation bugs are unacceptable.
ADR-20 Parallelism rayon thread pool over immutable Modules; one core reserved for orchestrator std::thread hand-rolled; tokio async; single-threaded. Module processing is embarrassingly parallel + CPU-bound (not I/O-bound) → work-stealing data parallelism, not async; Send+Sync arena modules make it safe; scales ~linearly to cores−1. tokio async wins when the bottleneck is I/O-bound module loading from a remote registry or network filesystem, where waiting for I/O dominates CPU work and async multiplexing outperforms thread-per-module. Single-threaded wins for a tiny-graph tool (≤100 modules) where thread-pool overhead exceeds the parallelism benefit, or for a WASM target where threads are unavailable.
ADR-21 Dev server Vite-style ESM-native DevServer + on-demand transform + dependency pre-bundling; websocket HMR (HmrEngine/HmrBoundary/ModuleRunner) Full rebuild-on-save; SSE/long-poll for HMR; bundle-everything dev. Serve unbundled ESM on demand → < 15 ms p95 transform (P4); ws = low-latency bidirectional push for < 30 ms HMR propagation (P6); boundary computation limits reload scope. Full rebuild-on-save wins when the browser target does not support native ESM modules (old IE/Edge) or when complex non-JS assets (CSS modules, WASM, binary assets) require a full bundling pass even in dev mode. SSE wins when the HMR client is in a restricted iframe environment where WebSocket connections are blocked by CSP.
ADR-22 Extensibility Plugin trait: resolve/load/transform/render_chunk hooks, hosted in bndl-cli No plugins (closed); dynamic dlopen/wasm plugins. Rollup/Vite-shaped first-class hook contract covers the real extension points; in-process trait objects = type-safe + fast; dynamic loading deferred (security + ABI cost). Dynamic dlopen/wasm plugins win when the bundler is a long-running server daemon (not a short-lived CLI) and plugins must be hot-swapped without restarting the process, or when plugins are written in languages other than Rust and WASM sandboxing is needed for security isolation. No plugins win for a tightly-scoped single-purpose bundler where extensibility adds surface area without value.
ADR-23 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer): lex / parse / parse-print roundtrip targets No fuzzing; AFL only; property tests only. Untrusted-source prime directive (never panic/hang/OOM) → continuous coverage-guided fuzzing is the enforcement mechanism; roundtrip target catches printer/parser asymmetry. AFL wins when the fuzzing corpus must run on a non-x86 target (e.g., ARM embedded, WASM) where libFuzzer is unavailable or when the AFL persistent-mode integration is already established. Property tests only win for a tool with a purely trusted input domain (internal tool, not user-facing) where coverage-guided fuzzing setup cost exceeds the safety benefit.
ADR-24 Correctness oracle Differential vs esbuild + Rollup (dev-dep/feature-gated) on a fixture suite Golden-file snapshots only; hand-written assertions only. Real bundlers are the behavioral spec for interop/shaking/splitting edge cases; differential testing surfaces divergence we'd never enumerate by hand. Golden-file snapshots win when the reference bundler's behavior is itself incorrect or unstable across versions, and the goal is to define a new behavioral spec rather than match an existing one. Hand-written assertions win for narrow unit tests of individual algorithmic components (e.g., testing the Pratt parser in isolation) where differential comparison against a full bundler is too coarse.
ADR-25 Conformance corpus Test262 (parser conformance) + bundling fixture suite Ad-hoc samples; no external corpus. Test262 is the normative ECMAScript conformance suite → objective parser coverage gate; fixtures gate end-to-end bundle semantics. Ad-hoc samples win during early prototyping when Test262 integration overhead (harness, skip lists, result normalization) delays a working parser by weeks and the immediate goal is a proof-of-concept, not production conformance.
ADR-26 Benchmarking criterion micro + end-to-end benches vs section-4 targets std::time ad-hoc; no benches. Statistical rigor (outlier detection, regression flags) → perf targets P1–P10 are CI-enforced, not aspirational. std::time ad-hoc wins for a one-off profiling session where statistical rigor is unnecessary (single run to find an obvious hotspot) and the criterion overhead (warm-up, multiple samples, HTML report) is more friction than value. No benches win for a pure-correctness prototype where performance is explicitly deferred and the benchmark infrastructure would be throwaway.
ADR-27 CI GitHub Actions: fmt + clippy -D warnings + build + test + fuzz-smoke + bench-regression None; bespoke runner. Standard, free for the workspace; gates every PR on the production bar; bench job flags perf regressions against P1–P10. Bespoke runner wins when the project requires hardware-specific CI (custom GPUs, specific CPU microarchitectures for SIMD benchmarks, or bare-metal latency measurements) that GitHub Actions hosted runners cannot provide, or when the organization already operates a self-hosted Buildkite/TeamCity cluster with cost advantages at scale.
ADR-28 Dependency policy Minimal; no proc-macro deps in the hot core (bndl-lexer/-ast/-parser); std elsewhere Liberal crate use; serde_derive in core types. Hot-path crates must compile fast + carry no surprise codegen/alloc; proc-macros inflate compile time + obscure cost; each dep is an audited supply-chain + ABI liability. Liberal crate use with serde_derive wins for a tool that must serialize/deserialize its AST across process boundaries (language server protocol, build cache serialized to JSON/CBOR) and where the compile-time cost of derive macros is acceptable given the total compile budget.
ADR-29 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual) GPL/copyleft; proprietary. Ecosystem-standard permissive dual license; matches Rust + the reference bundlers; maximizes adoption; all deps must be license-compatible. GPL/copyleft wins when the goal is to ensure improvements to the bundler must be contributed back (a political/business choice, not a technical one) and the user base is exclusively open-source projects unencumbered by copyleft restrictions. Proprietary wins for a commercially-sold product where the tool itself is the revenue-generating artifact.

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace, 12 bndl-* members under crates/. Layout is phase-aligned: each crate maps to one phase (§12–§25), each crate's internal module set maps to that phase's deliverable. Governance + legal + ops live at root; conformance/fuzz/bench are workspace-external dev infra. No code in this section — the tree fixes file homes for every type named in the canonical spine.

10.1 Top-level tree

  • bndl/
    • README.mdwhat bndl is, quickstart (build a.js -> ESM), feature matrix vs esbuild/Rollup/Vite, non-goals (no typecheck/#42, no JS exec/#126, no map format/#265)
    • CONTRIBUTING.mddev setup, make targets, PR rules: clippy -D warnings, fmt, no panics on untrusted input, differential-oracle must stay green
    • CODE_OF_CONDUCT.mdContributor Covenant v2.1
    • SECURITY.mddisclosure policy; parser is the untrusted-input attack surface; fuzz expectations; see docs/threat-model.md
    • CHANGELOG.mdKeep-a-Changelog; SemVer; output-determinism + runtime-helper changes flagged BREAKING (CDN cache + map impact)
    • BLUEPRINT_LOG.mdappend-only phase-completion ledger (date, phase number, acceptance gate hit, oracle diff count)
    • BUILD_PLAN.mdphase order §12..§25, crate dependency DAG, milestone gates, "done" = §25 conformance/perf gate
    • LICENSE-MITdual MIT
    • LICENSE-APACHEdual Apache-2.0 (+ NOTICE)
    • Makefilebuild|test|fmt|clippy|fuzz|bench|conformance|oracle|serve-example|coverage|deny targets
    • Dockerfilemulti-stage: rust:1.78 builder -> distroless runtime; static-ish bndl binary; reproducible build args
    • docker-compose.ymldev: bndl serve on :5173 + ws HMR, watched ./examples volume, hot reload
    • rust-toolchain.tomlchannel="1.78" (MSRV pin), components=[rustfmt,clippy], profile=minimal
    • deny.tomlcargo-deny: license allowlist (MIT/Apache/BSD), advisory DB gate, ban duplicate/yanked deps
    • Cargo.toml[workspace] members=crates/* + fuzz; resolver="2"; shared [workspace.dependencies]; release profile lto=true codegen-units=1 panic="abort"
    • Cargo.lockcommitted (binary crate; reproducible builds)
    • .gitignore/target, /conformance/test262 (submodule), *.profraw, fuzz/corpus artifacts
    • rustfmt.tomledition=2021, max_width=100
    • clippy.tomlcognitive-complexity + msrv=1.78 lints
    • crates/the 12 workspace members (§10.2)
    • benches/criterion harnesses crossing crates (§10.3) -> section-4 perf targets
    • conformance/Test262 + bundling fixtures + differential oracle (§10.4)
    • fuzz/cargo-fuzz targets (§10.5)
    • examples/runnable sample projects exercising features end-to-end (§10.6)
    • docs/ADRs, runbooks, threat model, perf, failure analysis (§10.7)
    • templates/scaffolding: new-pass, new-plugin, fixture skeletons
    • scripts/dev/CI shell+python: import-test262, gen-oracle, hash-check, update-corpus
    • tools/auxiliary Rust bins not shipped (oracle-runner, map-validator-#265-shim, corpus-minimizer)
    • notes/design scratch, parser-grammar notes, resolver edge-case log
    • papers/archived normative refs index (TC39 ES spec, TS grammar, JSX, Node resolution algos)
    • legal/third-party license texts, NOTICE aggregation, SPDX manifest
    • brand/logo/svg, name/usage guidelines
    • .github/
      • workflows/
        • ci.ymlfmt + clippy -D warnings + build + test (matrix: linux/mac, stable+MSRV)
        • fuzz.ymlscheduled cargo-fuzz smoke (5 min/target) + crash-corpus regression replay
        • bench.ymlcriterion on perf-labeled PRs; regression > threshold fails
        • release.ymltag -> build binaries (linux/mac/win), publish crates, attach Dockerfile image

10.2 crates/ — the twelve members

Each crate is Cargo.toml + src/ + colocated tests/ (integration) + per-crate README.md. Internal module list below is the complete src/ set per the spine; deps shown are the locked DAG.

crates/ — parsing crates: bndl-lexer, bndl-ast, bndl-parser (Phases 1–3)

  • crates/
    • bndl-lexer/Phase 1 (§13) — ECMAScript/TS/JSX tokenizer. deps: std
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports; pub Token, TokenKind, Lexer, Span, SourceId
        • token.rsTokenKind enum (Ident,PrivateName,Keyword,Num(f64),BigInt,Str,
  • template variants,Regex,Punct,Jsx*,Eof,Invalid);
  • Token{kind,span,had_newline_before}
    • lexer.rsLexer<'a>: byte cursor; regex-vs-divide ctx flag;
  • template-stack; ASI had_newline_before; never panics -> Invalid
    • classifier.rscode-point classifier: IdentifierStart/Part (Unicode),
  • whitespace, line-terminators, digit/hex/bin/oct
    • span.rsSpan{start:u32,end:u32} byte offsets; SourceId(u32); SourceFile
  • │
    • bndl-ast/Phase 2 (§14) — ESTree-shaped AST + visitors. deps: std
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports; pub Program, ModuleItem, NodeId, SourceType
        • node.rsNodeId(u32); Program{items,source_type}
        • stmt.rsStmt enum (Expr,Var,Func,Class,Return,If,For,Block,...)
        • expr.rsExpr enum (Ident,Lit,Bin,Call,Member,Arrow,Object,Array,
  • Template,Import(dynamic),Assign,Optional(OptChain),...)
    • pat.rsPat: binding/array/object/rest/assignment patterns
    • visit.rsVisit (read-only walk) + VisitMut (in-place rewrite) traits
    • atom.rsAtom interned-string type + interner
  • │
    • bndl-parser/Phase 2-3 (§14,§15) — RD + Pratt. deps: bndl-lexer, bndl-ast
      • src/
        • lib.rspub parse_program(src,SourceId,Syntax)->(Program,Vec<ParseError>)
        • parser.rsParser<'a>{lexer,cur,lookahead,errors,syntax}; ASI; error-recovery
        • expr.rsPratt expression parser: binding powers, assoc, optional chaining
        • stmt.rsstatements/declarations: var/func/class, control flow, import/export
        • ts.rsTS annotations/interfaces/enums/generics/decorators — parse-DISCARD
        • jsx.rsJSX elements/fragments; gated by Syntax.jsx -> JsxNotEnabled
        • error.rsParseError + ParseErrorKind variants

crates/ — semantic crates: bndl-resolver through bndl-treeshake (Phases 4–7)

  • crates/
    • bndl-resolver/Phase 4 (§16) — Node CJS+ESM resolution. deps: std
      • src/
        • lib.rspub Resolver, ResolveOptions, Specifier, ResolveResult, Condition
        • resolver.rsResolver{options,fs cache,package.json cache,realpath cache}
        • package_json.rsPackageJson: main/module/browser/type/sideEffects; cached parse
        • exports.rsPACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE / imports: conditional + pattern matching
        • tsconfig.rsTsConfig: paths/baseUrl mapping — runs BEFORE node_modules
        • cjs.rsCJS algo: LOAD_AS_FILE / LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY / LOAD_NODE_MODULES
        • esm.rsESM algo: PACKAGE_RESOLVE, ESM_FILE_FORMAT
  • │
    • bndl-graph/Phase 5 (§17) — module graph. deps: bndl-parser, bndl-resolver
      • src/
        • lib.rspub ModuleId, Module, ModuleGraph, ImportRecord, ExportRecord
        • module.rsModule{id,path,kind,ast,imports,exports,symbols,has_side_effects}
        • graph.rsModuleGraph{modules,entries,edges,cycles}; Tarjan SCC; parallel load
        • scan.rsscan ESM import/export + CJS require -> ImportRecord/ExportRecord
        • bindings.rslive-binding model; re-export + export-star resolution
  • │
    • bndl-transform/Phase 6 (§18) — AST transforms. deps: bndl-ast
      • src/
        • lib.rspub TransformPass, TransformContext, Target, DefineMap, JsxRuntime
        • driver.rsVisitMut driver + pass ordering (Define->TypeStrip->Jsx->Lowering)
        • type_strip.rsTypeStrip: erase TS type syntax, keep value semantics
        • jsx.rsJsxTransform: Classic createElement | Automatic jsx-runtime
        • lower.rsLowering to Target: optional chaining, class fields, async scope
        • define.rsDefine: DefineMap name->Expr substitution
  • │
    • bndl-treeshake/Phase 7 (§19) — tree-shaking. deps: bndl-graph
      • src/
        • lib.rspub SymbolGraph, TreeShaker, ReachableSet; shake()->ReachableSet
        • symbol_graph.rsSymbolGraph: per-symbol dependency edges + side-effect markers
        • reachability.rsworklist reachability from entry exports; dead elimination
        • side_effects.rsSideEffect{Pure,Impure,Unknown}; sideEffects:false + __PURE__

crates/ — back-end crates: bndl-chunk through bndl-cli (Phases 8–13)

  • crates/
    • bndl-chunk/Phase 9 (§21) — code splitting. deps: bndl-graph, bndl-treeshake
      • src/
        • lib.rspub Chunk, ChunkId, ChunkGraph, OutputFormat; split()->ChunkGraph
        • split.rsmodule-group split by reachable-entry-set; dynamic import() boundary
        • chunk_graph.rsChunkGraph{chunks}; Chunk{id,modules,entry,is_dynamic,hash}
        • hash.rsBLAKE3 content hashing; fixed-point cross-chunk hash refs
  • │
    • bndl-codegen/Phases 8,10,11 (§20,§22,§23) — emit+runtime+scope+minify
      • src/deps: bndl-ast, bndl-chunk
        • lib.rspub Emitter, Renamer, Minifier, ScopeTree, OutputChunk
        • emit.rsEmitter/Printer AST->JS; source-map builder (#265 codec)
        • runtime.rs__require/__esm/__commonJS/__toESM/__export interop helpers
        • scope.rsScopeTree: scopes, bindings, references, scope ids
        • rename.rsRenamer: scope-aware collision-free rename; stable order
        • hoist.rsscope hoisting / module concatenation + lazy-init fallback
        • minify.rsMinifier: mangle + DCE + constant fold + whitespace
        • formats.rsOutputFormat: Esm | Cjs | Iife wrappers + interop rewrite
  • │
    • bndl-cache/Phase 13 (§25) — content-addressed cache. deps: std
      • src/
        • lib.rspub CacheKey, Cache, Artifact{Parsed,Transformed,Emitted}
        • key.rsCacheKey([u8;32]) = BLAKE3(content + options)
        • store.rsin-memory + on-disk store; change-driven invalidation
  • │
    • bndl-server/Phase 12 (§24) — dev server + HMR
      • src/deps: bndl-graph, bndl-transform, bndl-codegen, bndl-cache
        • lib.rspub DevServer, HmrEngine, HmrBoundary, Watcher, ModuleRunner
        • server.rsDevServer: http+ws; ESM-native on-demand transform; pre-bundling
        • hmr.rsHmrEngine: boundary propagation; ws HMR protocol; full-reload fallback
        • watch.rsWatcher: fs events -> cache invalidation -> graph update
        • client_runtime.jsbrowser HMR client (import.meta.hot accept/dispose) — shipped asset
  • │
    • bndl-cli/Phase 0+ (§12..) — the bndl binary. deps: ALL above
      • src/
        • main.rsentry; run(args:CliArgs)->ExitCode; subcommands build/serve/transform
        • build.rsbndl build: resolve+load->graph->shake->chunk->emit->write
        • serve.rsbndl serve: wire DevServer + Watcher + HmrEngine
        • transform.rsbndl transform: single-file lex->parse->transform->print
        • analyze.rsbndl analyze + bndl graph: module/chunk report, sizes, shake stats
        • plugin.rsPlugin trait host: resolve/load/transform/render_chunk hooks
        • config.rsconfig loader: entries, OutputFormat, Target, DefineMap, conditions

10.3 benches/ — criterion harnesses (-> §4 targets)

  • benches/
    • lex.rstokens/sec on large bundles (react/typescript libs)
    • parse.rsMB/s parse throughput; deep-nesting + huge-file cases
    • resolve.rsresolutions/sec, cold vs warm fs cache
    • graph.rsmodule-graph build on 1k+/10k+ module trees (parallel scaling)
    • treeshake.rsreachability time vs symbol count
    • bundle_e2e.rsfull build wall-clock vs esbuild/Rollup on fixed corpora
    • incremental.rswarm-cache rebuild latency (single-file edit -> emit)
    • minify.rsminify throughput + size-reduction ratio

10.4 conformance/ — Test262 + fixtures + differential oracle

  • conformance/
    • test262/imported submodule (parser conformance; scripts/import-test262.sh); gitignored content
    • runner/Test262 harness: feature-flag filter, negative-parse expectations, pass/skip ledger
    • fixtures/
      • bundle/input project trees + expected output chunks (golden) for build determinism
      • resolve/specifier -> expected path cases (exports/imports/tsconfig/symlink/browser)
      • treeshake/sideEffects + PURE annotation in/out pairs
      • interop/CJS<->ESM interop expectations (__toESM/__commonJS)
      • hmr/edit-sequence -> expected boundary/propagation transcripts
      • sourcemap/post-transform map fixtures validated via #265 codec
    • oracle/differential runner: bndl vs esbuild/Rollup output-semantics diff (dev-dep/feature gated)

10.5 fuzz/ — cargo-fuzz

  • fuzz/
    • Cargo.tomllibfuzzer-sys; separate non-workspace-published crate
    • fuzz_targets/
      • lex.rsarbitrary bytes -> Lexer never panics, always terminates
      • parse.rsarbitrary source -> parse_program never panics; bounded recovery
      • roundtrip.rsparse -> emit -> re-parse == structural-equal AST (printer/parser agreement)
      • resolve.rsfuzz package.json/exports/tsconfig -> Resolver never panics / no path escape

10.6 examples/

  • examples/
    • hello-esm/a.js imports b.js -> single ESM file (Phase 0 smoke)
    • react-jsx/automatic jsx-runtime + tree-shaking + minify
    • ts-library/TS type-strip + ESM+CJS dual output + d.ts pass-through
    • code-split/static + dynamic import() -> entry/shared/async chunks + manifest
    • cjs-interop/mixed CJS/ESM graph exercising interop helpers
    • dev-server/vite-style serve + HMR demo (docker-compose target)

10.7 docs/

  • docs/
    • adr/numbered Architecture Decision Records (ADR-0001..): arena alloc, Pratt powers, module-group splitting, hash fixed-point, #265 map boundary
    • runbooks/operate/debug: triage-nondeterministic-output, oracle-diff-investigation, cache-corruption-recovery, hmr-stuck-reload
    • threat-model.mduntrusted-source attack surface (lexer/parser/resolver), DoS budgets, path-traversal in resolve, plugin trust
    • performance/§4 target tables, criterion baselines, flamegraph notes, parallel-scaling analysis
    • failure-analysis/post-mortem-style writeups: over-shaking correctness bugs, interop edge cases, ASI/regex misclassification

11. Core Schema and Core Types

Normative API surface the phases (## 12–## 25) build against. Types are grouped by owning crate. Signatures + doc comments only; bodies are illustrative (≤2 lines) per CRITICAL RULE 10. Renaming, abbreviating, or re-homing any of these is a spec violation.

11.1 Cross-cutting invariants

Invariant Rule Enforced where
AST mutability window Program/Expr/Stmt mutated only during bndl-transform (VisitMut). Before & after transform the AST is read-only. Phases 6, 8, 10
Module immutability Module is frozen after bndl-graph build completes; downstream (shake/chunk/codegen) take &Module. Phases 5–11
Send + Sync modules Module, ModuleGraph are Send + Sync after build → shared & across the worker pool with zero locks. Phases 5, 7, 9
Per-thread transform TransformContext is constructed per worker, never shared. &DefineMap inside it is shared & (read-only). Phase 6
Interned strings All identifiers/specifiers/export names are Atom (interned, Copy-cheap, == is pointer/id compare). Never String on the hot path. All
Span discipline Every AST node carries a Span (byte offsets into one SourceFile); preserved through transform for source maps (#265). Phases 1–3, 10
Determinism All collections that affect output use insertion-stable or sorted iteration order. No HashMap iteration order leaks into output. Phases 9–11, 13
Numeric id newtypes SourceId, NodeId, ModuleId, SymbolId, ChunkId are u32 newtypes (dense, Copy, array-indexable). u32::MAX reserved as NONE sentinel. All

Atom: 32-bit interned-string handle (per-process interner, Copy, Eq/Hash by id). Lives in bndl-ast; re-exported everywhere. Interner is thread-safe (sharded) so parser workers intern concurrently.

11.2 bndl-lexer — tokens & spans

/// Byte offsets into a single SourceFile. Half-open [start, end).
/// 32-bit caps a source file at 4 GiB — enforced at read time.
pub struct Span { pub start: u32, pub end: u32 }

/// Dense index into the source-file table. Copy. u32::MAX = synthetic/no-source.
pub struct SourceId(pub u32);

pub enum TokenKind {
    Ident, PrivateName, Keyword(Keyword), Num(f64), BigInt, Str,
    NoSubTemplate, TemplateHead, TemplateMiddle, TemplateTail,
    Regex, Punct(Punct),
    JsxText, JsxIdent,
    Eof,
    Invalid,                 // recovery token; never panics
}

pub struct Token {
    pub kind: TokenKind,
    pub span: Span,
    pub had_newline_before: bool,   // drives ASI in the parser
}

/// Borrows source for its lifetime; single-threaded; one Lexer per SourceFile.
pub struct Lexer<'a> { /* src: &'a str, pos: u32, regex_ctx: bool, tmpl_stack: Vec<()> */ }
  • Ownership: Token is Copy-ish/Clone; owns no heap. Num carries the parsed f64 (IEEE-754, incl. NaN/Infinity from 1e999); BigInt/Str carry only the Span — value materialized lazily by parser via Atom to avoid per-token allocation.
  • Mutation: Lexer is the only mutable holder; advances pos, flips regex_ctx on parser feedback (regex-vs-divide), pushes/pops tmpl_stack for nested ${}.
  • Concurrency: not Sync (interior cursor). One per worker; lexers on distinct files run in parallel.
  • Compatibility: TokenKind is #[non_exhaustive] for additive growth; match arms must include _.

11.3 bndl-ast — ESTree AST + visitors

/// Dense node id, assigned at parse time, stable for a Program's lifetime.
pub struct NodeId(pub u32);

pub enum SourceType { Module, Script }

pub struct Program { pub items: Vec<ModuleItem>, pub source_type: SourceType }

pub enum ModuleItem { Stmt(Stmt), Import(ImportDecl), Export(ExportDecl) }

pub enum Stmt {
    Expr(Box<Expr>), Var(VarDecl), Func(FuncDecl), Class(ClassDecl),
    Return(/* .. */), If(/* .. */), For(/* .. */), Block(Vec<Stmt>),
    // While, DoWhile, Switch, Try, Throw, Break, Continue, Labeled, Empty, Debugger ...
}

pub enum Expr {
    Ident(Ident), Lit(Lit), Bin(BinExpr), Call(CallExpr), Member(MemberExpr),
    Arrow(ArrowExpr), Object(/* .. */), Array(/* .. */), Template(/* .. */),
    Import(ImportExpr),          // dynamic import()
    Assign(/* .. */), Optional(OptChain),
    // Unary, Update, Logical, Cond, New, Seq, Tagged, Class, Func, Await, Yield, Paren ...
}

pub enum Pat { Ident(Ident), Array(/* .. */), Object(/* .. */), Rest(/* .. */),
               Assign(/* default */), Expr(Box<Expr>) /* member target */ }

pub struct Ident { pub sym: Atom, pub span: Span, pub node_id: NodeId }

pub struct ImportDecl { pub specifiers: Vec<ImportSpec>, pub source: Atom, pub span: Span }
// ImportSpec ::= Default(Ident) | Namespace(Ident) | Named { imported: Atom, local: Ident }

pub enum ExportDecl {
    Named { specifiers: Vec<ExportSpec>, source: Option<Atom> },  // export {a as b} [from "x"]
    Default(Box<Expr>),                                           // export default <expr>
    All { source: Atom, alias: Option<Atom> },                   // export * [as ns] from "x"
    Decl(Box<Stmt>),                                             // export const/function/class
}

pub trait Visit   { /* read-only walk; default impls recurse */ }
pub trait VisitMut{ /* in-place rewrite; transform passes implement this */ }
  • Ownership: arena/Box mix — recursive nodes boxed to bound enum size; Program owns its Vec<ModuleItem> tree. AST allocated in a bump arena tied to one module → freed wholesale; no per-node drop cost.
  • Mutation semantics: mutated only via VisitMut during transform (§11.1). node_id is allocation-stable; transforms creating nodes mint fresh ids from the module's counter.
  • Concurrency: a Program is owned by one worker until frozen into Module; not shared mutably.
  • Visit vs VisitMut: read passes (graph scan, scope analysis, tree-shake marking, emit) use Visit; rewrite passes use VisitMut. Both are #[non_exhaustive]-friendly via default-recursing methods.

11.4 bndl-parser — parser, syntax flags, errors

pub struct Syntax { pub typescript: bool, pub jsx: bool }   // gates TS/JSX grammar

/// Borrows source; holds the Lexer, 1-token lookahead, error sink, syntax flags.
pub struct Parser<'a> { /* lexer: Lexer<'a>, cur: Token, ahead: Option<Token>,
                           errors: Vec<ParseError>, syntax: Syntax */ }

/// Total: never panics. Returns a (possibly partial) Program + accumulated errors.
pub fn parse_program(src: &str, id: SourceId, syntax: Syntax)
    -> (Program, Vec<ParseError>);

pub struct ParseError { pub kind: ParseErrorKind, pub span: Span }

pub enum ParseErrorKind {
    UnexpectedToken, UnterminatedString, UnterminatedTemplate, InvalidRegex,
    BadImport, BadExport, ReservedWord, TrailingTsType, JsxNotEnabled,
}
  • Contract: parsing is total — malformed input yields error nodes + Vec<ParseError>, never panic/unwind. Hot path consumes untrusted source.
  • Ownership: Parser owns the Lexer; produced Program outlives the parser (arena-backed). Errors are owned, Copy-light (kind + Span).
  • Concurrency: per-thread; N files parse on N workers; Syntax is Copy, broadcast freely.
  • Compatibility: parse_program signature is frozen API. ParseErrorKind #[non_exhaustive]. Pratt binding powers (§ key algorithms) live internal; had_newline_before drives ASI.

11.5 bndl-resolver — specifier resolution

pub enum Condition { Import, Require, Browser, Node, Default,
                     Development, Production, Custom(Atom) }

pub struct ResolveOptions {
    pub conditions: Vec<Condition>,     // ordered; first match wins in exports map
    pub extensions: Vec<String>,        // probe order, e.g. [".ts",".tsx",".js",".jsx",".json"]
    pub main_fields: Vec<String>,       // e.g. ["browser","module","main"]
    pub ts_config: Option<TsConfig>,    // paths/baseUrl — applied before node_modules
    pub prefer_relative: bool,
}

pub enum Specifier {
    Relative(String),                   // "./x", "../y"
    Absolute(String),                   // "/abs", "C:\\abs"
    Bare { pkg: String, sub: String },  // "react" / "lodash/fp" -> pkg + subpath
}

pub struct ResolveResult {
    pub path: PathBuf,                  // realpath'd (symlinks resolved)
    pub kind: ModuleKind,               // from extension + package "type"
    pub side_effects: SideEffects,      // package.json sideEffects -> tree-shake hint
    pub from_node_modules: bool,        // pre-bundling / external heuristics
}

pub enum ResolveError { NotFound, InvalidExports, PackageNotFound, NotExported, BadTsPaths }

/// Caches fs stat, parsed package.json, and realpath. Shared read-mostly.
pub struct Resolver { /* opts, fs_cache, pkg_cache, realpath_cache */ }
  • Algorithm binding: implements Node ESM (PACKAGE_RESOLVE, PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE with conditional + pattern * matching, ESM_FILE_FORMAT) and CJS (LOAD_AS_FILE/LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY/LOAD_NODE_MODULES); tsconfig paths precede node_modules.
  • Ownership/concurrency: one Resolver shared Arc<Resolver> across workers; internal caches are interior-mutable + thread-safe (sharded maps) — resolution is &self. Cache populated once, read concurrently.
  • Persistence: caches are in-memory per build; not serialized (cheap to rebuild from fs). ResolveResult is the value bndl-graph consumes to mint a ModuleId.
  • Compatibility: Condition/ResolveError #[non_exhaustive]. Custom(Atom) carries user conditions from config.

11.6 bndl-graph — module graph

pub struct ModuleId(pub u32);

pub enum ModuleKind { Esm, Cjs, Json, Css, Asset, Builtin, External }

pub struct SymbolId(pub u32);

pub struct Symbol { pub name: Atom, pub kind: SymbolKind, pub local: bool }
// SymbolKind ::= Var | Func | Class | Import | Default | Namespace | ...

pub struct ImportRecord {
    pub specifier: Atom,
    pub resolved: Option<ModuleId>,     // None until resolution wires the edge
    pub kind: ImportKind,
}
pub enum ImportKind { Static, Dynamic, Require, ExportStar }

pub struct ExportRecord {
    pub name: Atom,                              // exported name ("default" for default)
    pub local: Option<SymbolId>,                 // Some -> local binding
    pub reexport: Option<(ModuleId, Atom)>,      // Some -> re-export from another module
}

pub struct Module {
    pub id: ModuleId,
    pub path: PathBuf,
    pub kind: ModuleKind,
    pub ast: Program,                  // frozen after build
    pub imports: Vec<ImportRecord>,
    pub exports: Vec<ExportRecord>,
    pub symbols: Vec<Symbol>,          // indexed by SymbolId
    pub has_side_effects: bool,        // top-level effects pin the module
}

pub struct ModuleGraph {
    pub modules: Vec<Module>,          // indexed by ModuleId
    pub entries: Vec<ModuleId>,
    /* edges (derived from ImportRecord.resolved), cycle set (SCCs) */
}
  • Ownership: ModuleGraph owns all Modules (arena of modules, Vec indexed by ModuleId). After the build phase the graph is frozen: &Module/&ModuleGraph everywhere downstream.
  • Concurrency: build phase loads modules in parallel (resolve+parse per worker), then publishes into the graph; once frozen the graph is Send + Sync, shared & by shake/chunk/codegen with no locks (§11.1).
  • Live bindings / interop: ExportRecord.reexport + ImportKind::ExportStar model re-export & export *; ESM import = live read-only view of exporter's binding (identity + mutation-visibility preserved through shake/rename/concat). CJS exports = value snapshot at require-time; bridged by interop helpers (§11.11). has_side_effects is the soundness pin for tree-shaking.
  • Cycles: SCC detection records cyclic groups → forces wrapper/lazy-init in codegen (§11.10).
  • Compatibility: ModuleKind, ImportKind, SymbolKind #[non_exhaustive].

11.7 bndl-transform — transform passes

pub enum Target { Es5, Es2015, Es2017, Es2020, Es2022, EsNext }

/// name -> replacement Expr (e.g. process.env.NODE_ENV -> "production").
pub struct DefineMap(/* Map<Atom, Expr> */);

pub trait TransformPass {
    fn name(&self) -> &str;
    fn visit_mut(&mut self, p: &mut Program, ctx: &mut TransformContext);
}

pub enum JsxRuntime { Classic, Automatic }    // createElement vs jsx-runtime

/// Constructed per worker per module. Holds shared (&) read-only inputs +
/// per-pass mutable scratch. NEVER shared across threads.
pub struct TransformContext<'a> {
    pub target: Target,
    pub defines: &'a DefineMap,        // shared read-only
    pub syntax: Syntax,
    pub jsx_runtime: JsxRuntime,
}
  • Pass set & order (Phase 6): TypeStrip → JsxTransform → Lowering → Define. Order is normative (strip TS types before JSX/lowering; define before lowering so folded constants lower correctly). The VisitMut driver runs passes in this order over one Program.
  • Ownership/mutation: this is the only stage that mutates the AST. Each pass takes &mut Program; &mut self lets a pass carry scratch state. New nodes get fresh NodeIds.
  • Concurrency: TransformContext per-thread (§11.1); &DefineMap is the only shared datum and is read-only (Send + Sync). Modules transform fully in parallel.
  • Compatibility: Target, JsxRuntime #[non_exhaustive]; TransformPass is the plugin-extensible seam (CLI Plugin::transform produces a Program consumed here).

11.8 bndl-treeshake — reachability & side effects

/// Per-symbol dependency edges + side-effect markers, derived from the graph.
pub struct SymbolGraph { /* edges: SymbolId -> [SymbolId], effects: [SideEffect] */ }

pub enum SideEffect { Pure, Impure, Unknown }   // Unknown is treated conservatively

/// Worklist reachability from entry exports.
pub struct TreeShaker { /* worklist: Vec<SymbolId>, reached: BitSet */ }

/// Marks reachable symbols; mutates graph metadata (not AST shape) for pruning.
pub fn shake(graph: &mut ModuleGraph, entries: &[ModuleId]) -> ReachableSet;
// ReachableSet: bitset of reachable (ModuleId, SymbolId); statements/exports/imports
// not in the set are eliminated by codegen.
  • Soundness (binding): never remove observably-effecting code unless proven Pure. sideEffects:false (package.json) and /*#__PURE__*/ are explicit pure-contracts; Unknown ⇒ keep.
