wrap/cycle_lazy.js (A↔B) both EsmWrapped, init order correct, no TDZ throw
wrap/eval_present.js EsmWrapped (eval poisons scope)
malformed/shadow_eval.js rename suppressed in eval scope; conservative keep-names
malformed/dup_const.js parser-level error surfaced, no hoist attempt

8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause → debug
Renaming breaks live binding snapshotted value instead of ref → assert imported ref maps to exporter's SymbolId, not a copy; dump BindingMap
Shadowing collision after rename inner scope binds chosen global name → rename inner too; check captured + scope-chain walk
eval/with defeats renaming dynamic name lookup → set has_eval ancestor-wide; suppress rename in poisoned subtree
Hoisting a cycle reorders init inlined cycle exposes TDZ → force EsmWrapped for cycle members in lazy-init set
Namespace object loses liveness object built with values → emit getter per export or direct ref; verify mutation fixture
CJS hoisted by mistake module.exports reassigned dynamically → never hoist CJS; classify in eligibility.rs

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Scope build: single two-pass walk, O(nodes); target ≤0.4 ms / 1k LOC module. FxHashMap for bindings (no crypto hashing); intern names via Atom — compare by id.
  • Rename slot allocation amortized O(1) per binding; reserved-word set is a static phf.
  • Concatenation splices Vec<ModuleItem> (no per-node clone where ownership transfers); avoid re-walking inlined bodies.
  • Parallel ScopeBuilder per module (immutable AST, Send+Sync); merge maps in topo order single-threaded.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • export { x as default } + export default clash → keep distinct slots.
  • TDZ: const/let/class referenced before decl across hoisted boundary → must preserve order or wrap.
  • Re-export of a wrapped module's binding from a hoisted module → bridge via __toESM.
  • import of unused binding (tree-shaken) → no rename slot, drop ref.
  • Self-referential function/class names shadowing a global → keep local name, rename global.
  • arguments/this/labels are not bindings — exclude from Renamer.
  • Top-level await in hoisted ESM → preserve ordering; cannot inline behind sync wrapper.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Unresolved ref after concat post-pass: every ref ∈ BindingMap or local scope hard error w/ span; abort hoist for that module → wrap
Init-order cycle deadlock wrapped-fn re-entrancy via memo guard partial-export semantics (CJS-like), match Node
Collision allocator non-determinism sort key = (topo_idx, span) asserted total order panic in debug if equal keys
Eval scope mis-flagged conformance shadow_eval fixture fail-closed: treat as poisoned

8.10 Operational Notes

  • bndl analyze --hoist reports hoist rate + per-module WrapKind + reasons (why-not-hoisted).
  • --no-scope-hoist flag forces all-wrapped (debugging / source-readability) — keeps semantics, drops inlining.
  • RUST_LOG=bndl_codegen::hoist=debug traces classify decisions + rename assignments.
  • BindingMap snapshot dumped to .bndl/hoist-map.json under cache key for diff across rebuilds.
  • Renamed-name stability is a CDN-cache contract: changing the slot allocator invalidates all chunk hashes — version it.

21. Phase 9 — Code splitting + chunking

Crate: bndl-chunk. Consumes ModuleGraph + ReachableSet; produces ChunkGraph + manifest. Algorithm = esbuild/Rollup module groups: a module's chunk is keyed by the set of entries (static + dynamic) that reach it. Entry chunks, dynamic-import() async chunks, shared chunks for multi-entry modules. Deterministic naming + BLAKE3 content hashing with a fixed-point pass converging cross-chunk hash references. Codegen (Phase 10) consumes the chunk layout; this phase decides which module → which chunk and which chunk imports which, not byte emission.

9.1 Objectives

  • Compute per-module reachable-entry-set (bitset over entries) → chunk-key; modules with identical key co-locate.
  • Entry chunk per ModuleGraph.entries; dynamic import() (ImportKind::Dynamic) → new async-entry chunk boundary.
  • Shared chunk extraction: modules reachable from ≥2 entries split out, deduped (no module duplicated across chunks).
  • Wire Chunk.imports (cross-chunk edges) from inter-chunk module references; topologically orderable, acyclic for ESM.
  • Deterministic Chunk.hash (BLAKE3 of canonical content) + stable file naming; fixed-point pass so hashes referencing hashes converge. Emit manifest.json. Invariant: identical input+opts → byte-identical names/manifest.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

File Adds
bndl-chunk/src/lib.rs Chunk, ChunkId, ChunkGraph, OutputFormat, pub fn split(...) re-exports
bndl-chunk/src/group.rs EntrySet(BitSet), assign_groups() — reachable-entry-set per module
bndl-chunk/src/algo.rs ChunkingAlgo (group→Chunk), shared-chunk extraction, entry/dynamic chunk creation
bndl-chunk/src/wire.rs cross-chunk import wiring, imported_symbols per edge
bndl-chunk/src/hash.rs content_hash(), fixed-point hash convergence, file-name templating
bndl-chunk/src/manifest.rs Manifest, ManifestEntry, serialize
  • assign_groups (P2 esbuild module-group algorithm; ADR-13 reachable-entry-set; R2 Rollup chunkAssignment.ts): per entry e (incl. dynamic-import targets, each a synthetic entry), BFS over static + treat dynamic edge as separate entry root; OR entry-bit into each reached module's EntrySet. Key = EntrySet. Restrict walk to ReachableSet (tree-shaken survivors).
  • ChunkingAlgo: bucket modules by EntrySet → one Chunk per distinct set. Chunk.entry = Some(m) iff set is a singleton AND m ∈ entries. is_dynamic iff chunk root is a dynamic-import target. Module order within chunk = stable post-order DFS of graph (deterministic; ADR-14 determinism).
  • wire: for each module-ref crossing a chunk boundary → add from.imports.push(to_chunk); record imported Atom symbol names for codegen's cross-chunk import {...} (P9 Webpack ChunkGraph cross-chunk manifest).
  • Signatures:
pub fn split(graph: &ModuleGraph, reachable: &ReachableSet, fmt: OutputFormat) -> ChunkGraph;
struct EntrySet(roaring::RoaringBitmap);          // entry-index membership
fn assign_groups(g: &ModuleGraph, r: &ReachableSet) -> HashMap<ModuleId, EntrySet>;
fn content_hash(c: &Chunk, dep_hashes: &[u64]) -> u64;   // BLAKE3 -> truncated u64
  • hash (ADR-14 BLAKE3 content hash; P13 deterministic hashing with fixed-point; R2 Rollup placeholder hash): hash placeholder = chunk's module-id list + opts; then fixed-point: recompute including resolved dep file-names, iterate until no hash changes (bounded ≤ N iters, N=#chunks). File name = template [name]-[hash:8].js (entry uses entry basename; shared uses chunk).
  • manifest: map entry source path → output file, list dynamic-import chunk map (ModuleId → file) for runtime loader, plus per-chunk imports/exports for HTML injection / preload (P9 Webpack under-the-hood chunk manifest).

9.3 Deliverable

bndl-chunk crate: split() returns a ChunkGraph + Manifest for a multi-entry, dynamic-import fixture; chunks deterministic, content-hashed, cross-referenced, no module duplicated. Wired into orchestrator between bndl-treeshake and bndl-codegen. bndl analyze/bndl graph dump chunk layout.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Module shared by 2 entries appears in exactly one shared chunk (0 duplication); verified by module-id ∩ across chunks = ∅.
  • import('./x') produces a distinct is_dynamic chunk; static-only graph → exactly 1 chunk per entry, 0 shared.
  • Re-run on identical input → byte-identical chunk file names + manifest.json (hash diff = 0); flip one module's source → only chunks containing it (and dependents whose hash refs changed) get new hashes.
  • Fixed-point hash pass converges ≤ #chunks iterations; cross-chunk hash refs in manifest match emitted names.
  • cargo test -p bndl-chunk green; chunk-graph DAG acyclic (ESM); cross-chunk imports resolve to existing ChunkId.

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
two-entries-shared.js a.js,b.js both import shared.js 3 chunks: a, b, shared; shared has both as importers
dynamic-split.js entry import('./lazy') 2 chunks: entry + dynamic lazy; manifest dynamic-map present
diamond.js e→{x,y}, x&y→z z in entry chunk (single reachable-entry), no spurious shared chunk
multi-dynamic-shared.js 2 dynamic imports share util util in shared chunk reachable by both dynamic roots
determinism.json any of above ×2 runs identical names + manifest bytes
cycle-entry.js (malformed) dynamic import forms ref cycle across chunks no infinite hash loop; bounded iters; warn, still emit
empty-after-shake.js (malformed) module fully tree-shaken excluded from all chunks; no empty chunk emitted

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Hash non-convergence / oscillation: chunk A's hash depends on B's name and vice-versa in a cycle → fixed-point never settles. Root cause: mutual hash refs. Fix: hash content sans cross-refs first, inject names, iterate with cap; on cap-hit fall back to id-based name. Debug: log per-iter hash delta set.
  • Module duplication: walking each entry independently and copying reached modules → same module in N chunks (bloat + double side-effects). Root cause: grouping by entry not by entry-set. Fix: key on EntrySet. Debug: assert disjoint module-id sets.
  • Dynamic import not splitting: import() treated as static edge → no async chunk; bundle ships eagerly. Root cause: missing ImportKind::Dynamic branch in assign_groups. Debug: dump per-module EntrySet, expect synthetic dynamic-entry bit.
  • Non-determinism from HashMap iteration: chunk/module order from HashMap → name churn run-to-run. Root cause: unordered iteration. Fix: sort by ModuleId/post-order DFS before hashing. Debug: diff two-run manifests.
  • Side-effect-only module dropped from chunking: tree-shake kept it but it has no live symbol export → grouping skips it. Root cause: grouping iterates exports not modules. Fix: iterate ReachableSet modules. Debug: check has_side_effects modules present in some chunk.
  • Cross-chunk circular ESM import: shared chunk imports entry chunk back → ESM cycle, TDZ at runtime. Root cause: shared module references entry-only symbol. Debug: chunk-graph cycle detector; pull offending module into shared.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • EntrySet as RoaringBitmap (or fixed u64 bitset when entries ≤64) → O(1) union, compact; grouping O(modules × entries) bounded by reachable set.
  • BLAKE3 hashing parallel across chunks (rayon); fixed-point iterations rare (>1 only with cross-chunk hash refs), cap = #chunks; target chunking < 50ms for 5k-module graph.
  • Avoid re-hashing unchanged chunks across incremental builds — key by CacheKey (Phase 13). Single allocation of module-order vec per chunk; reuse DFS stack.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Single entry, no dynamic import → exactly 1 chunk, no shared, no hash iteration cycle.
  • Entry that is also a dynamic-import target of another entry → both entry bit and dynamic bit; remains its own entry chunk.
  • Module reachable only via dynamic import from a single entry → goes in that dynamic chunk, not entry chunk.
  • All modules shared (every entry imports everything) → 1 shared chunk + thin entry chunks (re-export only).
  • CJS module pulled into ESM chunk → marked for wrapper (Phase 10 interop); chunk wiring unaffected.
  • Zero dynamic imports + OutputFormat::Iife → splitting disabled (IIFE = single chunk); assert entries ≤ 1 or error.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Hash fixed-point exceeds cap iter counter > #chunks fall back to id-stable names; emit warning, deterministic still
Cross-chunk import to nonexistent ChunkId post-wire validation pass hard error (internal bug); abort build
Chunk-graph cycle (ESM) Tarjan SCC on chunk edges warn; merge SCC into one chunk to preserve init order
Module assigned to 0 chunks reachable-but-ungrouped check hard error; dump module path + EntrySet
OutputFormat::Iife with >1 entry precondition check in split Resolve/Config error before chunking

9.10 Operational Notes

  • bndl analyze --chunks prints chunk table: id, entry?, dynamic?, module count, hash, importers; bndl graph --format=dot includes chunk clustering.
  • manifest.json is the integration contract for HTML injection + the dynamic-import runtime loader (Phase 10/12); schema versioned (manifestVersion).
  • Determinism is a CI gate: build twice, diff manifests + file names → must be identical (long-term CDN caching depends on it).
  • Tunables: chunkFileNames/entryFileNames templates, min-shared-chunk-size threshold (skip tiny shared chunks → inline back), hash length (default 8 hex).

22. Phase 10 — Code generation, module runtime, and output formats

Implements bndl-codegen output layer: AST Emitter/Printer, the injected module runtime (interop helpers), OutputFormat::{Esm,Cjs,Iife} wrappers, import/export rewriting + CJS↔ESM interop at chunk boundaries, and composed source-map emission via the #265 codec. Consumes the ChunkGraph (§21) + hoisted/renamed AST (§20). NON-GOAL: minification (§23, Phase 11).

10.1 Objectives

  • AST→JS Printer: minimal-by-default whitespace (single-space separators, newline-per-stmt, no indent unless pretty), byte-deterministic for identical input+options.
  • Inject module runtime helpers __require __esm __commonJS __toESM __export __reExport; emit only the subset a chunk references → zero dead runtime.
  • Three OutputFormat wrappers: Esm (native import/export), Cjs (require/module.exports), Iife ((function(){…})() + optional global name).
  • Rewrite import/export bindings → hoisted local refs (concatenated) or runtime calls (wrapped); bridge CJS↔ESM via __toESM/__commonJS/__reExport.
  • Compose per-stage mappings (lex→parse→transform→hoist→print) → final SourceMap via #265 codec; never hand-roll VLQ.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

File (crates/bndl-codegen/src/) Adds
printer.rs Emitter{ out:String, opts, mapper:#265 builder, line,col }; emit_program/stmt/expr; precedence table → parenthesization; ASI-safe token gluing
runtime.rs RuntimeHelper enum + canonical bodies (string consts); RuntimeSet:HashSet<RuntimeHelper>; topological emit (helpers may call helpers)
wrap.rs wrap_chunk(chunk, fmt, body) -> String; format-specific preamble/epilogue; export {…} / module.exports= / IIFE return-binding
interop.rs boundary rewrite: ESM-imports-CJS → __toESM; CJS-requires-ESM → namespace object; export * → __reExport; __esm/__commonJS module wrappers
emit.rs emit_chunk(chunk,graph,fmt,opts)->OutputChunk orchestrator; deterministic module order = chunk topo order, ties by ModuleId
  • Printer (ADR-08 VisitMut; R1 esbuild js_printer; ADR-18 #265 codec for maps): precedence-driven; emit parens only when child binding-power < parent or assoc forces it. ASI hazards (return\n, ++/--, leading (/[/`) → insert ; or space. Number printing: shortest round-trip (f64→minimal decimal, reuse Ryū-style; not full minify yet).
  • Runtime helpers (ADR-15 injected minimal helpers; P12 CJS↔ESM interop; ADR-10 live-binding model): __export(target,all) defines lazy getters preserving live bindings; __toESM(cjs,isNodeMode) wraps a CJS value-snapshot into an ESM namespace w/ default; __commonJS(fn) memoizes first-call module.exports; __esm(fn) memoizes lazy ESM init (cycle/wrapper path); __reExport(target,src) copies enumerable keys for export *; __require is the in-bundle CJS loader.

Runtime helper contracts (signatures only — no function bodies):

Helper Signature (no body) Contract
__export (target: object, all: Record<string, () => unknown>) => void; Defines a lazy getter on target for each key in all, preserving ESM live-binding semantics across CJS output
__commonJS (cb: (exports: object, module: object) => void) => () => unknown; Memoizes first-call module.exports evaluation; subsequent calls return cached exports — models CJS require-time snapshot
__toESM (mod: unknown, isNodeMode?: boolean) => object; Wraps a CJS value-snapshot into an ESM namespace object with a default property; handles the ADR-10 interop boundary
__esm (fn: () => void) => () => void; Memoizes lazy ESM initializer; used on the cycle/wrapper code path to defer execution until first import
__reExport (target: object, source: object) => void; Copies enumerable own keys from source to target; implements export * from "…" for CJS targets
  • Wrappers (ADR-16 OutputFormat; P9 Webpack runtime concept): Esm → emit hoisted body then export { local as exported, … }; re-exports inlined or __reExport. Cjs → body + Object.defineProperty(exports,…) getters for live bindings + 0 && (module.exports={…}) annotation for tooling. Iife → var Name = (function(exports){ …; return exports; })({}) when globalName set, else bare effect IIFE.
  • Interop matrix drives RuntimeSet population during emit; helper bodies appended to chunk preamble in topo order before first use (ADR-10 live-binding contract; ADR-15 tree-shakeable helpers).
  • Source map (ADR-18 #265 codec; S10 Source Map v3): Emitter feeds (genLine,genCol)→(origSource,origLine,origCol,name?) segments to #265 builder using the post-transform/hoist span carried on each Ident/node; #265 composes against upstream stage maps → final OutputChunk.map.

10.3 Deliverable

emit_chunk() produces runnable OutputChunk{file_name,code,map} for the worked-example project in all three OutputFormats; each code executes under Node (Esm/Cjs) and a browser shim (Iife) yielding identical program output; each .map validated by #265 and resolves generated positions back to original .ts/.jsx sources.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Determinism byte-identical code+map across 100 re-emits, 2 OSes, 2 thread counts
Runtime tree-shake unreferenced helpers absent; emitted set == referenced set (assert)
Interop correctness ESM↔CJS round-trip fixtures: runtime output == oracle (esbuild)
Live bindings mutate-after-export fixture: importer observes new value (getter, not snapshot)
Map fidelity #265 validator: 100% segments decode; ≥99% stmt-start positions map to correct orig line
Map composition every stage delta present; no identity-collapse losing transform offsets

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
esm_basic/ (a imports b) Esm/Cjs/Iife outputs; named export resolves; 3 maps valid
interop_esm_imports_cjs/ __toESM injected; default + named both work
interop_cjs_requires_esm/ namespace object; __esm memoization once
export_star/ (re-export all) __reExport; no own-binding shadow drop
live_binding/ (counter++) importer sees incremented value
dynamic_import_chunk/ cross-chunk import() rewritten to chunk URL; map per chunk
iife_global/ (globalName=App) var App=(function…)(); window assign
malformed: cyclic_cjs/ wrapped __commonJS, no infinite loop; partial-exports semantics match Node
malformed: bad_global_name/ (a-b) emit error InvalidGlobalName, not invalid JS

10.6 Pitfalls

  • ASI breakage on glue: concatenating return + newline-stripped expr, or a then (b)() → silent semantic change. Root cause: minimal-whitespace printer drops the newline ASI relied on. Debug: diff token stream vs AST re-parse; assert reparse(emit(ast)) ≡ ast.
  • Live-binding lost via snapshot: emitting exports.x = local (value copy) instead of a getter → mutation invisible. Root cause: treating ESM export like CJS assignment. Debug: live_binding fixture; grep emitted for get x().
  • Helper emission order: __toESM body printed after its first call site → ReferenceError. Root cause: non-topo helper ordering. Fix: topo-sort RuntimeSet, emit before module bodies.
  • Map offset drift: feeding pre-transform spans to #265 after hoist renamed/moved nodes → mappings point at wrong columns. Root cause: stage that mutates AST didn't update spans/deltas. Debug: per-stage map round-trip test.
  • export default <expr> with side effects rewritten as var default_n = expr reorders effects vs original eval order. Debug: effect-order fixture vs oracle.
  • Double-wrapping: a module both hoisted (§20) and wrapped (__esm) → duplicate init. Root cause: emit path disagrees with hoist decision. Assert: each ModuleId has exactly one emit strategy.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Printer single-pass, String with reserve (est. 1.3× source bytes) → no realloc thrash; ≥40 MB/s emit throughput.
  • Map-segment push is hot: pre-size segment vec to node count; #265 builder receives batched per-line deltas, not per-token.
  • Helper bodies are interned &'static str; zero per-chunk allocation for runtime.
  • Determinism cost: sorted module/export order is O(n log n) once per chunk — negligible vs print.
  • Parallel chunk emit across thread pool (chunks are Send); maps composed per-chunk independently.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty chunk / entry with only side-effect imports → emit effect body, no export {} noise (but valid module).
  • Reserved-word export alias (export { x as default }, as "string name" ESM2022 string exports) → quoted in export {…}.
  • TDZ-sensitive hoist: function decls hoist, let/const don't → preserve order across concatenation.
  • Iife with no globalName and exports → exports discarded (warn), pure-effect IIFE emitted.
  • BigInt / -0 / NaN / Infinity literals → correct round-trip print (no NaN as identifier).
  • CJS module with __esModule flag already set → don't double-define via __toESM.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Emitted JS unparseable self-check reparse(code) in debug builds hard error, dump offending chunk + AST
Map decode fails (#265) validator on emit in CI/debug fail build; never ship bad map silently
Nondeterministic output golden-hash compare in CI fail; bisect to unsorted collection
Helper missing at call site runtime ReferenceError in fixture exec topo-order assert pre-emit catches earlier
Interop value mismatch differential vs esbuild output flag boundary; fall back to wrapped __commonJS

10.10 Operational Notes

  • bndl build --format esm|cjs|iife, --global-name, --sourcemap=external|inline|none (delegates to #265 emitter mode).
  • Debug: BNDL_EMIT_VERIFY=1 enables reparse+map-validate per chunk (off in release for throughput).
  • bndl analyze reports per-chunk emitted runtime-helper set + bytes attributed to runtime vs user code.
  • Output writes are atomic (temp + rename); manifest (§21) records file_name→content hash for the three formats.
  • Source maps always via #265 codec; bundler version + --format folded into CacheKey (§25) so cache never serves wrong-format artifact.

23. Phase 11 — Minification (mangle, DCE, fold)

bndl-codegen Minifier: post-print-graph, pre-emit AST rewrite. Runs after Phase 10 printing setup but operates on the concatenated/wrapped AST. Composes maps via #265. Semantics-preserving, deterministic, scope-correct.

11.1 Objectives

  • Scope-aware identifier mangling -> shortest collision-free names from ScopeTree, frequency-ordered (hot symbols get 1-char names).
  • DCE: unreachable stmts, unused locals, dead branches post-define/fold, drop unreferenced functions/classes.
  • Constant folding + propagation; expression simplification (algebraic, boolean, string concat).
  • Optional property mangling (opt-in, reserved set, quoted-key exempt).
  • Whitespace/punct minimization (no semantic newlines; ASI hazards guarded).
  • Invariants preserved: live bindings, observable side effects, source-map fidelity (mangled -> original via #265 names), determinism.
  • Non-goal: type-driven opts (types stripped, #42); cross-module inlining beyond what scope-hoist already merged.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

File (bndl-codegen/src/) Adds
minify/mod.rs Minifier{opts: MinifyOptions, scope_tree: &ScopeTree, renamer: Renamer}; fn minify(&mut self, p:&mut Program, st:&ScopeTree) -> NameMap; drives passes to fixpoint (≤opts.max_passes, default 3)
minify/mangle.rs fn mangle(st:&ScopeTree, freq:&FreqTable, reserved:&ReservedSet) -> NameMap; base54 generator (a..z A..Z lead, +0..9 $ _ tail), skip keywords/reserved; per-scope assign honoring nested-scope live sets
minify/dce.rs VisitMut marking: liveness from ScopeTree refs + reachability worklist; eliminate_dead_branches, drop_unused_locals, drop_unreachable_after_terminator
minify/fold.rs fn fold_expr(e:&mut Expr) -> Folded; numeric/string/bool const ops, typeof lit, member-on-lit; respects -0, NaN, IEEE-754, BigInt distinctness
minify/simplify.rs boolean (!!x->x only in bool ctx), a?b:c collapse, void 0->undefined mangle, comma seqs, block flattening
minify/prop.rs opt-in mangle_props; collect property names not in reserved/quoted/computed; consistent rename across all members
minify/options.rs MinifyOptions{mangle, mangle_props:Option<PropOpts>, fold, dce, keep_names:HashSet<Atom>, drop_debugger, drop_console, max_passes}
  • Pass order per fixpoint iteration (B3 §8 constant fold/DCE; ADR-17 in-house minifier on post-hoisted AST): fold -> propagate -> simplify -> DCE -> (final) mangle. Mangle runs last, once after fixpoint (rename is not iterated).
  • Name allocation: FreqTable counts post-DCE references; sort symbols desc by count, ties by stable SymbolId; assign base54 sequence -> determinism + max compression (ADR-14 stable rename order; R4 SWC minifier mangle).
  • Map composition: Renamer records (mangled_span -> original Atom); Emitter feeds #265 builder names[]; no VLQ here (ADR-18 #265 codec; S10 Source Map v3 names[] field).

11.3 Deliverable

Minifier integrated into emit_chunk behind CodegenOptions.minify. bndl build --minify emits minified OutputChunk{code, map}. Semantics-equivalence suite + size benchmark vs terser/esbuild green; maps decode to original identifiers.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Size vs esbuild --minify (fixture corpus, gzipped) within +3%
Size vs terser default within +8%
Semantics suite (eval before==after) 100% pass
Map fidelity (mangled ident -> original via #265) 100% of mangled names resolvable
Determinism byte-identical output across 2 runs + 2 thread counts
Mangle throughput ≥4 M idents/s single thread
Zero panics on fuzz corpus 10M iters

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
mangle/nested_scope.js inner shadow renamed independently; no capture of outer free var
mangle/with_eval.js scope containing eval/with -> mangling disabled for that scope (names may be referenced dynamically)
fold/arith.js 1+2*3->7; -0 preserved; 0.1+0.2 not folded to 0.3
dce/dead_branch.js if(false){...} block dropped; define-driven branch removed
dce/pure_call.js /*#__PURE__*/f() unused -> removed; bare f() kept
simplify/bool_ctx.js !!x in if -> x; !!x as value kept
prop/mangle_props.js obj.foo renamed iff opt-in and foo not reserved/quoted
keep/live_binding.js exported let c mutated post-export still visible to importer after mangle
malformed asi/return_newline.js return\n x semantics (returns undefined) preserved; no return x join
malformed mangle/reserved_collision.js generator skips if,do,in,for -> never emits reserved word

11.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
Capture bug: inner ref binds to mangled outer name scope chain mis-walked; shadowing ignored dump ScopeTree + per-scope live set; assert renamed ident's resolved binding unchanged
eval/with breaks mangling dynamic name lookup sees mangled names mark tainted scopes; disable mangle there; flag in --analyze
Over-fold drops side effect folded away an impure subexpr (a.b getter, +x valueOf) gate fold on is_pure_lit; never fold member/unary on non-literal
ASI breakage after whitespace strip removed newline that ASI relied on (return,throw,break,continue,++/--, yield) printer ASI-guard table; insert ; or keep newline; roundtrip-parse check
Live binding frozen by inlining propagated a let that is reassigned post-export only propagate const/never-reassigned (consult ScopeTree write count)
Map drift after mangle name change without names[] update assert every renamed Span emits a names entry; #265 decode test
Prop-mangle corrupts dynamic access obj["foo"] / reflection / JSON keys renamed inconsistently reserved set + bail on computed/string-literal member to same prop name

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Mangle is O(symbols); base54 gen O(1) amortized. FreqTable via Vec<u32> indexed by SymbolId (no hashmap).
  • Fixpoint capped at max_passes; track changed flag, early-exit when stable -> avoid wasted sweeps.
  • Single AST arena pass per phase; reuse buffers; no per-node allocation during mangle.
  • Parallel: per-chunk minify on thread pool (chunks Send); intra-chunk single-threaded (shared ScopeTree).
  • Target: minify ≤15% of total emit time on large bundle (1M LoC).

11.8 Edge Cases

  • arguments/this/super never mangled; eval/with taint whole scope.
  • Labels mangled in own namespace (distinct from idents); break L consistency.
  • TDZ: don't hoist/reorder let/const across use that would create false TDZ.
  • -0, NaN, Infinity, BigInt n suffix, \uXXXX in idents — fold/mangle must not normalize away distinctions.
  • export { x as default } + mangle: external export name pinned, local mangled.
  • Getter/setter side effects: never fold/drop member access that could trigger them.
  • Directive "use strict" preserved; sloppy-mode-only constructs respected.
  • Comments: drop all except /*#__PURE__*/-consumed (already actioned) and license /*! */ (keep).

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Mitigation
Semantic divergence eval-equivalence suite diff bisect passes via --minify-passes; disable suspect pass
Non-deterministic output 2-run hash mismatch check freq tie-break uses SymbolId not iteration order
Reserved-word emission parse-back of output fails reserved skip table assert in mangle gen
Map unresolvable #265 decode returns no name fail emit; require 1:1 rename->names entry
Infinite fixpoint pass count exceeds max_passes hard cap + log non-converging node id

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Flags: --minify, --mangle-props, --keep-names, --no-fold, --no-dce, --minify-passes N, --drop-console, --drop-debugger.
  • keep_names preserves fn.name/class.name (for frameworks relying on reflection) — wraps mangle exemption.
  • --analyze reports: size delta, mangled-symbol count, scopes skipped (eval/with), props mangled.
  • Default off in serve (dev), on in build --minify/production; cache key (bndl-cache) includes MinifyOptions hash.
  • Disable mangling per-scope is observable in source-map names; document for downstream error-tracking integration.

24. Phase 12 — Dev server + Hot Module Replacement

12.1 Objectives

  • bndl serve: HTTP server (std TcpListener + minimal HTTP/1.1) serving native ESM on demand, Vite-style — no bundle, browser drives the graph via <script type=module>.
  • Transform-per-request: each GET /src/x.tsx → resolve → load → bndl-transform → bndl-codegen Printer (ESM, no chunking), memoized through bndl-cache.
  • Dependency pre-bundling: bundle CJS/large node_modules deps once to a single ESM artifact (fewer requests, CJS→ESM interop done upfront).
  • fs Watcher → bndl-graph invalidation → HmrEngine boundary computation → websocket update/prune/full-reload.
  • Client runtime: import.meta.hot (accept/dispose/invalidate/prune) + WS receiver + ESM re-fetch with cache-busting ?t= query; target < 50 ms edit→browser-apply for a single accepting module, deterministic full-reload fallback when no boundary accepts (see 12.4).

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (all under crates/bndl-server/src/):

File Adds
server.rs DevServer{listener, graph: ModuleGraph, cache: Cache, transform pipeline, watcher, ws_clients: Vec<WsConn>}; fn serve(&mut self, addr); request router
http.rs minimal HTTP/1.1 parse + response; MIME by ext; ETag = CacheKey; 304 on If-None-Match
rewrite.rs import-specifier rewrite: bare react → /@id/react, relative kept, inject ?import for assets; inject HMR preamble into entry HTML
prebundle.rs scan entry import-records for bare/CJS deps; bndl-graph+bndl-chunk single-chunk ESM build → node_modules/.bndl/<dep>.js; keyed by lockfile+dep version hash
watch.rs Watcher (notify-style poll/event); debounce 10 ms; path → ModuleId; emits Changed(ModuleId) / Created / Removed
hmr.rs HmrEngine{accepted: HashSet<ModuleId>}, HmrBoundary{module, accepts}; fn compute_update(changed: ModuleId) -> HmrUpdate
protocol.rs enum HmrMsg{ Update{updates: Vec<UpdateEntry>}, Prune{paths}, FullReload, Error{msg}, Connected }; JSON wire
client.rs embedded JS (include_str!) HMR client runtime; import.meta.hot shim

Per-request flow GET /<path> (ADR-21 Vite-style ESM-native dev server; P5 Vite on-demand transform; ADR-19 content-addressed cache): parse → resolve to ModuleId (load if absent, add edges to graph) → cache.get(CacheKey) else run TypeStrip→JsxTransform→Lowering→Define + Printer(ESM) + rewrite.rs → set ETag, Content-Type: text/javascript → store. CSS/JSON/asset → ESM-wrapped (export default URL/parsed value).

compute_update(changed) — HMR boundary propagation state machine:

State Condition tested on module m Transition / output
Init — worklist ← [changed]; seen ← ∅; boundaries ← ∅
Visit m already in seen skip m; advance to next in worklist
Visit m calls import.meta.hot.accept() record BoundaryFor(m) in boundaries; advance
Visit importers of m = ∅ (root/entry) OR any importer of m is an HTML entry point emit FullReload — no accepting boundary found / propagation reached entry
Visit importers non-empty, none of the above enqueue all importers of m; advance
Done worklist empty emit HmrUpdate {acceptingModule, changedChain, ?t=<bust>} per boundary

Invalidation: Watcher::Changed(id) → cache.invalidate(id) + downstream parse/transform/codegen artifacts + bump graph.version for that node → trigger compute_update (P6 Vite HMR boundary semantics; ADR-21 ws low-latency; S11 WebSocket RFC 6455).

12.3 Deliverable

bndl serve ./example boots a dev server (default :5173), serves the example app as native ESM, pre-bundles its node_modules deps once, and applies a single-module edit in the browser via HMR in < 50 ms with no full reload; editing a non-accepting leaf escalates deterministically to one full reload.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Cold first-request transform (1 module, ~5 KB) < 8 ms (cache miss)
Warm request (cache hit, 304/ETag) < 1 ms
HMR edit→apply (accepting module) < 50 ms p95
Full-reload decision latency < 10 ms
Pre-bundle (10 CJS deps) once, < 400 ms; cached across restarts
WS message correctness exactly one Update per accepted edit; exactly one FullReload per non-accepted edit
Determinism rewritten module URL set stable across runs

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
serve/accept-self.test/ (module a.js w/ import.meta.hot.accept()) edit a → single Update, no reload; module re-executed once
serve/accept-dep.test/ (a accepts ./b) edit b → Update targets a, b re-fetched w/ ?t=
serve/no-accept.test/ (plain chain entry→a→b) edit b → FullReload
serve/prune.test/ (remove import of c) Prune{["/src/c.js"]} + dispose callbacks fire
serve/cjs-dep.test/ (require-based dep) pre-bundled to ESM; served from .bndl/; one request
serve/css-hmr.test/ CSS edit → CSS-only Update, no JS re-exec, no reload
Malformed: serve/parse-error.test/ (introduce syntax error mid-edit) WS Error{msg}; overlay shown; prior module kept; recover on fix
Malformed: serve/circular-accept.test/ (cycle, one accepts) no infinite walk (seen guard); Update at accepting node

12.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
HMR applies stale module browser ESM cache; missing ?t=<ts> bump log fetched URL; assert query changes per update
Infinite boundary walk importer cycle without seen set dump seen + queue; assert termination
Full-reload storms bare/node_modules files watched; non-debounced editor save (write-temp+rename) filter watch to project src; debounce + coalesce by ModuleId
Pre-bundle staleness dep updated but artifact key only hashed specifier, not version/lockfile include lockfile hash + dep package.json version in CacheKey
import.meta.hot undefined in prod-shaped code client runtime not injected / hot not tree-shaken in build mode gate behind dev; assert hot is undefined in build output
Accept callback double-fire dispose not invoked before re-exec; state leaks (timers, listeners) enforce dispose→re-import order; track per-module dispose handlers

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Transform-per-request hot path: must hit bndl-cache ≥ 99% in steady state; cold module budget < 8 ms ⇒ no full-graph work per request, only the requested module.
  • WS push, not poll; single broadcast per edit; diff payload (changed URLs only), not whole graph. Pre-bundle amortizes: N bare-dep requests → 1; without it browser issues 100s of node_modules requests (waterfall).
  • Watcher debounce 10 ms coalesces editor multi-write saves; avoid O(modules) rescan — index path→ModuleId in a map.
  • Keep-alive HTTP/1.1 + small response header set; 304 short-circuit before transform.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Editor save = delete+create+rename → treat as Changed, not Removed+Created (debounce window).
  • New file added that nothing imports yet → no update (not reachable); becomes live on next importer edit. Removed module still imported elsewhere → Prune only when truly orphaned; else Update.
  • Self-accepting entry module → Update not FullReload.
  • import.meta.hot.accept(deps, cb) array form vs bare self-accept — both supported; bare = re-exec self.
  • invalidate() inside accept callback → bubble up to next boundary (re-run compute_update from importer).
  • Dynamic import() of a changed chunk → re-fetch on next call; no eager HMR (not statically pinned).
  • CSS module: replace <style> text in place, never re-run JS, never reload.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Parse/transform error on edit pipeline returns ParseError WS Error{msg} + browser overlay; keep last-good module; clear on fix
WS disconnect (client navigates/sleeps) socket EOF client auto-reconnect w/ backoff; on reconnect send Connected → client does full reload to resync
Boundary walk reaches entry importers.empty() / HTML importer deterministic FullReload (documented, not a bug)
Pre-bundle build fails bndl-chunk error surface in terminal + overlay; serve nothing for that dep; fail fast
Watcher misses event (fs limits) optional poll fallback timer periodic mtime reconcile; manual reload always works
State leak after many HMR cycles dispose handlers not registered dev-only leak warning if accept w/o dispose mutating globals

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Flags: --port, --host, --open, --no-hmr (force full-reload on every change), --force (rebuild pre-bundle cache).
  • Logs: per-request method path status ms (hit|miss); HMR update <accepting-module> in <ms>ms / full reload.
  • Endpoints: /@id/* (bare-resolved), /@bndl/client (HMR client), /@bndl/hmr (WS upgrade), /@fs/* (out-of-root abs paths, gated).
  • Pre-bundle cache: node_modules/.bndl/; metadata _metadata.json with key hashes; auto-invalidate on lockfile change.
  • Dev-only: HMR client + import.meta.hot injected only under serve, build strips them (no runtime cost in prod); security: bind 127.0.0.1 by default, /@fs/ restricted to configured roots to prevent arbitrary file read.

25. Phase 13 — Incremental cache, conformance, fuzzing, benchmarks

The project "done" gate. Lands bndl-cache (content-addressed memoization + on-disk persistence + incremental rebuild) and the full validation harness: Test262 parser conformance, golden bundling fixtures, differential vs esbuild/Rollup, fuzzing (no-panic + roundtrip), and criterion benchmarks gating the section-4 targets. No new compiler semantics — only memoization, validation, and reproducibility infra over the now-complete pipeline (Phases 0–12).

13.1 Objectives

  • bndl-cache: CacheKey = BLAKE3(content ⊕ canonicalized-options ⊕ tool-version); two-tier store (in-mem DashMap + on-disk CAS); memoize Artifact::{Parsed,Transformed,Emitted}.
  • Incremental rebuild: reuse unchanged artifacts; only changed transitive-closure modules re-parse/transform/emit; correct invalidation (content + option + dependency-edge changes).
  • Conformance: Test262 parser pass/fail buckets (expect-pass parse-clean, expect-fail → ParseError); golden bundle fixtures (byte-stable output); differential semantics vs esbuild + Rollup on shared corpus.
  • Fuzzing: cargo-fuzz targets — lex/parse never panic, parse→print→parse roundtrip stability, resolver robustness on adversarial paths.
  • Benchmarks: criterion suites asserting section-4 throughput/latency with CI regression gates.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Module / file Work
bndl-cache/src/key.rs CacheKey([u8;32]); fn key(content:&[u8], opts:&CanonOpts) -> CacheKey = BLAKE3 over (VERSION_TAG, content, bincode(opts)). CanonOpts: sorted maps, normalized paths, no HashMap iteration order → determinism (ADR-14 BLAKE3; ADR-19 content-addressed cache over mtime).
bndl-cache/src/store.rs Cache{ mem: DashMap<CacheKey,Artifact>, dir: PathBuf }. get(k)->Option<Artifact> (mem→disk fallback, hydrate mem); put(k,a) (mem + atomic disk write: temp + rename). On-disk layout <dir>/<aa>/<aabbcc…>.art, sharded by first byte.
bndl-cache/src/codec.rs Artifact ser/de (bincode v2). Program made Serialize with Atom interner side-table → re-intern on load (atoms not stable across runs). Validate magic + VERSION_TAG; mismatch → cold miss, not error.
bndl-cli/src/build.rs Orchestrator memoizes per stage: parse(src) → Parsed; transform(ast,ctx) → Transformed; emit_chunk → Emitted. Cache miss recomputes + put.
bndl-cli/src/incremental.rs RebuildPlan: diff prev vs new ModuleGraph by content hash; mark dirty set = changed ∪ {m : edge-set(m)≠prev}; recompute dirty + downstream chunks whose member hash changed; reuse rest.
tests/conformance/ test262.rs harness: walk vendored Test262 test/, read frontmatter (negative.phase==parse → expect ParseError), bucket results, write test262-buckets.json.
tests/fixtures/ golden.rs: per-fixture input/ + config → bndl build → compare OutputChunk{file_name,code} to expected/. UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 rewrites.
tests/differential/ diff.rs: run bndl vs esbuild + Rollup (behind dev-dep/feature from Phase 0); execute all three outputs in a sandboxed JS runner; assert observable-result equality (not byte equality) (ADR-24 differential oracle; ADR-25 Test262 corpus).
fuzz/fuzz_targets/ lex.rs, parse.rs, roundtrip.rs, resolve.rs (libFuzzer).
benches/ bench_lex.rs, bench_parse.rs, bench_build.rs, bench_incremental.rs (criterion); bench-baseline.json for gate.

13.3 Deliverable

bndl build --cache <dir> performs incremental rebuilds; cargo test -p conformance green; cargo fuzz run <t> clean for the CI budget; cargo bench emits bench-report.json; CI gate scripts/check-bench.sh fails on >10% regression. Project ships.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Cold-build determinism byte-identical output across 3 runs + 2 platforms (linux/macOS)
Incremental: 1-leaf-module edit, 5k-module graph <50 ms rebuild; only dirty closure recomputed (assert recompute count)
Cache hit ratio, unchanged rebuild ≥99% (≤1% cold misses tolerated for atom re-intern edge)
Test262 parser 100% of curated expect-pass parse-clean; 100% expect-fail (parse-phase) → ParseError; allowlist for known-unsupported stage-3 proposals
Golden fixtures 100% byte-match; no implicit update in CI
Differential vs esbuild + Rollup observable-equal on full corpus; documented divergences allowlisted w/ rationale
Fuzz 0 panics/OOM/timeouts over 15-min CI budget per target
Bench regression gate wall-time & alloc within 10% of bench-baseline.json

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture / file Expected
fixtures/cache_incremental/ (edit b.ts, rebuild) a,c artifacts reused (cache hit), only b + owning chunk recomputed; output byte-stable vs full build
fixtures/cache_option_change/ (flip Target es2022→es5) full transform/emit miss (option in key); parse artifacts reused
fixtures/golden_treeshake_split/ golden entry-<hash>.js + shared chunk; hashes stable run-to-run
test262/expect_pass/*.js parse → 0 ParseError
test262/expect_fail_parse/*.js (e.g. with in strict, dup params strict) exactly one ParseError of the right ParseErrorKind
diff/cjs_esm_interop/ bndl/esbuild/Rollup runtime outputs equal (default-export + named interop)
Malformed: truncated .art file / wrong VERSION_TAG / bit-flipped payload cold miss + recompute, never panic/incorrect artifact
Malformed: 0-byte source, lone surrogate, 8 MB single line, deeply nested ((((…)))) lexer/parser return Invalid/ParseError, no panic, bounded memory

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Root cause Debug
Stale cache returns wrong code after option change option not in CacheKey (e.g. DefineMap, jsx_runtime) dump key inputs; assert every codegen-affecting field in CanonOpts; add option-flip fixture
Non-deterministic output across runs HashMap iteration in chunk/rename/export ordering replace with sorted/IndexMap; run determinism fixture in CI; bisect by hashing per-stage output
Atom interner IDs leak into on-disk artifact Atom is per-run interner index, not stable serialize symbol strings + re-intern on load; never persist raw NodeId/Atom ints
Incremental misses a downstream chunk dirty set computed on module content only, ignores edge changes include import/export record diff in dirty test; chunk dirty if any member hash changed
Test262 "failures" that are really unsupported proposals stage-3 syntax not in Phase 2/3 grammar maintain explicit allowlist file; fail CI only on regressions vs allowlist, not raw count
Differential false-positive on whitespace/key order comparing bytes not behavior compare executed output values; normalize via JSON of exported bindings
Fuzz corpus rot / coverage stall minimized corpus not committed commit fuzz/corpus/; seed from Test262 + fixtures; track coverage in CI summary

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • BLAKE3 keying on hot path: hash once per source read, reuse for content-hash + cache key; ~GB/s, negligible vs parse.
  • Disk store: shard by first byte (256 dirs) to avoid huge-dir readdir cost; atomic rename (no fsync per-artifact, single dir fsync at end) → throughput over durability.
  • Memoize at coarse stage boundaries (parse/transform/emit), not per-node → bincode amortized; avoid per-module syscall storm via batched disk writes.
  • Bench harness: pin CPU governor, warm FS cache, fixed thread count; report wall + peak RSS + alloc count; criterion sample ≥50 for stable medians.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty cache dir / read-only dir → fall back to in-mem only, warn, never fail build.
  • Concurrent builds sharing one cache dir → atomic rename makes writes safe; reads tolerate partial absence (cold miss).
  • Cache version bump (VERSION_TAG) → all old artifacts treated as misses; no migration, no crash.
  • File touched (mtime change) but content identical → content hash unchanged → cache hit (mtime never in key).
  • Symlinked source resolving to already-cached realpath → single artifact keyed by content, not path.
  • Test262 file with BOM / \r\n / module-vs-script frontmatter → honor flags: [module]; set SourceType accordingly.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
Silent stale artifact (correctness) option-flip + content-flip fixtures in CI; differential suite treat as P0; key audit; ship --no-cache escape hatch
Corrupt on-disk artifact magic + VERSION_TAG + length check on load cold miss + recompute; optional checksum on payload
Determinism regression 3×/2-platform byte-compare gate bisect per-stage hash; block merge
Fuzz crash libFuzzer artifact reproducer committed to corpus minimize, add as regression test, fix, re-run budget
Benchmark regression check-bench.sh vs baseline block merge unless baseline re-blessed with justification

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: --cache <dir> (default node_modules/.bndl-cache), --no-cache, bndl analyze --cache-stats (hit/miss, size, evictable).
  • CI matrix: {linux,macOS} × {debug,release} for tests/determinism; fuzz as nightly 15-min/target + PR 60-s smoke; benches nightly with trend dashboard.
  • Cache GC: LRU by on-disk mtime over a size cap (default 512 MB); bndl analyze --gc.
  • Reproducibility contract: pin MSRV 1.78, vendored Test262 commit SHA, esbuild/Rollup versions recorded in bench-report.json + diff allowlist header.
  • Done-gate checklist (must all pass): conformance green · golden byte-match · 0 fuzz crashes · bench within gate · incremental rebuild verified on 5k-module corpus.

Threat Model

bndl runs untrusted inputs at build time: source files, transitive node_modules (npm packages are arbitrary code + arbitrary package.json), config, and third-party Plugin implementations. The bundler is a compiler, not a runtime — but it reads adversarial bytes on the parser hot path, walks attacker-controlled package.json exports maps, and follows symlinks into the filesystem. Adversary goals: crash/hang the build (DoS), escape the project jail to read/write arbitrary files, execute code at build time (RCE), poison the content-addressed cache, inject code into output chunks, or exfiltrate proprietary source. Non-goal: bndl does not sandbox executed JavaScript (no engine — #126); it never runs user JS, only transforms it.

26.1 Trust boundaries

  TRUSTED                          | SEMI-TRUSTED                | UNTRUSTED
  ---------------------------------+-----------------------------+---------------------------------
  bndl binary + bndl-* crates      | project source (1st-party)  | node_modules/** (transitive deps)
  vetted std + deny.toml deps      | bndl.config.* (in-repo)     | every package.json (exports/main)
  the OS + the build user's perms  | 1st-party Plugin code       | source bytes fed to lexer/parser
                                    |                             | 3rd-party Plugin code
                                    |                             | tsconfig.json / JSON assets
  jail root = project dir + each resolved package's node_modules subtree (realpath-confined)
  • B1 source-bytes boundary — parse_program(src,...) consumes untrusted UTF-8 (or invalid UTF-8). Must be total (returns (Program, Vec<ParseError>), never panics, never infinite-loops).
  • B2 filesystem boundary — Resolver may only open paths whose realpath is inside the jail. Specifier → path is the escape vector.
  • B3 config/plugin boundary — config + Plugin hooks are data + pure functions, never an implicit code-execution trigger. bndl has no postinstall/lifecycle-script execution (that is #135 Package Manager).
  • B4 cache boundary — an Artifact is trusted iff its CacheKey (BLAKE3) covers every input that influenced it; otherwise a poisoned/stale entry crosses into trusted output.
  • B5 output boundary — emitted chunks + maps may carry secrets (sourcesContent) or injected code (define/JSON) across to the published artifact / browser.

26.2 Threat catalog (STRIDE-tagged)

# Threat (STRIDE) Vector Mitigation Detection / Telemetry Recovery
T1 Algorithmic-complexity DoS (D) pathological source: deeply nested (((…))) / a.b.c… / nested template; multi-MB file; huge import fan-out; long cycle chain parser recursion depth cap (default 1000, configurable) → ParseErrorKind::UnexpectedToken("nesting too deep"); per-file byte cap (default 32 MiB); per-module wall-clock timeout under the parallel pool; bounded worklists in graph/treeshake/chunk (no unbounded recursion — explicit stacks) per-module parse-time + token-count histograms; warn on files >4 MiB or depth >256; emit module that tripped a cap skip + report the offending module, continue build with a hard error (non-zero ExitCode); never hang
T2 Parser panic / OOM on adversarial bytes (D) malformed UTF-8, lone surrogates, truncated template/regex/string, 4-byte code-point split, NUL bytes, BOM games lexer never panics — emits TokenKind::Invalid + recovers; parser uses panic-mode recovery (sync to ;/}); arena/bump allocator with a ceiling; #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in lexer/parser/ast where feasible; total Result/diagnostics, no unwrap on input-derived data cargo-fuzz targets fuzz_lex, fuzz_parse, fuzz_roundtrip run in CI; panic-hook counter must stay 0; ASan/leak under fuzz corpus minimization + regression fixture added on any crash; blueprint gate (Phase 13) blocks on a clean 24h fuzz run
T3 Path traversal / symlink escape via exports map (E/T) malicious package.json "exports": {"./x":"../../../../etc/passwd"}; symlinked node_modules/evil → /; absolute target; .. in subpath pattern * PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE rejects targets that escape the package dir (no leading /, no .. after normalization, must start ./) → ResolveError::InvalidExports; realpath every resolved path (symlink resolution) then assert prefix ∈ jail roots; reject resolved paths outside jail → ResolveError::NotFound; cache realpath results log every rejected target with package name + raw target string; counter of out-of-jail rejections resolution fails for that specifier; build errors with the offending package + specifier; no file is opened
T4 Build-time RCE via malicious dependency (E) dep expects postinstall/lifecycle scripts; a "loader" that shells out; a Plugin doing Command::new bndl executes no package lifecycle scripts — installation is out of scope (#135); Plugin hooks (resolve/load/transform/render_chunk) are pure data transforms over &self returning Option<…>, with no bundler-provided exec/fs-write/network API; OS-level isolation is the user's responsibility (documented: run builds as a low-priv user / container); plugins are 1st-party-vetted code, treated as semi-trusted (B3) plugin host logs which plugin handled each hook; (future) optional per-plugin time/alloc budget; deny-by-default network is an OS/container control, not in-process a misbehaving plugin can be removed from config; no implicit execution means adding a dep cannot run code
T5 Cache poisoning / stale artifact (T/S) attacker (or a bug) seeds the on-disk store with a forged Artifact; or an input changed but the key didn't → stale output silently trusted CacheKey = BLAKE3(content ‖ ALL options) — key folds source bytes and every option that changes output: Target, Syntax, DefineMap, OutputFormat, jsx_runtime, resolver conditions, minify flags, bndl version, and dependency content hashes (transitive); content-addressed read verifies the stored blob hashes back to its key (integrity check) before use; reject + recompute on mismatch cache hit/miss/integrity-fail counters; log every integrity rejection (corrupt or forged); fixture asserting an option flip → new key integrity failure ⇒ discard entry, recompute from source (cache is an optimization, never authoritative); bndl build --no-cache bypass
T6 Prototype pollution / code injection via define + JSON (E/T) DefineMap value __proto__/constructor keys; a define replacement string parsed as an expression with side effects; a JSON-loaded module containing "__proto__"; define value smuggling JS define values are parsed once into an AST Expr and inserted as nodes (never string-concatenated into output) — no textual injection; define keys validated as dotted identifier paths (reject keys touching __proto__/prototype/constructor); JSON loader parses to data and emits a frozen object literal — keys emitted as quoted string literals, never as accessors, so __proto__ becomes a normal own property warn on define keys matching the pollution denylist; reject malformed define expr at config load config-load error rejects the build before any module is processed
T7 Source leak via source maps (I) published .map embeds sourcesContent (full proprietary source) or a sourceRoot pointing at internal paths sourcesContent is opt-in (default off for production build; on for serve); sourceRoot / source-path rewriting policy is configurable; map emission + path policy delegated to the #265 codec (bndl never hand-rolls VLQ or the map JSON) build summary states whether maps + sourcesContent were emitted; warn if production build emits inline maps omit/strip maps via config; policy enforced at emit time in Emitter via #265
T8 Supply-chain risk in our own dependency tree (S/T) a transitive crate bndl depends on is compromised/yanked/typosquatted minimal dependency surface (spine mandates std for all 12 crates; few external crates — e.g. BLAKE3, criterion dev-only); cargo deny (deny.toml): advisory DB check, license allowlist, ban duplicate/unknown sources, pin git deps; generate an SBOM (CycloneDX) per release; Cargo.lock committed; MSRV 1.78 pinned CI fails on cargo deny check advisories/license/bans; SBOM diff reviewed per release pin/patch/replace the offending crate; lockfile rollback
T9 Non-determinism enabling cache/CDN attacks (T) nondeterministic output ⇒ a cache key maps to varying bytes ⇒ CDN/cache confusion, or a poisoned variant served determinism invariant: identical inputs+options ⇒ byte-identical output. Sources: stable module ordering (by resolved path/ModuleId), stable identifier-rename order in Renamer, stable chunk ordering, content hash = BLAKE3 of final chunk bytes with a fixed-point pass for cross-chunk hash refs, no time/RNG/HashMap-iteration-order in output paths (use sorted maps / IndexMap) a "build twice, diff bytes" CI gate; per-chunk hash logged in the manifest determinism is a release gate; a diff fails CI and blocks publish
T10 DoS via resolver fan-out / cyclic re-export (D) export * from chains forming huge/cyclic re-export graphs; node_modules with millions of files; resolution loops export-star resolution memoized + cycle-guarded (visited ModuleId set) → terminates, no double-count; resolver caches package.json/realpath/dir-listing; directory probing bounded by configured extensions list (no unbounded globbing); cycle detection in bndl-graph records, never recurses infinitely count of resolved modules + cycles in build summary; warn past a module-count threshold bounded work ⇒ build completes or errors deterministically; never hangs
T11 Tree-shaking soundness as a security property (T) over-aggressive DCE drops a security-relevant side effect (CSP nonce install, polyfill, license banner) soundness rule: never remove code with observable side effects unless proven pure; sideEffects:false + /*#__PURE__*/ are explicit author contracts, honored not assumed; SideEffect::Unknown is treated as Impure (conservative); top-level effectful statements pin their module differential tests vs esbuild/Rollup on the fixture suite assert effect preservation; conformance gate (Phase 13) a divergence is a correctness bug (release blocker), not tunable
T12 Resource exhaustion of the dev server (D) serve mode: many connections, rapid file churn, huge HMR fan-out, watch-event storm bound concurrent transforms (thread-pool sized); debounce Watcher fs events; cap HMR boundary propagation breadth before falling back to full reload; per-request transform timeout; dev server binds localhost by default (not 0.0.0.0) ws connection count, transform queue depth, HMR boundary fan-out logged reject excess connections; full-reload fallback when boundary set explodes; restart watcher on event-stream error

26.3 Hardening posture (defaults)

Control Default Rationale
Parser recursion cap 1000 bound stack depth (T1)
Max file size 32 MiB bound parse memory/time (T1)
Per-module parse timeout on (parallel pool) escape pathological inputs (T1, T10)
unsafe in lexer/parser/ast forbidden where feasible untrusted-byte hot path (T2)
Realpath + jail check always on symlink/traversal escape (B2, T3)
Plugin exec API none provided no implicit RCE (T4)
Lifecycle/postinstall scripts never run installation is #135 (T4)
sourcesContent off for build, on for serve avoid source leak in published maps (T7)
Cache integrity verify-on-read on reject poisoned/corrupt blobs (T5)
cargo deny + SBOM CI-enforced per release own supply chain (T8)
Determinism (build-twice) CI gate cache/CDN integrity (T9)
Dev server bind addr 127.0.0.1 reduce exposure (T12)

26.4 Residual risk & explicit non-mitigations

  • No in-process plugin sandbox — a malicious 3rd-party Plugin runs with the build user's privileges; isolation is delegated to the OS/container (documented). bndl mitigates only implicit execution (T4), not plugins the user chose to install.
  • No JS execution sandboxing — bndl never executes bundled code (no engine, #126); runtime exploits in output are out of scope.
  • No type-safety guarantees from stripped types — TS types are parsed and discarded, never checked (#42); a type error is not a bundler security concern.
  • Decompression/zip-bomb-style inputs — bndl reads plain source files; archive extraction is out of scope (#135).
  • DoS resistance is best-effort with caps, not a hard real-time guarantee; an attacker who controls the entire entry tree can still make a build slow within the configured bounds — caps ensure it terminates, not that it is fast.

Failure Modes and Recovery

Taxonomy of failures by stage, ordered by severity. Severity scale: S0 = silent miscompile (wrong output, no error) — gravest; S1 = build abort / crash; S2 = degraded result (bloat, slow); S3 = dev-loop annoyance. Tree-shaking over-removal (FM-04) and rename capture (FM-06) are the only S0 classes and get the deepest tooling.

Severity index

ID Failure Stage Sev Caught by Recoverable?
FM-01 Parse error bndl-parser S1→S3 ParseError + span yes, recovery node
FM-02 Unresolved specifier bndl-resolver S1 ResolveError::NotFound no (operator)
FM-03 Cyclic dep needs wrapper bndl-graph/codegen S2→S0 cycle detector yes (wrapper)
FM-04 Tree-shake over-removal bndl-treeshake S0 differential + soundness trace + fix marker
FM-05 Tree-shake under-removal bndl-treeshake S2 size budget diff yes (annotation)
FM-06 Rename capture / scope bug bndl-codegen Renamer S0 scope-invariant assert bisect + fix scope
FM-07 Cross-chunk hash non-convergence bndl-chunk S1 fixed-point iteration cap yes (more iters)
FM-08 CJS↔ESM interop mismatch bndl-codegen runtime S0→S1 interop conformance yes (helper fix)
FM-09 Source-map drift bndl-codegen / #265 S2 map verifier yes (recompose)
FM-10 Stale cache artifact bndl-cache S0→S2 CacheKey mismatch yes (invalidate)
FM-11 HMR boundary miss bndl-server S3 accepted-set check yes (full reload)
FM-12 OOM on giant graph orchestrator S1 memory watermark yes (spill/serial)

FM-01 — Parse error

  • Symptoms: Vec<ParseError> non-empty; build aborts (build) or red overlay (serve).
  • Root causes: invalid syntax; JsxNotEnabled/TrailingTsType (Syntax flags off for file); unterminated string/template/regex; reserved word as binding.
  • Detection: parser returns (Program, Vec<ParseError>) — never panics (Phase 1 invariant). Each ParseError{kind: ParseErrorKind, span: Span}.
  • Automatic recovery: insert error/Invalid recovery node, resync to next statement boundary (;, }, newline w/ ASI), continue → collect all errors in one pass (not fail-fast).
  • Manual recovery: operator fixes source; or set Syntax{typescript,jsx} correctly per extension.
  • Operator actions: read span-anchored diagnostic (file:line:col + caret + source line).
  • Forensic artifacts: error list w/ spans; --dump-tokens token stream around span.
  • Long-term mitigation: Test262 negative corpus (Phase 13) pins recoverable vs fatal; fuzzing asserts no-panic.

FM-02 — Unresolved specifier

  • Symptoms: ResolveError for a module specifier; graph build halts on that edge.
  • Root causes: NotFound (bad path/extension); NotExported (subpath not in exports); InvalidExports (malformed map); BadTsPaths; missing Condition (e.g. needs import but only require defined); case-mismatch on case-sensitive FS.
  • Detection: Resolver exhausts CJS LOAD_* + ESM PACKAGE_RESOLVE probes, returns ResolveError with the attempted candidate list.
  • Automatic recovery: none (resolution must be deterministic); but classify External/Builtin first to avoid false negatives.
  • Manual recovery: add extension to ResolveOptions.extensions; fix tsconfig paths/baseUrl; add export condition; mark external.
  • Operator actions: bndl analyze --resolve <spec> prints the probe trace (every path tried, every condition tested).
  • Forensic artifacts: ordered candidate-path log; matched/rejected condition set; resolved package.json location.
  • Long-term mitigation: resolver fixture suite mirroring Node's official resolution test matrix.

FM-03 — Cyclic dependency requiring a wrapper (deadlock if mishandled)

  • Symptoms: import cycle through ESM/CJS; if naively concatenated → TDZ ReferenceError or undefined binding at module-init; mishandled wrapper → init recursion / deadlock (module A's init awaits B's, B's awaits A's).
  • Root causes: cycle detected late; scope-hoisting a module that must stay wrapped; wrapper init not memoized before recursing into deps (esbuild __esm must set the "started" flag before running the body).
  • Detection: bndl-graph records SCCs; codegen flags any cyclic member as wrapper-required (cannot scope-hoist, per soundness rule).
  • Automatic recovery: wrap in runtime fn with first-call memoization — init_X sets inited=true and exposes the (live-binding) exports object before executing the body, so a re-entrant call from within the cycle gets the partial namespace (matches ESM live-binding semantics) instead of looping.
  • Manual recovery: operator breaks the cycle (move shared decl to a third module) to re-enable hoisting/perf.
  • Operator actions: bndl graph --cycles lists every SCC + the edge that closes it.
  • Forensic artifacts: SCC membership; per-module wrapper-required reason; init-order trace.
  • Long-term mitigation: fixtures for ESM↔ESM, CJS↔CJS, mixed cycles incl. the classic TDZ-in-cycle case; assert no infinite init.

FM-04 — Tree-shaking OVER-removal (gravest, S0)

  • Symptoms: runtime behavior changes — a polyfill/CSS-side-import/Object.defineProperty/registration call vanished; output runs but is semantically wrong. No build error — that is what makes it S0.
  • Root causes: symbol marked SideEffect::Pure that was Impure/Unknown; honored sideEffects:false on a module that has effects; misread /*#__PURE__*/; dropped a re-export still consumed via export-star; pruned a top-level statement with an observable effect.
  • Detection (defense in depth):
    1. Differential oracle (Phase 0/13): run same input through esbuild/Rollup, compare retained symbol set + executed effects; divergence on a removal = suspect over-removal.
    2. Soundness tests: corpus where every effect is observable (writes a sentinel); post-bundle execution under #126-external harness must still observe all sentinels.
    3. Conservative default: SideEffect::Unknown ⇒ treat as Impure (pin the statement). Removal requires proof of purity, never absence of proof.
  • Automatic recovery: none at runtime (already wrong). Build-time guard: if soundness sentinel missing, fail the build rather than ship.
  • Manual recovery: correct the sideEffects field / /*#__PURE__*/; downgrade aggressive setting.
  • Operator actions — trace-hook methodology: bndl analyze --why-dropped <module>#<symbol> emits the removal justification chain: which Reachability pass never reached it, which SideEffect verdict allowed removal, and the source annotation/sideEffects rule that produced the verdict. Every drop decision logs (SymbolId, reason, evidence) — there is no unexplained removal.
  • Forensic artifacts: full ReachableSet; per-SymbolId {verdict, evidence, source-of-verdict}; the SymbolGraph edge that was (not) traversed.
  • Long-term mitigation: the soundness corpus is a permanent CI gate; new optimization passes must pass it before merge; bias is always toward keeping (under-removal is FM-05, merely bloat).

FM-05 — Tree-shaking UNDER-removal

  • Symptoms: bundle larger than expected; dead code retained. Correct, just fat. S2.
  • Root causes: over-conservative Unknown→Impure; missing sideEffects:false; effectful module-init pinning otherwise-dead symbols; dynamic property access defeating symbol analysis.
  • Detection: size budget diff vs baseline/oracle; bndl analyze --bloat ranks retained-but-unused candidates.
  • Automatic recovery: none needed (safe). Optional: report-only.
  • Manual recovery: add sideEffects:false//*#__PURE__*/; refactor side-effecting top-level into functions.
  • Operator actions: --why-retained <symbol> (dual of --why-dropped).
  • Forensic artifacts: retained-symbol report w/ pinning reason.
  • Long-term mitigation: track bundle-size regression in benchmark suite; never trade FM-05 fixes for FM-04 risk.

FM-06 — Rename capture / scope bug (S0)

  • Symptoms: after Renamer/scope-hoist/mangle, a reference binds to the wrong declaration — shadowing collapsed, or a hoisted module's local collides with another's. Runs, wrong values. S0.
  • Root causes: ScopeTree mis-built (TDZ, var hoisting, block scope, with/eval not bailed); mangle map not scope-partitioned; cross-module concat introduced a name now captured by an inner scope; function hoisting vs let ordering.
  • Detection — scope invariant (Phase 8/11): after rename, every reference resolves to the same binding identity as before rename (compare pre/post (NodeId → declaring NodeId) maps). Any mismatch = capture bug, hard assert in debug + test.
  • Automatic recovery: none (already miscompiled) → assertion fails the build in conformance mode.
  • Manual recovery: disable mangle/hoist for the offending module (wrapper fallback) to unblock while fixing.
  • Operator actions — bisect methodology: bndl build --no-mangle / --no-hoist to localize stage; --trace-rename <NodeId> prints the scope chain + chosen new name + collision-avoidance decision for that reference; minimize fixture by SCC.
  • Forensic artifacts: pre/post reference-resolution maps (the diff is the bug); ScopeTree dump (scopes, bindings, references, ids); per-rename (old, new, scope-id).
  • Long-term mitigation: roundtrip property test — parse→rename→print→reparse, assert reference-resolution map invariant; with/eval/direct-eval force a hard bail (no mangle, no hoist).

FM-07 — Cross-chunk hash non-convergence

  • Symptoms: content hash of chunk A depends on B's hash, B's on A's; naive single pass yields unstable/incorrect hashes; CDN cache breaks.
  • Root causes: circular cross-chunk import in the hash references; fixed-point not run.
  • Detection: split runs a fixed-point pass — recompute hashes with placeholder→real substitution until stable; cap iterations (e.g. 10) → exceed = non-convergence error.
  • Automatic recovery: iterate to fixpoint; on cap-exceed, fall back to a topological tie-break (hash includes only acyclic dependency hashes + a stable chunk-id for back-edges).
  • Manual recovery: none typically; report bug if cap hit on real input.
  • Operator actions: bndl build --explain-hash <chunk> prints inputs to the hash + iteration count.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-iteration hash table; cross-chunk import graph.
  • Long-term mitigation: determinism test — two clean builds byte-identical; hash references modeled as a DAG with explicit back-edge handling.

FM-08 — CJS↔ESM interop mismatch (default-export shape)

  • Symptoms: import x from 'cjs-pkg' yields the namespace instead of module.exports (or vice-versa); x.default vs x confusion; __esModule flag mishandled.
  • Root causes: wrong interop helper chosen; __toESM not applied to a require of an ESM-faking CJS module; missing __esModule synthesis on __export.
  • Detection: interop conformance fixtures matching Node/esbuild/TS esModuleInterop semantics; default-import-of-CJS returns module.exports.
  • Automatic recovery: runtime helpers (__toESM, __commonJS, __esm, __export, __require) bridge per classified ModuleKind; __toESM(require(...), /*isNodeMode*/) reproduces the documented default-shape rule.
  • Manual recovery: operator overrides module kind / interop flag.
  • Operator actions: bndl analyze --interop <module> shows chosen helper + reasoning.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-edge (importer kind, importee kind, helper, default-shape).
  • Long-term mitigation: interop matrix fixture (ESM↔CJS × default/named/namespace/star) diffed against esbuild + Node.

FM-09 — Source-map drift vs emitted bytes

  • Symptoms: debugger lands on the wrong line; mappings off by N after a stage. S2 (DX, not correctness).
  • Root causes: a stage (transform/print/minify) mutated bytes without composing its position delta; map not recomposed through the pipeline.
  • Detection: map verifier — for sampled output positions, decode via #265 codec, assert mapped source span's text matches; mapping count vs token count sanity.
  • Automatic recovery: recompute the composed map (lex→parse→transform→print→minify deltas) via the #265 codec (bundler never hand-rolls VLQ — see #265).
  • Manual recovery: --no-minify to isolate which stage drifted.
  • Operator actions: bndl build --verify-map runs the verifier and reports first divergent mapping.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-stage delta map; first failing (generated pos → claimed source pos).
  • Long-term mitigation: map composition unit-tested per stage; round-trip fixture asserts column-accurate mapping; format ownership stays in #265.

FM-10 — Stale cache artifact

  • Symptoms: edit not reflected in output; old Artifact returned. S0 if a content change is missed.
  • Root causes: CacheKey (BLAKE3 of content + options) omitted an input (e.g. forgot to fold Target/DefineMap/resolver result into the key); on-disk artifact not invalidated; mtime trusted over content hash.
  • Detection: key is content-addressed over all inputs — content + every option that affects output. Self-check: recompute key on read, compare.
  • Automatic recovery: miss/mismatch ⇒ recompute + repopulate; --no-cache bypass.
  • Manual recovery: bndl build --force clears + rebuilds.
  • Operator actions: bndl analyze --cache <module> prints key inputs + hit/miss.
  • Forensic artifacts: CacheKey byte breakdown (which inputs hashed); hit/miss log.
  • Long-term mitigation: property test — any input/option change ⇒ different key (no missed dependency); never key on mtime alone.

FM-11 — HMR boundary miss (state lost / stale module)

  • Symptoms: edit triggers full reload when partial update expected (state lost), or stale module served (update not propagated). S3.
  • Root causes: HmrBoundary.accepts mis-computed; propagation didn't reach an accepting ancestor; non-self-accepting module with no accepting importer; CSS/asset edge not modeled.
  • Detection: HmrEngine computes the accepted-modules set by walking importers from the changed ModuleId until an accepts boundary; if none reached → must full-reload (not silently stale).
  • Automatic recovery: propagate up to nearest accepting boundary; if boundary set empty/cyclic-without-accept ⇒ full-reload fallback (correctness over speed).
  • Manual recovery: developer adds import.meta.hot.accept.
  • Operator actions: server log prints boundary computation + chosen update path per edit.
  • Forensic artifacts: changed module, importer walk, accepted set, fallback reason.
  • Long-term mitigation: HMR scenario tests (self-accept, parent-accept, no-accept→reload, cyclic) assert correct boundary + no stale module.

FM-12 — OOM on a giant graph

  • Symptoms: memory blows up on huge ModuleGraph (10⁵+ modules); allocator pressure; process killed.
  • Root causes: retaining full token streams/ASTs for all modules simultaneously; cache not bounded; arena never released.
  • Detection: memory watermark monitor; module count + bytes-per-module tracked against a budget (target: linear in source size, see §4).
  • Automatic recovery: drop per-module arenas after the module's Module{imports,exports,symbols} is extracted (AST kept only while needed for transform/codegen); cap in-memory cache (LRU spill to on-disk content store); fall back to serial module processing (less parallel memory) when over watermark.
  • Manual recovery: --max-memory, --no-cache, split into multiple builds.
  • Operator actions: bndl build --mem-stats reports peak RSS, per-stage allocation.
  • Forensic artifacts: watermark timeline; module-count vs RSS curve.
  • Long-term mitigation: allocation-aware arena/bump (Phase 1 rationale); benchmark peak memory on a synthetic 100k-module graph; immutable Send+Sync modules enable bounded parallelism.

Cross-cutting debugging methodology

  1. Reproduce deterministically — identical input+options ⇒ byte-identical output (determinism invariant); a non-reproducible failure is itself a bug (FM-07/FM-10 class).
  2. Bisect by stage — toggle --no-mangle/--no-hoist/--no-treeshake/--no-minify/--no-cache to localize the offending pass; binary-search the pass set.
  3. Trace hooks — --why-dropped / --why-retained (FM-04/05), --trace-rename (FM-06), --explain-hash (FM-07), --verify-map (FM-09), --cache (FM-10): every irreversible decision (drop, rename, hash, interop helper) is justified with (target, reason, evidence) — no unexplained transformation.
  4. Differential oracle — diff against esbuild/Rollup (Phase 0/13); a behavioral divergence on retention/interop is the fastest path to an S0 bug.
  5. Minimize — reduce to smallest failing module / SCC; feed back as a permanent regression fixture.

Severity discipline: when uncertain, always bias toward keeping code (FM-05 bloat) and correct binding (FM-06 fallback to wrapper) over the S0 alternatives — an oversized but correct bundle ships; a miscompiled one does not.

Operational Readiness

bndl is a developer-workstation + CI build tool plus a localhost dev server, not a 24x7 networked service. Operational readiness therefore optimizes for: fast feedback (incremental rebuild p95), reproducibility (byte-identical output), zero-panic on untrusted source, observability for "why did the bundler do X", and a clean crate/binary release surface. No paging, no on-call rotation, no multi-tenant SLA — the "operator" is the developer at their terminal and the CI runner. The dev server's threat surface is bounded to localhost (see Threat Model).

28.1 Metrics surface

Emitted at build/serve end as a BuildReport (machine-readable) and a terminal summary (human). No metrics daemon; metrics are per-invocation, optionally appended to a newline-delimited JSON log (--metrics-log <path>) for CI trend tracking.

Metric Type Source crate Target / note
modules_parsed counter bndl-graph total Module records in graph
modules_from_cache counter bndl-cache cache hits this run
cache_hit_rate gauge bndl-cache hits / (hits+misses); warm-rebuild ≥0.95
parse_ms p50/p95/p99 histogram bndl-parser per-module; p99 < 5× p50 (no pathological skew)
transform_ms p50/p95/p99 histogram bndl-transform per-module per-pass rollup
resolve_ms p50/p95/p99 histogram bndl-resolver per-specifier; cache-cold vs warm separated
emit_ms p50/p95/p99 histogram bndl-codegen per-chunk print+minify+map
graph_nodes / graph_edges gauge bndl-graph size + fan-out; flag cycles count
chunk_count gauge bndl-chunk entry + dynamic + shared
output_bytes (raw / gzip / brotli) gauge bndl-codegen per-chunk + total
tree_shaken_symbols / total_symbols gauge bndl-treeshake removal ratio
hmr_update_ms p50/p95 histogram bndl-server edit→client-applied; p95 < 50 ms small module
dev_request_ms p50/p95/p99 histogram bndl-server on-demand transform request; warm p95 < 20 ms
peak_rss_bytes gauge bndl-cli high-water; alarms vs section-4 mem target
wall_ms, cpu_ms, parallelism gauge bndl-cli cpu/wall ⇒ effective thread utilization

Histograms = fixed log-bucket boundaries (1/2/5/10/20/50/100/200/500/1000 ms) → byte-stable JSON regardless of run. All timings monotonic-clock; never wall-clock (DST/NTP-immune).

28.2 Structured tracing & "why" introspection

Tracing is opt-in and non-recording by default (zero overhead on the hot path). Two layers:

  • Subcommand introspection (always available):
    • bndl analyze → per-module table: bytes (raw/min/gzip), symbols kept/shaken, chunk assignment, side-effect verdict (Pure|Impure|Unknown), from_node_modules.
    • bndl graph → module graph as DOT / JSON: edges typed by ImportKind (Static/Dynamic/Require/ExportStar), cycle sets, entry reachability.
    • bndl graph --why-chunk <module> → ordered explanation: reaching entry-set (module group) → shared-vs-entry decision → final ChunkId + content hash.
