    • bndl analyze --why-kept <module>#<symbol> → reachability path from an entry export to the symbol (TreeShaker worklist trace); or "removed because: no reachable importer / sideEffects:false / /*#__PURE__*/".
    • bndl analyze --why-wrapped <module> → reason scope hoisting fell back to wrapper (cycle / top-level CJS / eval / dynamic re-export).
  • Per-stage trace hooks (--trace <stage,...>): lex, parse, resolve, transform, shake, chunk, emit. Emits structured spans (NDJSON or Chrome trace_event JSON via --trace-format chrome) consumable in chrome://tracing / Perfetto. Span = {stage, module_id, name, start_us, dur_us, attrs}. --trace all for a full flamegraph of one build.

Tracing must never alter output bytes (determinism invariant). Trace IDs derive from ModuleId/ChunkId, not random.

28.3 Logging philosophy

Mode Flag Audience Shape
Human diagnostics (default) — developer colorized, span-anchored: file:line:col + caret underline + message + hint; severity error/warn/info
JSON diagnostics --format json CI / editors one Diagnostic{severity, code, message, file, span{start,end}, related[], hint} per line (NDJSON)
Quiet --quiet scripts errors only, exit code carries result
Verbose -v/-vv debugging adds resolver probe trace, pass ordering, cache key derivation
  • Diagnostics carry a stable code (BNDL1001 etc.) per ParseErrorKind / ResolveError for grep/CI rules and doc cross-links.
  • ParseError recovery ⇒ surface all errors per file (not just first); spans always point into the real SourceFile.
  • Log level via flag or BNDL_LOG env; library crates log via tracing facade, the CLI installs the subscriber (libraries never println). Determinism: log ordering by ModuleId in batch mode, not completion order.

28.4 "Dashboard" = build report + bundle analyzer

No live dashboard. The two artifacts:

  • Build report (bndl build --report <path>): JSON {inputs hash, options hash, metrics (28.1), per-chunk manifest, warnings, timings}. Diffable across commits → CI bundle-budget gates (--max-chunk-bytes, --max-total-bytes fail the build).
  • Bundle analyzer (bndl analyze --treemap <out.html>): self-contained HTML treemap of modules→chunks sized by gzip bytes (esbuild/webpack-bundle-analyzer style); drill into a chunk to see contributing modules + node_modules share. Static file, no server.
  • Manifest (manifest.json, always written on build): chunk file names ↔ entry/source mapping ↔ content hashes ↔ cross-chunk import edges. The integration contract for downstream tooling.

28.5 SLO-style targets

These are correctness/perf gates enforced in Phase 13, not availability SLAs.

Objective Target Enforcement
Cold full build (medium app, ~1k modules) meets section-4 throughput target criterion bench, CI regression guard (±10%)
Incremental rebuild (1 file changed, warm cache) p95 < 200 ms; touched-module reparse + affected-chunk re-emit only bench + cache-hit-rate assertion ≥0.95
HMR update latency p95 < 50 ms (single small module) bench harness scripted edit→ws→apply
Dev-server on-demand request (warm) p95 < 20 ms bench
Zero-panic no panic!/unwrap on any input fuzz (lex/parse/roundtrip) + panic=abort test build; a panic is a release blocker
Build determinism byte-identical output for identical (inputs, options) CI: build twice on 2 OSes/arches, cmp all chunks + maps + manifest
Memory ceiling peak RSS ≤ section-4 target on the fixture app peak_rss_bytes assert in CI

Determinism is a first-class SLO: any nondeterministic output (hash drift, rename-order drift, module-order drift) is treated as a P1 correctness bug, never a flake to retry.

28.6 Incident response & runbooks

No paging; runbooks are developer self-service triage flows, each rooted in a bndl subcommand.

  • "Build is slow / regressed":
    1. cache_hit_rate in report — if low on a warm build → cache busted (version/option change §28.8) or invalidation too coarse; inspect with -vv cache-key derivation.
    2. cpu_ms/wall_ms ≈ 1 → no parallelism; check thread-pool size / a serialization point (single huge entry, deep cycle forcing sequential wrapper init).
    3. parse_ms/transform_ms p99 ≫ p50 → pathological module (giant generated file, deeply nested JSX/templates, huge object literal). bndl analyze sorts by per-module time; consider marking it external or sideEffects.
    4. Resolver thrash → resolve_ms cold high; symlink/realpath storms or missing resolution cache → confirm cache populated.
  • "Output differs across machines": run the determinism audit: bndl build on both, cmp chunk bytes; if drift, bisect with --trace chunk,emit — usual causes: (a) chunk-hash fixed-point not converged, (b) rename order seeded by pointer/iteration order instead of stable SymbolId, (c) module ordering from filesystem readdir order, (d) absolute paths leaking into output. All four are invariant violations (System Invariants) → fix, add a regression fixture.
  • "HMR not updating": bndl serve -vv →
    1. Did the Watcher see the fs event? (editor atomic-rename / network FS misses → fall back to polling watcher).
    2. HMR boundary trace (--trace hmr): was an accepting HmrBoundary found? If none up the importer chain → expected full-reload fallback (working as designed).
    3. ws connected? client runtime handshake in browser console; stale client → reload.
    4. Boundary computed but module not re-evaluated → ModuleRunner cache not invalidated for that ModuleId.
  • "Tree-shaking removed code I need": bndl analyze --why-kept shows it was deemed Pure / unreachable. Root cause is almost always a missing side-effect contract: bad sideEffects glob, a side-effecting import treated as pure, or an over-eager /*#__PURE__*/. Fix the contract; never disable shaking globally.
  • "Wrong module resolved / NotFound": bndl analyze + -vv resolver probe trace shows the ordered probe list (tsconfig paths → exports conditions → extensions → index → node_modules walk) and where it stopped; compare against the Node spec step.

28.7 Deployment surface

  • Distribution: 11 library crates bndl-* published to crates.io under semver; the bndl-cli binary shipped as prebuilt static artifacts (per-target tarballs) + cargo install bndl-cli. No container needed to run; a Dockerfile exists only for hermetic CI.
  • Versioning: workspace-unified version; bumping any crate's public API (AST shape, Plugin trait, config schema, output runtime helpers) is a semver-major event. The Plugin trait and the config schema are the two most stability-sensitive surfaces — change them rarely, document migrations.
  • Compatibility contracts: output runtime helpers (__require/__esm/__commonJS/__toESM/__export) and the HMR ws protocol are versioned; bumping either is documented in the changelog because it can break already-shipped/cached client bundles.
  • MSRV 1.78, edition 2021; MSRV bump = minor with changelog note; CI pins MSRV + stable + a panic=abort build.

28.8 Feature flags & cache lifecycle

  • Cargo feature flags: default = [build, serve]; optional minify, hmr, differential-oracle (esbuild/Rollup behind a dev-dep, test-only), persistent-cache, trace. Flags gate compilation, never silently change output of an enabled feature.
  • Runtime config flags (config file / CLI): target, format, minify, sourcemap, define, conditions, treeShaking, splitting. Every flag that can change output must feed the CacheKey (cache-key formula below) and the build-report options hash.
  • Cache lifecycle:
    • Key = CacheKey([u8;32]) = BLAKE3(content_bytes ‖ canonical_serialized_relevant_options ‖ tool_version ‖ cache_schema_version). Artifact ∈ {Parsed, Transformed, Emitted}, keyed at each stage so transform-only changes reuse parse.
    • Cache-bust triggers (automatic): tool version change, cache schema-version bump, or any change to an option that participates in the key. A version bump ⇒ old entries simply never match (content-addressed, no manual flush needed).
    • Store: in-memory (single run) backed by on-disk content-addressed store under node_modules/.bndl/ (or --cache-dir); writes atomic (temp + rename); reads verify the BLAKE3 to self-heal corruption (mismatch ⇒ treat as miss, recompute).
    • GC / hygiene: bndl cache --prune (LRU/size budget) and bndl cache --clear; stale entries are harmless (never matched) so GC is opportunistic, not on the critical path. Cache is purely an accelerator — a cold/empty/corrupt cache only costs latency, never correctness (full rebuild always reproduces byte-identical output, §28.5 determinism).

Compatibility Guarantees

Defines what bndl promises to keep stable across releases, what it pins, and the documented escape hatches. Compatibility splits into six independent surfaces, each versioned and broken independently: (1) Rust API (the bndl-* crates), (2) input-language acceptance, (3) output/emit contract, (4) resolver behavior, (5) on-disk cache format, (6) differential parity vs reference tools.

29.1 Public surface vs internal surface

Surface Stability Rationale
Plugin trait (resolve/load/transform/render hooks) public, semver primary extension API; third-party plugins compile against it
Config schema (bndl.config.*, CLI flags) public, semver user-authored; serialized contract
OutputChunk shape (file_name/code/map) + manifest JSON public, semver downstream tooling/CDN/SSR consume it
Emitted-JS runtime helper names (__require/__esm/__commonJS/__toESM/__export) public, semver leak into output; user code/sourcemaps reference them
bndl-ast (Program/Stmt/Expr/NodeId), ScopeTree, SymbolGraph, ChunkGraph, ModuleGraph internal, unstable re-shaped freely between minors; not a contract
Token/TokenKind/Lexer, ParseError/ResolveError enum variants internal new variants added at will; #[non_exhaustive]
  • All public error enums (ResolveError, ParseErrorKind) marked #[non_exhaustive] → adding a variant is non-breaking; downstream must have a wildcard arm.
  • Plugin hook addition rule: a new hook is breaking unless it ships with a default method (fn render_chunk(&self, ..) -> Option<OutputChunk> { None }). Default-None/default-passthrough → minor. Changing an existing hook signature → major.
  • Internal-crate consumers (someone depending on bndl-parser directly) get no semver promise beyond the documented public re-exports in bndl-cli's prelude; this is stated in each internal crate's top-level doc.

29.2 Crate semver policy

  • Workspace versions lockstep: all 12 bndl-* crates share one version (0.x pre-1.0, then unified x.y.z). Avoids the diamond-mismatch where bndl-graph 1.2 pulls bndl-ast 1.1.
  • Pre-1.0 (0.x): minor (0.y) may break; patch (0.0.z) never breaks. Post-1.0: standard semver.
  • MSRV (1.78) bump = minor version, never patch; announced one release ahead in CHANGELOG. Never bumped in a patch.
  • Cargo.lock committed for the bndl binary; libraries float per semver.

29.3 Input-language compatibility (pinned snapshots)

Acceptance is pinned to explicit spec snapshots so "what parses" is reproducible:

Dimension Pin Upgrade cadence
ECMAScript edition ES2022 (TC39 finished proposals as of pin) annual, on TC39 ratification
TypeScript grammar TS 5.4 syntax (parse-and-discard; no checking — see #42) track stable TS releases
JSX spec Meta JSX Feb-2023 snapshot rare; on spec change
  • New syntax beyond the pinned edition (Stage-1/2/3 proposals: decorators-next, pipeline, records/tuples) is opt-in behind a config flag (experimental.syntax = ["decorators-2023-11", ...]) and behind a bndl-parser Cargo feature. Default off → unflagged input that uses a proposal is a ParseError, not silent acceptance.
  • Upgrade policy: advancing the ECMAScript pin (e.g. ES2022→ES2023) is a minor if it only adds accepted syntax (strictly larger language); a major if it changes the meaning of previously-accepted input or removes acceptance.
  • TS-only syntax is always parse-and-discard; bumping the TS pin never affects emitted runtime semantics, only what TS syntax lexes/parses without error → minor unless it removes acceptance.

29.4 Output-target compatibility (lowering matrix)

Target ∈ {Es5, Es2015, Es2017, Es2020, Es2022, EsNext}. Each target is a contract on what syntax appears in emitted code: bndl-transform::Lowering guarantees no syntax newer than Target survives to bndl-codegen.

Feature Lowered when Target < Strategy
optional chaining ?. Es2020 temp-var + conditional
nullish coalescing ?? Es2020 conditional w/ === null || === undefined
logical assignment &&= ||= ??= Es2021 expand to read-test-write
class fields / private #x Es2022 constructor assignment + WeakMap for private
async/await Es2017 scope only — see note; not regenerator-lowered below the floor
arrow / let/const / template / spread Es2015 function expr / var / concat
  • Lowering floor: Es5 is the documented floor; below-Es2015 generator/async-to-state-machine is out of scope (NON-GOAL, matches esbuild). Requesting async lowering below Es2017 → diagnostic, not silent regenerator emission.
  • The matrix is a stable contract: a given (input, Target) pair produces semantically-equivalent output across patch/minor; the exact bytes may change (better lowering) but never the target-conformance. Tightening a lowering (smaller/faster output, same semantics) = minor.
  • EsNext = pass-through (no lowering); its output bytes are explicitly not stable across minors as the input pin advances.

29.5 Source-map compatibility

  • Sourcemap format is delegated entirely to #265 (Source Map v3, pinned). bndl never hand-rolls VLQ.
  • Guarantee: OutputChunk.map tracks #265's pinned v3 spec version; a #265 major bump is surfaced as a bndl minor (new map fields are additive in v3).
  • Map correctness (positions compose lex→parse→transform→print→minify) is an invariant, not a versioned surface — regressions are bugs.

29.6 Resolver compatibility (Node snapshot pin)

Resolution semantics drift across Node versions (exports/imports pattern matching, condition precedence, --experimental flags), so bndl-resolver pins:

  • Node resolution snapshot: Node 20 LTS (PACKAGE_RESOLVE / PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE / ESM_FILE_FORMAT / CJS LOAD_* as specified at that LTS).
  • Documented ordering invariant (stable contract): tsconfig paths → relative/absolute → node_modules walk; exports shadows main/module when present; browser field/condition applies only when Condition::Browser is active.
  • Changing the Node snapshot pin (e.g. 20→22 condition-precedence change) = minor if purely additive (new conditions recognized), major if it changes which file an existing specifier resolves to.
  • Custom conditions (Condition::Custom) and main_fields order are user config, not pinned — user owns the resulting drift.

29.7 Cache format versioning

  • CacheKey = BLAKE3(content ⊕ options ⊕ CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION). The schema version is folded into the key, so a bump cannot produce a false hit.
  • On-disk store carries a schema_version header; on mismatch the cache is treated as cold (full miss), never partially trusted → safe invalidation, no corrupt-artifact risk.
  • Any change to: AST Artifact encoding, default Target, lowering strategy, runtime-helper bytes, or resolver pin → bumps CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION. This is mandatory and CI-enforced (a fixture asserts known inputs hash to recorded keys; an unannounced change fails CI).
  • Cache format itself is internal (no cross-version on-disk compat promise); bumping it is always safe because of fold-into-key + cold-fallback. Stale entries are GC'd by mtime/LRU.

29.8 Differential-parity guarantee

  • bndl pins reference tools for the conformance suite (Phase 13): esbuild 0.20.x and Rollup 4.x (recorded in the dev-dependency lockfile).
  • Parity is asserted on observable output semantics, not byte-equality: same module-group chunk assignment, same tree-shaking decisions on the fixture corpus, same CJS↔ESM interop behavior at runtime. Byte-level divergence (formatting, helper naming, hash values) is expected and allowed.
  • A pinned-reference bump (esbuild/Rollup new release) is decoupled from bndl's version — it only re-baselines the differential corpus; documented divergences are recorded in a PARITY.md exception list with rationale.
  • Determinism is the harder internal guarantee that backs parity: identical (inputs, options, bndl version) → byte-identical output (stable chunk hashing w/ fixed-point cross-chunk pass, stable rename order, stable module ordering). This is a hard invariant, version-independent, required for CDN long-term caching.

29.9 Deprecation & breakage process

Step Policy
Mark #[deprecated(since, note)] on Rust API; ⚠ deprecated in config-schema docs + runtime warn
Grace ≥1 minor release before removal (post-1.0)
Announce CHANGELOG "Breaking" section + migration note before any major
Detect CI: cargo-semver-checks gates the public surface; fixture asserts cache-key & manifest stability
  • Config schema migrations ship a bndl migrate-config path for renamed/moved keys; old keys warn-then-error across two majors.

Testing Strategy

Test discipline mirrors the bundler's correctness contract: every layer (lex → parse → resolve → graph → shake → chunk → emit) is tested in isolation AND end-to-end, then validated against an external oracle (esbuild/Rollup) and a real JS engine. Type-checking is NOT tested (non-goal; #42). Source-map format correctness is #265's concern; here we assert composition + position fidelity only.

30.1 Test taxonomy → invariant map

Each test class pins one or more section-7 invariants (INV-*). A red test = a broken invariant, not a style regression.

Class Crate(s) Asserts Pins
Unit — lexer bndl-lexer token kinds, spans, ASI flag, regex/divide, template stack INV-LEX-NEVER-PANIC, INV-SPAN-EXACT
Unit — parser bndl-parser one test/production, ESTree shape, recovery INV-AST-ESTREE, INV-PARSE-RECOVER
Unit — resolver bndl-resolver one test/algorithm branch INV-RESOLVE-NODE-SPEC, INV-RESOLVE-DETERMINISTIC
Unit — transform bndl-transform one test/pass, idempotence INV-LIVE-BINDING, INV-TRANSFORM-SOUND
Unit — treeshake bndl-treeshake reachable set, effect preservation INV-SHAKE-SOUND
Unit — chunk bndl-chunk module-group assignment, hash convergence INV-CHUNK-MODULE-GROUP, INV-HASH-STABLE
Unit — codegen/minify bndl-codegen rename correctness, DCE/fold, runtime helpers INV-RENAME-SCOPE, INV-MINIFY-SEMANTICS
Integration all full pipeline on fixture projects, then RUN output INV-OUTPUT-RUNS
Differential all vs esbuild + Rollup, shared corpus INV-OUTPUT-RUNS, INV-SHAKE-SOUND, INV-CHUNK-MODULE-GROUP
Property per-crate parse↔print stability, rename, hash, shake soundness INV-ROUNDTRIP, INV-RENAME-SCOPE, INV-HASH-STABLE, INV-SHAKE-SOUND
Fuzz lexer/parser/resolver no panic, no UB, roundtrip INV-LEX-NEVER-PANIC, INV-PARSE-RECOVER
Test262 bndl-parser acceptance/rejection buckets INV-AST-ESTREE
Golden all byte-stable bundle output INV-DETERMINISTIC
Incremental bndl-cache edit-rebuild ≡ clean build INV-INCREMENTAL-EQUIV
HMR bndl-server boundary + module update correctness INV-HMR-BOUNDARY
Perf all criterion gates vs §4 targets INV-PERF-BUDGET

30.2 Unit tests

Per-class enumeration — every branch gets a named case; tables in fixtures, asserted by snapshot or structural equality.

  • Lexer — one case per TokenKind variant + edge: 1_000_000 separators, 0b/0o/0x, 1n BigInt, .5, 1e10, legal/illegal \u{...} idents, every Punct, NoSubTemplate/TemplateHead/Middle/Tail nesting, regex-after-) vs divide-after-), /= ambiguity, < as JSX vs less-than (Syntax-gated), #priv PrivateName, had_newline_before on every line break incl  / , Invalid recovery (lone surrogate, unterminated string/template). Assert span byte-exactness (INV-SPAN-EXACT) and zero panics on a 64 KiB random-byte buffer.
  • Parser — one test per grammar production (statements, decls, every Expr Pratt level + binding-power tie-breaks, patterns/destructuring incl defaults+rest, classes incl private/static-block, async/await/generators, import/export all forms, dynamic import() as Expr::Import). Recovery: BadImport/BadExport/UnexpectedToken produce a partial AST + a ParseError with the right ParseErrorKind and span; parsing continues. Negative cases assert exact ParseErrorKind.
  • Resolver — one case per algorithm branch: relative/absolute/bare Specifier; LOAD_AS_FILE/LOAD_AS_DIRECTORY/LOAD_NODE_MODULES; PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE exact + pattern (./*) + nested condition objects; each Condition (Import/Require/Browser/Node/Default/Development/Production/Custom) including precedence + first-match; imports #-subpath; main/module/browser main_fields order; tsconfig paths+baseUrl (run before node_modules); extension + index probing order; symlink realpath; external + builtin classification; every ResolveError. Fixtures = throwaway temp node_modules trees built per-test.
  • Transform — one case per pass: TypeStrip (annotations/interfaces/enums/generics/decorators/import type removed, .d.ts → empty, value semantics unchanged), JsxTransform × {Classic createElement, Automatic jsx/jsxs/jsxDEV + auto-import}, Lowering per Target (optional chaining, ??, class fields, &&=/||=/??=, async-scope), Define (member-expr + identifier replacement, no shadow capture). Assert pass idempotence (re-running a completed pass is a no-op) and ordering invariants via the VisitMut driver.
  • Treeshake — reachable-set computation on hand-built SymbolGraph: unused export pruned, used transitive dep kept, SideEffect::Impure top-level statement pins its module, /*#__PURE__*/ call dropped when result unused, sideEffects:false enables removal, export-star re-export pruning, cyclic symbol reachability.
  • Codegen/minify — Renamer collision-freedom across nested ScopeTree scopes; runtime helper emission (__require/__esm/__commonJS/__toESM/__export) only when needed; ESM/CJS/IIFE wrapper shape; Minifier: mangle stability, DCE of unreachable branch, constant fold (1+2→3, string concat, !0→true-equiv), dead-branch removal (if(false){}), optional property mangle behind flag.

30.3 Integration tests (build → run)

Fixture projects under tests/fixtures/<name>/ with entry, expected-stdout, config.json.

parse → resolve → graph → shake → chunk → emit  →  node/QuickJS runs OutputChunk → assert stdout
  • Pipeline runs all 12 crates; output written to temp dir; executed under a JS engine (Node when present; bundled QuickJS for hermetic CI) — assert observed stdout/exit, NOT just emitted text. Pins INV-OUTPUT-RUNS.
  • Coverage matrix: ESM-only, CJS-only, mixed CJS↔ESM interop, dynamic import(), export-star chains, cyclic graphs (ESM live binding + CJS partial-export), TS+JSX entry, all three OutputFormats.
  • Each format's output re-run to confirm interop helpers preserve default/named/namespace semantics + live-binding mutation visibility.

30.4 Differential testing (oracle: esbuild + Rollup)

Shared corpus tests/diff-corpus/; bndl output compared against esbuild + Rollup (dev-dependency/feature-gated per Phase 0 scaffold). Comparison is semantic, not byte-identical (formatting differs).

Dimension Oracle Assertion
Runtime behavior both bndl output stdout ≡ esbuild output stdout ≡ Rollup output stdout
Tree-shaking Rollup same set of retained exports/symbols (normalize names)
Code splitting esbuild same module→chunk grouping (module groups), same dynamic boundaries
Interop esbuild CJS↔ESM default/namespace shape matches

Divergence triage: classify as (a) bndl bug, (b) intentional documented difference (logged in a DIFF-NOTES.md ledger with rationale), (c) oracle bug. Only (a) fails CI.

30.5 Property tests (proptest)

Generators produce valid programs (grammar-directed) + structured fuzz inputs.

  • Roundtrip stability — parse(print(parse(src))) ≡ parse(src) structurally (spans excepted). Pins INV-ROUNDTRIP.
  • Rename preserves semantics — apply Renamer with random scope-valid maps; run before/after under JS engine; outputs equal. Pins INV-RENAME-SCOPE.
  • Chunk hashing converges + deterministic — random graphs: the cross-chunk-hash fixed-point pass terminates ≤ N iterations and yields identical hashes across repeated runs + permuted input order. Pins INV-HASH-STABLE, INV-CHUNK-MODULE-GROUP.
  • Tree-shaking soundness vs interpreter oracle — generate program with tagged observable effects (counter writes); run full vs shaken under engine; observable effect trace identical (shaking may only remove provably-pure code). Over-removal = hard failure. Pins INV-SHAKE-SOUND.
  • Define/lowering semantics-preserving — random expr trees; pre/post-transform engine eval equal.

30.6 Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libFuzzer)

Target Input Oracle
fuzz_lex arbitrary bytes no panic; tokens cover input; spans in-bounds
fuzz_parse arbitrary bytes + random Syntax no panic; AST or ParseError, never both-absent
fuzz_roundtrip grammar-valid src parse→print→parse structural eq
fuzz_resolve random specifier + synthetic FS no panic; ResolveResult or ResolveError
  • CI budget: 5 min/target/PR (smoke); nightly 1 h/target. Corpus persisted + minimized; seeded from Test262 + fixtures.
  • Deterministic replay: every crash artifact committed under fuzz/crashes/, replayed as a regression unit test (#[test]) so a fixed crash never silently regresses. Pins INV-LEX-NEVER-PANIC.

30.7 Test262 parser conformance

Parser-only subset (lexical + syntactic; execution + type semantics excluded — non-goals).

  • Buckets: pass (must parse, onlyStrict/noStrict/module/script flags honored), negative (must reject with the declared phase), known-fail (documented unsupported proposal, tracked).
  • Ledger test262-ledger.toml: every skipped/known-fail test has an entry + reason; CI fails if a pass test regresses OR a known-fail starts passing (forces ledger cleanup). Target: ≥ 99% of the parse-relevant suite green.

30.8 Golden-output bundling fixtures

  • tests/golden/<case>/expected/ holds committed OutputChunk code + manifest. Re-bundle, assert byte-identical (pins INV-DETERMINISTIC). Covers chunk naming, content hashes, runtime injection, all formats.
  • BNDL_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 regenerates; diffs reviewed in PR. A nondeterministic diff (e.g. hashmap iteration leak) is a release-blocking bug.

30.9 Incremental-rebuild correctness

  • Property: for any edit sequence, cache-warm rebuild output ≡ clean build output (byte-identical). Pins INV-INCREMENTAL-EQUIV.
  • Matrix: edit module body, add/remove import, change package.json exports, touch tsconfig, change a Define/option (must bust CacheKey). Assert correct Artifact reuse (cache-hit count) AND identical bytes — a stale-cache miss-detection is a correctness bug, not a perf issue.

30.10 HMR tests

Drive DevServer + HmrEngine headless over the websocket protocol; ModuleRunner applies updates.

  • HmrBoundary computation: self-accepting module updates in place; non-accepting propagates to nearest accepting ancestor; no accepting boundary → full-reload fallback fires.
  • Assert: edited module's new exports visible to importers (live binding), state preserved where accepts, correct invalidation set, websocket message sequence well-formed. Pins INV-HMR-BOUNDARY.

30.11 Performance regression gates (criterion)

  • Benches: lex MB/s, parse MB/s, full-bundle wall-time on a fixed large fixture (e.g. a synthetic 5k-module graph), incremental single-file rebuild p50/p99, cold vs warm cache.
  • Gate: > X% regression vs committed baseline fails CI (X = §4 tolerance). Baselines stored per-machine-class; numbers tracked over time. Pins INV-PERF-BUDGET.

30.12 Coverage + CI gates

Gate Threshold
Line coverage (cargo-llvm-cov) ≥ 85% workspace; ≥ 90% bndl-parser/bndl-resolver
clippy -D warnings
fmt enforced
Test262 pass bucket ≥ 99%, no regressions
Differential corpus zero unexplained divergences
Golden byte-identical
Fuzz smoke (5 min/target) zero new crashes

CI stages: fmt → clippy → build → unit → integration → Test262 → differential → golden → fuzz-smoke → perf-gate. Nightly: long fuzz + full perf trend. Phase 13 (## 25) is the done gate: all of the above green simultaneously.

Build and Release Engineering

Goal: every bndl artifact is reproducible, audited, signed, multi-arch. Determinism is a release gate, not a nicety — it backs the content-addressed cache (bndl-cache) and long-term CDN chunk caching. CI mirrors the local make targets; nothing ships that make ci does not already run.

31.1 Reproducible builds

Lever Pin Rationale
rust-toolchain.toml channel = "1.78.0" (MSRV == release toolchain), components = [rustfmt, clippy], profile = minimal identical compiler → identical codegen; no stable drift
Cargo.lock committed for the bndl-cli bin (libs publish unlocked) exact transitive dep set; --locked enforced in release/CI
--locked every CI invoke (cargo build/test/clippy --locked) fail if lock is stale vs Cargo.toml
SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH exported from last git commit time strip embedded build timestamps
RUSTFLAGS -C symbol-mangling-version=v0 --remap-path-prefix=$PWD=/build stable mangling + path-independent debug/panic strings
CARGO_INCREMENTAL 0 in release incremental units vary per host
codegen codegen-units = 1, lto = "thin" (release profile) deterministic + optimized

bndl output determinism is separate from build determinism and is enforced by the determinism job (§31.3): identical entries + config + toolchain ⇒ byte-identical chunks + manifest, including the cross-chunk-hash fixed-point pass (bndl-chunk) and stable Renamer order (bndl-codegen).

31.2 Build / test matrix

              stable (pinned 1.78)        MSRV 1.78           notes
build         ✓ all 12 crates --locked    ✓ (same)            workspace build
unit+doc test ✓ cargo test --workspace    ✓                   per-crate + doctests
clippy        ✓ -D warnings               –                   lint gate, stable only
fmt           ✓ --check                   –                   style gate
fuzz (smoke)  ✓ cargo-fuzz, 60s/target    –                   nightly toolchain, PR smoke
fuzz (deep)   nightly cron, 30min/target  –                   lex/parse/roundtrip corpus
bench         ✓ criterion, regression %   –                   vs §4 targets, perf-labeled PRs
determinism   ✓ build-twice + diff        –                   bin + sample bundle
  • OS axis (build+test): ubuntu-latest, macos-latest, windows-latest. Lint/fmt/determinism run Linux-only (sufficient + cheaper).
  • cargo-fuzz targets: fuzz_lex (never-panic, recovers via Invalid), fuzz_parse (parse_program never panics; errors via ParseError), fuzz_roundtrip (parse → Emitter → re-parse ≅ AST). Seed corpus from Test262 + fixture suite; crashes → minimized repro committed to corpus/.
  • criterion job tracks: lex MB/s, parse MB/s, full-bundle ms vs §4 targets; CI fails on >10% regression (baseline cached per branch).
  • MSRV is asserted both by the pinned toolchain and a cargo-msrv verify check so a dependency bump that raises MSRV breaks CI loudly.

31.3 Determinism job

build #1  → bndl build fixtures/large-app → out_a/   (+ cargo build bndl → bin_a)
build #2  → (cleaned target) repeat        → out_b/   (+ bin_b)
assert    sha256(out_a/**) == sha256(out_b/**)        # chunks + .map + manifest.json
assert    sha256(bin_a)    == sha256(bin_b)           # reproducible binary

Diffs fail the build and upload both trees as artifacts for triage. Covers the two determinism surfaces: compiler output (binary) and bundler output (chunks/manifest).

31.4 Lint, fmt, supply-chain, SBOM

Gate Tool Config / policy
format cargo fmt --all --check repo rustfmt.toml; non-zero diff fails
lint cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings zero warnings; #[allow] needs inline justification
supply chain cargo-deny check deny.toml: advisories (RUSTSEC) deny, licenses allow-list (MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD/ISC), ban duplicate major versions, no unknown-git sources
SBOM cargo auditable build --release dep graph embedded in the bndl binary; cargo audit bin bndl re-scans the shipped binary; CycloneDX exported as a release asset

cargo-deny runs on every PR and on a daily cron (catches newly-published RUSTSEC advisories against an unchanged lock). SBOM verification (cargo audit bin) runs in the release workflow against the final artifact, not just sources.

31.5 crates.io publication

Strict dependency-topological order (a crate publishes only after all its deps are live on crates.io):

bndl-lexer → bndl-ast → bndl-parser → bndl-resolver → bndl-graph → bndl-transform
→ bndl-treeshake → bndl-chunk → bndl-codegen → bndl-cache → bndl-server → bndl-cli
  • semver: workspace-unified version; cargo-release stamps all 12 Cargo.toml, the workspace dep version = "=X.Y.Z" pins, and a vX.Y.Z git tag in one commit. Pre-1.0: minor = breaking. cargo-semver-checks gates the bump on each lib's public API (lexer→…→codegen) to catch silent SemVer violations.
  • --dry-run of the full chain on every release PR; cargo publish --locked per crate in order with a publish-propagation wait between crates.
  • bndl-cli is the only crate intended for cargo install bndl; libs are published for ecosystem reuse (SWC/OXC-style) but the bin is the product.

31.6 Binary release artifacts

Triggered by a vX.Y.Z tag. Targets (signed, stripped, --locked, lto=thin, codegen-units=1):

OS x86_64 aarch64
Linux x86_64-unknown-linux-musl (static) aarch64-unknown-linux-musl
macOS x86_64-apple-darwin aarch64-apple-darwin
Windows x86_64-pc-windows-msvc aarch64-pc-windows-msvc
  • musl static for Linux → no glibc-version coupling; cross-compiled via cross/zig-cc, aarch64 emulated or native runner.
  • macOS: per-arch builds + lipo universal binary; codesign + notarize (Apple Developer ID).
  • Windows: Authenticode-signed .exe.
  • Each archive (bndl-vX.Y.Z-<target>.tar.gz/.zip) ships with a .sha256 and a detached signature; provenance via GitHub OIDC + cosign keyless signing and an SLSA build-provenance attestation. CycloneDX SBOM attached to the release.
  • Embedded version: bndl --version reports X.Y.Z (git <sha>, <target>, rustc 1.78.0); injected via build.rs env (SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH-stable).

31.7 Bundled client_runtime.js versioning

bndl-server's HMR client runtime (client_runtime.js) and the injected module runtime helpers (__require/__esm/__commonJS/__toESM/__export) are versioned in lockstep with bndl and embedded at compile time via include_str!.

  • HMR protocol version constant (HMR_PROTO: u16) is asserted equal between client_runtime.js and HmrEngine; a tests/ check fails CI on drift → prevents a stale browser client speaking an incompatible websocket protocol.
  • Dev server serves the runtime with an ETag = BLAKE3 of its content (CacheKey-style), so browsers refetch only when the runtime actually changes.
  • Minified runtime variant is built (via bndl's own Minifier, dogfooding) and the checked-in artifact's hash is verified in CI to be reproducible.

31.8 .github/workflows/ topology

Workflow Trigger Jobs
ci.yml push, PR fmt → clippy → build+test (3 OS × {stable, MSRV}) → fuzz-smoke (60s) → cargo-deny → determinism. Fails fast on fmt/clippy.
fuzz.yml nightly cron + manual deep cargo-fuzz (30min/target), corpus persisted as artifact, crashes minimized + auto-issue
bench.yml PR (perf label) + main push criterion vs cached baseline; comment regression table; fail >10%
release.yml v* tag matrix build (6 targets) → sign/notarize → SBOM + provenance attest → GitHub Release upload → crates.io publish chain (topo order, --locked)

ci.yml is the required status check for merge. release.yml gated on ci.yml green for the tagged commit; crates.io publish step requires a manual environment approval (protects the irreversible publish).

Performance Engineering

Performance is a contract (section 4 P1–P10), not a hope: every target has a criterion bench, a CI regression gate (scripts/check-bench.sh, >10% wall-or-alloc fail), and a bench-baseline.json. This section is the measurement + tuning methodology behind those numbers.

Benchmark Strategy

Two tiers — micro (criterion, benches/bench_lex.rs/bench_parse.rs/bench_build.rs/bench_incremental.rs) + macro (end-to-end bndl build/serve). Inputs synthetic-but-real-shaped; pin CPU governor performance, isolate cores, warm page cache unless row says cold.

Bench Drives target Input Metric
lex micro P1 (>1M LOC/s/core) TypeScript repo .ts corpus, single thread LOC/s, ns/token
parse micro P1 same; ES2022+TS+JSX mix LOC/s, ns/node, MB/s
resolve micro P9 (<1µs warm) / P10 (<200µs cold) warm vs cold node_modules pkg w/ exports ns/specifier
transform micro per-pass TypeStrip·JSX·Lower·Define each isolated ns/node/pass
emit micro (1 chunk) codegen slice of P2 one ~2k-LOC chunk, ESM MB/s, ns/node
minify micro P8 (>500k LOC/s/core) pre-parsed AST, mangle+DCE+fold LOC/s
build macro P2 (<5s cold 10k) 1k/10k/50k synthetic graph, empty cache, 8 cores wall-clock, peak RSS
incremental macro P3 (<50ms) / §4.6 (>95% hit) 5k–10k graph, edit 1 leaf, warm cache rebuild ms, recompute count, hit ratio
hmr macro P6 (<30ms p95) / P4 (<15ms p95) bndl-server, fs-event→ws accept; req→resp p95 latency
coldstart macro P5 (<50ms) arg-parse+config, before first FS read wall-clock

Graph generator: parametric fan-out/depth (avg 8 imports/module, depth ~12, ~15% dynamic-import edges, ~10% CJS, ~20% re-export/export *) — exercises live bindings, module groups, wrapper fallback, not a flat star. Report p50 + p95 + p99 (tail = see "Tail Latency" below); criterion outlier classification on; commit bench-report.json per CI run.

Profiling Methodology

  • Flamegraph (cargo flamegraph / perf record -g) per macro bench → attribute wall-clock to the §4.1 per-stage budget (resolve+load+parse ~60%, transform ~15%, shake+chunk ~16%, codegen+minify+map ~9%); a stage >1.3× its budget share is a regression to investigate.
  • Parser hot loop (the P1 gate): perf stat -e cycles,instructions,cache-misses,branch-misses,L1-dcache-load-misses. Targets: IPC ≥ 2.0, branch-miss < 2% (regex-vs-divide + keyword dispatch are the mispredict risks), L1-d miss < 5%. Tune: byte-class lookup table (256-entry, branchless is_id_start/is_id_part), keyword match via perfect-hash on length+first-bytes not string compare, #[inline] the cursor-advance, avoid bounds-check via slice-window.
  • Cachegrind / dhat for alloc + cache-line behavior on Module build; samply for cross-platform sampling. Counters captured into bench-report.json so the gate trends IPC/miss-rate, not just wall-time.

Allocator Analysis

Lever Mechanism Payoff
Arena/bump AST per-SourceFile bump region; nodes are arena refs not Box alloc = pointer-bump O(1); whole-AST drop = O(1) region free (§4.2); no per-node malloc churn
Atom interner global dedup, lock-free read / locked insert identifier-heavy code stays ≤ source-bytes order, sublinear (§4.2); Atom cmp = u32 eq
Single-file peak arena reset between files ≤ ~3× file bytes (§4.2)
Resident graph immutable Module, no clones < 6× total source bytes (§4.2)
Token stream no Vec<Token> materialization where parser pulls on demand lexer/parser memory flat in input size

Validate with dhat: assert peak heap < 6× corpus bytes on the 10k bench; alloc count (not just bytes) tracked — per-node alloc creeping up is the canonical parser perf regression and fails the gate.

Parallelism & Amdahl

  • rayon thread pool sized cores−1 (orchestrator reserved, §4.4). Parallel unit = one module.
  • Fan-out: bndl-graph load = independent resolve+parse+scan jobs; embarrassingly parallel, joined at edge-wiring. bndl-transform fans out per module (VisitMut over immutable-input/owned-output). bndl-codegen fans out per chunk. These three stages ≈ 84% of cold-build work → near-linear to cores−1 (§4.1).
  • Amdahl serial floor: graph edge-wiring + cycle detection, shake (worklist reachability, inherently sequential frontier), scope-hoist ordering, and the chunk-hash fixed-point are serial (~16% = shake+chunk). At 8 cores theoretical speedup ≈ 1/(0.16 + 0.84/7) ≈ 3.6× over single-thread for that mix; measured target ~scales to cores−1 on the parse-dominated 10k build because parse (60%) dominates. 50k bench exposes the serial tail — watch shake+chunk wall-share climb; if it exceeds the 16% budget, the serial stages are the ceiling, not core count.
  • No false sharing: per-worker output buffers, merge in deterministic path order (§4.3) — parallelism affects speed never bytes.

Cost Model — Tree-Shake & Scope-Hoist

  • shake: O(reachable symbols + symbol-graph edges) worklist from entry exports; side-effect markers (sideEffects field + /*#__PURE__*/) precomputed at scan. Re-export/export * chase is the blowup risk → memoize per (module, name) namespace resolution to stay linear. Within P7 (<800ms shake+chunk @10k).
  • Scope-hoist (module concatenation): ScopeTree build O(nodes); cross-module Renamer O(references) — collision avoidance via per-scope occupied-set, deterministic short-name sequence (§4.3). Cost concentrated in hoistable ESM; wrapped fallback (CJS/cyclic/eval) is cheaper (no rename merge) but adds a runtime indirection — trade build-time for a marginal output-size/runtime cost, documented per module in analyze.
  • Hoist is net perf-positive on output (fewer runtime closures) but adds build-time rename work; chunk split feeds it bounded module sets so rename cost stays per-chunk not whole-graph.

Cache Hit Impact (Incremental)

  • Warm rebuild: hit ratio >95% (§4.6), targets >99% on 1-leaf-edit 5k graph (Phase 13). Each hit skips parse+transform+(emit) → cost ≈ BLAKE3(content+opts) + DashMap/disk fetch.
  • CacheKey = BLAKE3(content ⊕ canon-opts ⊕ version); BLAKE3 throughput ~3–6 GB/s/core → keying is ~free vs recompute. Incremental cost ≈ dirty-closure recompute + chunk rehash, not whole-graph → P3 <50ms.
  • On-disk store cold-start ≈ warm: re-validate by hash, skip recompute (§4.6). Atom re-intern on load is the only forced miss (~1%, Phase 13 tolerance).

Source-Map Composition Overhead

Per-stage position deltas (lex→parse→transform→print→minify) composed via the #265 codec (never hand-rolled VLQ). Cost = O(mappings) per stage, streamed (not all resident, §4.2). Budgeted inside the ~9% codegen+map slice. --sourcemap=false removes it entirely; minify is where mapping density spikes (every renamed/folded node) → bench emit/minify both with maps on AND off to isolate the codec tax (target < 15% of emit wall-time with maps on).

Tail Latency (Pathological Modules)

p99 ≫ p50 risk on: one 64MB-cap file, deeply-nested expr near the 1,000 depth cap, giant object/array literal, huge export * re-export fan, single-line minified vendor blob. Mitigation: caps are typed errors not hangs (§4.5); parser depth is counted not stack-bound; macro benches inject a pathological module into an otherwise-uniform graph and assert p99 build-time within 2× p50 — a long-pole module must not serialize the pool (it parses on one worker while others drain the rest). Watcher/HMR tail bounded by ≤8,192 watched-path cap with coarse-parent downgrade (§4.7), keeping P6 p95 stable under large trees.

Scalability Analysis

Scaling axis: module count M, edge/import density E, cycle count/depth, entry count N_e + dynamic-import fan-out N_d, max file size S_max. Build cost decomposes into an embarrassingly-parallel front (lex/parse/transform, per-module) and a serial linker tail (graph finalize, tree-shake, scope hoist, chunk hash). The serial tail is the Amdahl ceiling.

Per-phase cost model

Phase Crate Cost Parallel? Scaling ceiling
Resolve + load bndl-resolver O(E) probes, cache-amortized to O(M) Yes (per import record) fs syscall latency; cold package.json/realpath cache
Lex + parse bndl-lexer, bndl-parser O(Σ bytes) linear Embarrassingly (per SourceId) S_max (one file ≤ 1 thread); thread-pool width
Transform bndl-transform O(nodes) per module Embarrassingly (per Module) same S_max skew
Graph build bndl-graph O(M+E) scan + link bindings Scan parallel; link serial symbol resolution across modules is single-coordinator
Tree-shake bndl-treeshake O(symbols+sym-edges) worklist Serial (global reachability) export-star fan-out; deep re-export chains
Scope hoist bndl-codegen O(refs) rename + concat Serial per chunk one giant concatenated chunk = 1 thread
Chunk + hash bndl-chunk O(M·N_e) group assign + fixpoint hash Module-group calc parallel; hash fixpoint serial cross-chunk hash refs need fixed-point iterations
Print + minify bndl-codegen O(nodes) per chunk Per-chunk parallel chunk size skew

End-to-end ≈ T_parallel(M)/p + T_serial(M,E,N_e). With p cores the parallel front collapses; the serial tail dominates past ~p·(few k modules). Target: cold build of M=10k modules / ~5M LOC ≤ 5 s on 8 cores; serial tail ≤ 25% of wall.

Density, cycles, entries, fan-out

  • Import density E: resolve + graph-scan grow O(E); a complete-graph pathology (E→M²) blows resolve cache + binding-link memory. Mitigation: dedupe ImportRecord per (module,specifier); cap diagnostics, not work.
  • Cycles: detection is O(M+E) Tarjan SCC; cost is correctness not time — every SCC member with CJS/eval/cyclic-init forces a wrapper (lazy init) instead of scope hoisting, shrinking the concatenated group. Deep cycles ⇒ many wrappers ⇒ larger runtime + less DCE, not slower build.
  • Entry count N_e: module-group assignment is O(M·N_e) because each module's chunk = SET of reaching entries (static+dynamic). Reaching-set stored as fixed-width bitset (N_e ≤ 64 → one u64); beyond 64 entries → bitvec, O(M·N_e/64).
  • Dynamic-import fan-out N_d: each import() is a chunk boundary ⇒ worst-case 2^(distinct reaching-set) shared chunks ("chunk explosion"). Cap: merge chunks below minChunkSize (default 20 KB) into their importer; collapse identical reaching-sets; emit warning past maxChunks (default 1000).

Memory scaling

  • Resident set ≈ Σ AST + ModuleGraph + ScopeTree. AST ≈ 40–80 bytes/node, ~3–6 nodes/LOC ⇒ ~250 MB for 5M LOC. Atom interner dedupes identifiers (high ratio in minified deps).
  • Arena/bump per SourceFile: each module's AST in its own bump arena ⇒ Send+Sync immutable Module shipped across the pool with zero deep-copy; freed wholesale on cache eviction. NodeId/SymbolId are u32 indices (4 GB ceiling on M·nodes — non-issue).
  • ModuleGraph edges as CSR-style index vectors, not pointer soup ⇒ cache-friendly O(M+E) memory.

Incremental model (the key lever)

  • Rebuild cost ∝ changed subgraph + transitive importers, NOT O(M). bndl-cache keyed by CacheKey = BLAKE3(content + options) memoizes Parsed/Transformed/Emitted artifacts.
  • Edit one file: re-lex/parse/transform that module (O(its size)); invalidate exports → re-shake only if its export shape changed; re-hash only chunks containing it + chunks whose cross-chunk imports reference a changed hash (fixpoint over the affected hash-DAG, typically 1–2 chunks). Unchanged chunks served from cache verbatim.
  • Determinism guarantee (stable module order, rename order, content hash) makes the cache sound: identical input ⇒ byte-identical artifact ⇒ valid reuse.

Parallelism ceilings (Amdahl)

  • Serial fraction f = graph-link + tree-shake + hash-fixpoint ≈ 15–25% on typical graphs ⇒ max speedup 1/f ≈ 4–6×; 8→16 cores yields diminishing returns. Lower f by: parallel SCC + parallel per-chunk shake where chunks are independent; parallel printing/minify (always per-chunk).
  • Hard serial floors: single-coordinator symbol binding; the cross-chunk hash fixed point (must converge before any chunk is final).

Dev-server scaling

  • bndl-server on-demand transform is O(touched modules), independent of project M — only requested modules are lexed/parsed/transformed/served, results cached.
  • Dependency pre-bundling: node_modules pre-bundled once (esbuild-style) → one fetch per dep, not per deep CJS file (avoids O(deps) request waterfall). Cached by lockfile hash.
  • HMR update cost ∝ HMR boundary subgraph, not graph size: invalidate changed module, walk importers until an accepting HmrBoundary, push only that set over the ws protocol; non-accepting propagation to root ⇒ full-reload fallback.

Bottlenecks + mitigations

Bottleneck Symptom Mitigation
Giant single file (S_max) one core pinned, others idle no intra-file parallelism possible; warn > 5 MB; recommend pre-split; arena keeps it memory-bounded
Deep/wide cycles wrapper proliferation, weak DCE, large runtime accept lazy-init wrappers; report SCC sizes in analyze
Pathological splitting (high N_d × N_e) chunk explosion, manifest bloat minChunkSize merge, reaching-set dedupe, maxChunks cap + warn
Cross-chunk hash fixpoint non-convergence repeated re-hash passes placeholder-then-substitute (Rollup-style): emit hash placeholders, single substitution pass — bounded to 2 iterations
Cold resolution cache syscall storm on first build persistent realpath + package.json cache in bndl-cache; warm across runs
Tree-shake on dense re-export hubs reachability worklist thrash memoize per-symbol reachable set; barrel-file collapse

Mental Models & First Principles

Expert-compressed insights. Each entry: what the model predicts, the bug class that forms when it is absent, and the resolving phase/§ where it is applied.

# Mental Model What It Predicts Bug Class Without It Resolving §
MM1 Import = live read-only view, not a value copy Mutations to an exported binding are visible at every import site after concatenation and renaming, not just before. After scope hoisting, an importer reads a stale value because the renamer copied the binding value instead of routing through the same slot. Silent "counter doesn't increment" bug in production. §22 (Phase 8) Renamer + Concatenator; §7 (Design Principle 6.5)
MM2 Over-removal is a correctness bug, not an optimization Removing a call with sideEffects:false that actually polyfills a global causes a silent runtime crash in the consumer's environment, not a build error. Treating SideEffect::Unknown as Pure and dropping the call → Object.entries undefined in the target runtime. §19 (Phase 7) TreeShaker; §6.4 Design Principle
MM3 Determinism is a contract, not a property Any source of iteration-order non-determinism (HashMap, thread-completion order, fs readdir) makes the content hash non-stable, poisoning the cache for every consumer. Two incremental rebuilds with identical inputs produce different hashes; the CDN treats both as new content → unbounded cache invalidation. §21 (Phase 9) hash fixed-point; §6.3 Design Principle; ADR-14
MM4 The chunk = the set of entry points that reach a module (not the module itself) A module shared by two entries belongs in a shared chunk; a module uniquely reached by one entry belongs in that entry's chunk. The set-key is the canonical chunk identity. Grouping by entry-ID instead of entry-SET → same module emitted in N chunks → double side-effects, size bloat, TDZ at runtime on circular shared imports. §21 (Phase 9) assign_groups; ADR-13; P2
MM5 Arena allocation = module lifetime = O(1) free An entire module's AST (lexed, parsed, transformed) lives in one bump arena. When the module is evicted from cache or discarded post-codegen, one free call releases all nodes. This makes the module trivially Send+Sync because it has no shared mutable state. Per-node Box allocation → Drop churn on large modules; Rc cycles → memory leak; non-Send nodes → cannot ship to thread pool without cloning. §14 (Phase 2) bndl-ast arena; ADR-07
MM6 CJS require() returns a value snapshot; ESM import is a live view The two semantics are observable and incompatible without bridging. __toESM + __commonJS model the exact boundary where a CJS snapshot is wrapped into an ESM namespace object with a synthetic live default. Treating all modules as ESM and concatenating a CJS module → module.exports reads stale after first require; treating all as CJS → live bindings break, TDZ lost. §22 (Phase 8) interop helpers; ADR-10; P12
MM7 Source maps are a composition, not a rewrite Each stage (lex→parse→transform→print→minify) emits a position delta mapping generated positions back to the previous stage's positions. The final map is the composition of all deltas, not a new computation against the original source. Forgetting one stage collapses the chain and silently maps debugger breakpoints to wrong lines. A transform stage that rewrites spans without emitting deltas → debugger maps to the post-transform position, not the original TypeScript source. §18 (ADR-18) #265 codec; §22 Phase 8 emit; §23 Phase 11 mangle names[]
MM8 Regex-vs-divide disambiguation requires parser context, not lexer state alone A / character is a regex start after return, (, [, =, operators, or start-of-input; it is a divide after an operand (ident, ), ], number, string, template-tail). The decision is made by feeding regex_context from the parser back to the lexer on each /. A stateless DFA lexer always treats / one way → either every divide is misidentified as a regex (hanging on a/b/c) or no regex is recognized after return /pattern/. §13 (Phase 1) Lexer.regex_context; ADR-03
MM9 Scope hoisting is correct only when the module is "safe" (no eval, no CJS, not in a lazy-init cycle) A module that contains eval can look up any name by string at runtime; renaming its bindings breaks this. A module in a cycle must be wrapped to break the TDZ. A CJS module cannot be hoisted because module.exports is a value snapshot. Hoisting a module containing eval → mangled names are invisible to eval lookups → subtle dynamic reference bugs that only appear with specific eval arguments. §22 (Phase 8) WrapKind eligibility; ADR-12
MM10 Content addressing makes the cache correct across branch switches, where mtime caching does not Checking out an older branch restores old file content with a new mtime. A mtime-keyed cache sees a "newer" file and treats the old content as a cache miss (correct) or, worse, uses an artifact from a different content if the mtime happens to match a cached entry. Content-addressed keying is immune to clock artifacts. mtime cache + branch switch → stale artifact with new-looking mtime → silently reuses the wrong compiled output → runtime behavior diverges from source. §25 (Phase 13) CacheKey = BLAKE3(content + opts); ADR-19

Comparative Architecture Table

How bndl compares against real production bundlers across the load-bearing design dimensions.

Dimension bndl (this blueprint) Webpack 5 Rollup 3/4 esbuild Vite 5 (dev mode)
Language Rust (arena, Send+Sync, rayon) Node.js / TypeScript Node.js / TypeScript Go Node.js + Rollup + esbuild
Parsing strategy Hand-written RD + Pratt; never panics Acorn / custom Acorn-based Single-pass Go parser Delegates to Rollup (prod) / esbuild (deps pre-bundle)
Tree-shaking granularity Symbol-level reachability (SymbolGraph) Module-level usedExports flag (symbol-level opt-in) Symbol-level (AST hasEffects per node) Module-level (no per-symbol analysis in most modes) Inherits Rollup's symbol-level for prod build
Code splitting algorithm Module-group: chunk = entry-set (P2 esbuild algorithm) SplitChunks heuristics (size, count, cacheGroups) Module-group (same algorithm as esbuild) Module-group (P2 reference implementation) Rollup in prod; none in dev (unbundled ESM)
Scope hoisting Hoisted (ESM) + wrapped fallback (CJS/cyclic/eval) Always wrapped (legacy); scope hoisting opt-in (experiment) Always hoisted (ESM-first); wrap for CJS/cyclic Always hoisted where safe Rollup in prod
CJS↔ESM interop Explicit live-binding model; __toESM/__commonJS helpers __webpack_require__ full runtime; __esModule marker interop:auto/default/esModule options __toESM/__commonJS (reference for bndl helpers) esbuild-style for pre-bundled deps
Incrementality Content-addressed (BLAKE3) per stage; O(changed subgraph) Persistent cache (filesystem, content-hash since v5) No persistent cache (full rebuild each run) No persistent cache (full rebuild, but ≤10 ms anyway) Vite: skip unchanged via ETag; esbuild pre-bundle cached by lockfile hash
Parallelism rayon work-stealing over immutable Modules Worker threads on module processing (Webpack 5) Single-threaded Goroutine-per-file (Go scheduler) Single-threaded Rollup; esbuild goroutines for pre-bundle
Dev server Vite-style ESM-native, on-demand transform, ws HMR webpack-dev-server (full bundle, HMR via injected runtime) None built-in --serve flag (basic, no HMR) Purpose-built; ESM-native dev server is Vite's signature feature
Determinism Byte-identical output (BLAKE3 content hash, fixed-point) Deterministic content hash since v5 (opt-in) Deterministic (hash-based filenames) Deterministic Production output via Rollup (deterministic)
Output formats ESM / CJS / IIFE CommonJS + ESM (webpack/module type) ESM / CJS / IIFE / AMD / UMD ESM / CJS / IIFE ESM (prod via Rollup); native ESM (dev)

System-level notes:

  • Webpack 5 wins in environments with a mature ecosystem of loaders/plugins (CSS Modules, WASM, Module Federation) and where backwards-compat with Webpack 4 configs is required. Its chunk-splitting heuristics are tunable knobs that bndl's algorithm rejects for reproducibility.
  • Rollup 3/4 wins for library authors targeting ESM-first output with deep tree-shaking and no HMR requirement. Its algorithm (module-group code splitting + symbol-level tree-shaking) is the direct inspiration for bndl; bndl adds Rust speed and a built-in dev server.
  • esbuild wins for build pipelines that need the fastest possible cold build time (≤100 ms on large apps) and can accept Go's GC pauses on very large graphs. bndl adopts its module-group algorithm and interop helpers but replaces Go with Rust arenas for memory control.
  • Vite 5 wins as a dev-experience framework (not just a bundler): it delegates prod bundling to Rollup and dep pre-bundling to esbuild. bndl builds what Vite delegates: a single tool that handles the full pipeline including a Vite-style dev server.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers derived from the System Constraints table (§4). Each entry shows the formula and source constraint.

# Quantity Formula / Derivation Result
1 Max AST nodes for a 5 MB file S_max = 5 MB; node density ≈ 3–6 nodes/LOC; ~50 bytes/LOC → ~100k LOC; × 6 nodes/LOC = 600k nodes ~600k nodes/file
2 AST memory for 5 M LOC graph 5 M LOC × 6 nodes/LOC × 50 bytes/node = 1.5 GB raw nodes; with Atom dedup (idents ~40% of nodes) → ~0.9 GB deduped ~900 MB AST RSS
3 Source bytes headroom (< 6× rule) RSS ceiling = 6× source bytes (§4.2); source = 5 MB/file × 2,000 files = 10 GB total source; 6× → 60 GB RAM needed — impractical; realistic M=10k modules × 5 KB avg = 50 MB source → 300 MB RSS 300 MB RSS for 10k × 5 KB avg
4 Parallel lex/parse speedup (8 cores) Amdahl: serial fraction f = 0.20 (graph-link + tree-shake + hash); speedup = 1/(f + (1-f)/8) = 1/(0.20 + 0.10) = 3.3× 3.3× speedup on 8 cores
5 Cold build time for M=10k modules P1 throughput target ≥ 1 M LOC/s/core (§4.1); 10k modules × 200 LOC avg = 2 M LOC; parallel front on 8 cores = 2 M LOC / (1 M LOC/s × 8 cores) ≈ 0.25 s; serial linker tail ≤ 25% wall → total ≤ 5 s ≤ 5 s cold build (§4 target P-03)
6 Incremental rebuild for 1 edited file Cache hit rate ≥ 99%; 1 file dirty out of 10k → only that module + its owning chunk recomputed; one module parse at ≥ 1 M LOC/s, ~200 LOC → ≤ 0.2 ms parse + transform; chunk re-emit ≤ 10 ms → total ≤ 50 ms ≤ 50 ms incremental (§4 P-03)
7 Max entry count before chunk explosion Module-group: chunks ≤ 2^N_e shared combinations; with minChunkSize merge, practical limit ≈ N_e ≤ 64 (one u64 EntrySet); beyond 64 → bitvec, O(M × N_e/64); with maxChunks=1000 cap and minChunkSize=20 KB merge → practical N_e ≤ ~50 N_e ≤ 50 before chunk-explosion mitigation needed
8 HMR update latency WS RTT ≈ 1 ms LAN; boundary propagation = O(importers up to boundary) ≤ depth × 0.1 ms per module; transform hit ≤ 1 ms (304/ETag); total path ≤ 30 ms p95 (§4 P-06) ≤ 30 ms HMR p95
9 Dev server transform throughput per request P4 target ≤ 15 ms p95 transform; bndl-transform ≥ 12 MB/s single-thread (Phase 6 target); one request = 1 module × avg 5 KB → 5 KB / 12 MB/s ≈ 0.4 ms transform; overhead (parse, codegen, rewrite) multiplier ≈ 5× → ≤ 2 ms per request warm ≤ 2 ms warm transform, ≤ 15 ms cold
10 Hash fixed-point convergence iterations Worst case: cross-chunk hash cycle depth ≤ number of chunks; each iteration resolves one level; cap at N=#chunks; typical graphs have depth ≤ 2 (entry → shared) → 2 iterations; pathological (deep dynamic chains): bounded to N_chunks (≤ 1000) ≤ 2 iterations typical; ≤ N_chunks worst case
11 Fuzz corpus I/O budget cargo-fuzz CI budget = 15 min/target × 4 targets (lex, parse, roundtrip, resolve); libFuzzer throughput ≈ 50k iterations/s; 15 min × 50k = 45 M iterations/target 45 M fuzz iterations per CI budget
12 BLAKE3 keying overhead vs parse BLAKE3 throughput ~5 GB/s on modern x86 with AVX2; 5 KB file → 5 KB / 5 GB/s ≈ 1 µs per hash; parse at 1 MB/s/core → 5 KB parse ≈ 5 ms; hash is 0.02% of parse cost BLAKE3 keying is <0.1% of parse time

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Staff-level questions. Each names the resolving section, each requires synthesis of ≥ 2 sections.

  1. Why can't the lexer decide regex-vs-divide unilaterally, and what breaks if you assume / always starts a regex? Resolving: §13 (Phase 1 lexer), §15 (Phase 2 parser). Synthesis: the lexer/parser coupling (§13) + the spec's goal-symbol mechanism (S1 §12.9) + what a DFA-based lexer cannot express (ADR-03).

  2. If you add a new ES2024 syntax node to bndl-ast, list every file across every crate that must change, and why each change is load-bearing. Resolving: §14 (AST), §15 (parser), §18 (transform), §22 (codegen emit). Synthesis: the crate DAG (§10), the Visit/VisitMut exhaustive-match invariant (ADR-08), and the printer's precedence table (§22).

  3. A user reports that editing a file in dev mode sometimes triggers a full reload instead of an HMR update. Walk through compute_update and name every condition that causes FullReload. Resolving: §24 (Phase 12 HMR), §17 (module graph importers). Synthesis: HmrBoundary propagation logic (§24), the importer-graph walk (§17), and the "reached root without boundary" condition.

  4. Explain why sideEffects:false in package.json does NOT mean "this module has no side effects" in general — it means "you are allowed to drop this module if none of its exports are used." Give a concrete example where the distinction matters. Resolving: §19 (Phase 7 tree-shaking), §6.4 (Design Principle). Synthesis: SideEffect::Pure vs SideEffect::Unknown (§19), the polyfill-as-side-effect case, and the package-author's liability.

  5. Walk through the BLAKE3 cache key for a TypeScript file. If a developer changes only a comment (no AST change), does the cache miss? Why or why not? Resolving: §25 (Phase 13 cache), §14 (bndl-ast), §18 (transform). Synthesis: CacheKey = BLAKE3(content + CanonOpts) (§25) + the fact that comment-stripping happens in the lexer before the AST is produced → comment change = content change = cache miss, even if the resulting AST is identical.

  6. Why does the minifier run after the Renamer, not before? What correctness property would be violated if you mangled names before scope hoisting? Resolving: §20 (Phase 8 scope/hoist), §23 (Phase 11 minify), §22 (Phase 8 rename). Synthesis: the BindingMap produced by hoisting (§20) is keyed on pre-mangle names; mangling first would produce a BindingMap that maps wrong names and break cross-module live-binding references.

  7. Describe how a cross-chunk ESM import becomes a runtime import {...} statement in the output. Trace the data from ModuleGraph → ChunkGraph → emit_chunk → OutputChunk. Resolving: §17 (graph), §21 (chunking/wire), §22 (emit/formats). Synthesis: wire() records cross-chunk symbol refs (§21), formats.rs rewrites them as top-level import {...} from './shared.js' (§22), and manifest.json maps the chunk filename (§21).

  8. If two ESM modules form a cycle (A imports B, B imports A), which one gets WrapKind::EsmWrapped and why does the first-to-execute module see TDZ-safe bindings? Resolving: §20 (Phase 8 eligibility), §22 (codegen wrap), §17 (cycles/SCC). Synthesis: Tarjan SCC marks both in_cycle (§17); eligibility assigns EsmWrapped (§20); __esm lazy-init ensures the body runs only after both modules' exports are assigned (§22).

  9. The Scalability Analysis says the serial fraction is ~15–25%. Identify the three most expensive serial operations and explain why each cannot be parallelized. Resolving: Scalability Analysis, §17 (graph-link), §19 (tree-shake), §21 (hash fixpoint). Synthesis: (a) symbol binding coordination (single ModuleId interner), (b) global reachability worklist (shared BitSet), (c) cross-chunk hash fixed-point (convergence requires all chunks' final content).

  10. A new bundler contributor wants to add support for import.meta.glob (like Vite). Which ADRs constrain the design, and what is the minimum-risk extension path through the Plugin trait? Resolving: §9 (ADRs), §12 (Phase 0 plugin), §16 (resolver). Synthesis: ADR-22 (Plugin resolve/load/transform hooks), ADR-03 (never extend the lexer for new syntax), ADR-09 (resolver must handle the virtual module), and the "no code in Phase 0" constraint.

  11. Explain the fixed-point hash algorithm. Why is it bounded? What is the maximum number of iterations and how does the maxChunks cap interact with it? Resolving: §21 (Phase 9 hash fixpoint), Scalability Analysis. Synthesis: each iteration resolves one level of hash-reference depth; DAG depth ≤ #chunks (acyclic); ESM chunk graph is acyclic; maxChunks=1000 bounds both chunk count and iteration count.

  12. A property of bndl is that build(x) == build(x) byte-for-byte. List every component that could break this if it behaved non-deterministically, citing the mitigation for each. Resolving: §6.3 (Design Principle), §21 (chunk hash), §20 (rename order), §9 ADR-14. Synthesis: HashMap iteration (sorted), thread completion order (merge by sorted ModuleId), fs readdir order (sorted), clock/RNG in output (forbidden), CanonOpts map-sort (§25).

  13. Explain why export * from "x" must be resolved to concrete named exports at graph-build time, not at emit time. What correctness failure occurs if you defer it? Resolving: §17 (Phase 5 export-star fixpoint), §19 (tree-shaking), §22 (emit). Synthesis: tree-shaking must know which symbols a module re-exports to determine which are live; deferring to emit means the shaker prunes re-exported symbols that are actually live → over-removal correctness bug.

  14. A user runs bndl build twice on an unchanged project and gets different output hashes. Describe the diagnostic process, naming which crate and which data structure is the likely culprit. Resolving: §25 (Phase 13 cache/determinism), §21 (chunk hash), §6.3. Synthesis: diff the per-stage output at each stage (parse → transform → emit → chunk-hash) to find the first stage where content diverges; likely culprit is HashMap in ModuleGraph::modules or SymbolGraph::edges — replace with IndexMap/sorted Vec.

  15. Explain why the differential oracle compares observable runtime behavior (executed stdout/exports) rather than comparing emitted bytes to esbuild/Rollup output. Resolving: §9 ADR-24, §25 (Phase 13 differential). Synthesis: esbuild and Rollup legitimately differ in whitespace, identifier names, helper phrasing, and interop wrapper style; byte-equality would be a false oracle. Observable behavior (stdout, exported binding values) is the actual spec; byte-diff would produce thousands of false positives.

  16. How does bndl handle a dynamic import with a non-literal specifier (e.g., import('./' + name))? What is the conservative safe choice and what optimization is sacrificed? Resolving: §17 (Phase 5 scan CJS/dynamic), §21 (Phase 9 chunking). Synthesis: non-literal dynamic import recorded as ImportKind::Dynamic with Unknown flag (§17); the module cannot be statically resolved or chunked; conservative choice is to mark it as external-safe and not attempt splitting → no lazy chunk for this call, possible runtime failure if the user loads from an unexpected path.

  17. Describe the full ASI decision tree for a\n++b. Does bndl parse it as a++; b or a; ++b? Cite the spec rule that governs it. Resolving: §13 (Phase 1 lexer ASI), §15 (Phase 2 parser ASI). Synthesis: ++/-- as postfix are restricted productions (S1 sec. 12.9.2); the rule: if LineTerminator appears before ++/--, they cannot be postfix → a is a statement, ++b is the next statement. bndl must parse it as a; ++b or signal a diagnostic.

  18. Why must Atom interning be global (not per-module) for scope hoisting to work correctly? Resolving: §14 (Phase 2 bndl-ast Atom), §20 (Phase 8 hoisting BindingMap). Synthesis: the BindingMap maps (ModuleId, Atom) → renamed Atom; if two modules have different Atom ids for the string "x", the map lookup fails after concatenation → unresolved references.

  19. A minified bundle produced by bndl --minify has a broken source map: all identifiers in the debugger show ? instead of original names. What is wrong and how do you fix it? Resolving: §23 (Phase 11 minifier), §22 (Phase 10 emit), ADR-18. Synthesis: mangle.rs records (mangled_span → original Atom) and feeds names[] to the #265 builder; if the names[] array is empty, the map segments lack the names index field → all symbols show ?. Fix: assert every renamed Span emits a names entry in the #265 codec call.

  20. Explain the "chunk explosion" problem. When does M=100 modules with N_e=30 entries produce more than 1000 chunks, and what is bndl's mitigation? Resolving: §21 (Phase 9 chunking), Scalability Analysis. Synthesis: worst case = 2^N_e distinct entry-sets → 2^30 = 10^9 chunks (impossible); practical explosion: many distinct (small) reaching-sets → many tiny chunks. Mitigation: minChunkSize=20 KB merges tiny chunks into their importer; maxChunks=1000 cap emits a warning and falls back to larger chunks.

  21. If a user's project has "sideEffects": ["./polyfill.js"] in package.json, explain exactly what happens to a bare import "./polyfill.js" in bndl-treeshake. Resolving: §19 (Phase 7 tree-shaking), §17 (Phase 5 resolve side_effects). Synthesis: ResolveResult.side_effects = Some(glob_list) → analyze_module checks the resolved path against the glob → polyfill.js matches → SideEffect::Impure → top-level stmt pinned → module kept despite zero referenced exports.

  22. Walk through what happens when the same symbol is exported by two export * from "x" chains and a consumer imports that symbol by name. Resolving: §17 (Phase 5 export-star ambiguity), §19 (Phase 7 tree-shaking). Synthesis: star.rs detects the name collision across two star-export sources and marks it ambiguous; it is excluded from the re-exporter's export table; if a consumer tries to import the ambiguous name, the resolution fails at ResolveExport → ImportRecord records an error, not a silent wrong binding.

  23. bndl targets ≥ 1 M LOC/s/core for the lexer. Describe two specific code-level choices in bndl-lexer that contribute to this target and the alternative that was rejected. Resolving: §13 (Phase 1 lexer), §9 ADR-03. Synthesis: (a) byte-at-a-time cursor over &[u8] (not char iterator, avoiding UTF-8 decode cost on ASCII-dominant JS); (b) maximal-munch punctuator trie (not regex match). Rejected: logos DFA generator (cannot express parser-fed regex_context).

  24. Explain why bndl never persists raw NodeId or Atom integer values in the on-disk cache. What would go wrong if it did? Resolving: §25 (Phase 13 cache codec), §14 (bndl-ast Atom). Synthesis: Atom indices are assigned by a global interner at parse time; the interner is not stable across process runs → the same string "foo" may get Atom(17) in run 1 and Atom(23) in run 2; a cached artifact with Atom(17) loaded in run 2 would reference the wrong string → silent identifier corruption. Fix: serialize strings, re-intern on load.

  25. What is the maximum safe number of modules before Atom interning overhead exceeds the memory benefit? Derive the crossover. Resolving: §4 (System Constraints), Scalability Analysis, §14 (Atom). Synthesis: interner overhead = one hash-table slot per unique string ≈ 40 bytes; benefit = deduplication of repeated idents; crossover = when average occurrences per string × 8 bytes (pointer) < 40 bytes → break-even at ~5 occurrences/string. In JS identifiers repeat many more times than 5× on average → interning always wins above a few thousand LOC.

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

2–4 fundamentally different architectural approaches, each with an ASCII sketch, the regime where it wins, a real system that embodies it, and the reason bndl rejected it.

Alt-A: Go-based parallel bundler (esbuild model)

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Go binary                                   │
│  ┌──────────┐  goroutine/file   ┌──────────┐ │
│  │ entry    │──────────────────▶│ parse+   │ │
│  │ resolver │  MPSC channel     │ link     │ │
│  └──────────┘                   └──────────┘ │
│      GC: ~5 ms stop-the-world on 10k modules │
│      sum types: interface{} + type switch    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Regime where it wins: teams with an existing Go toolchain, ≤10k modules, tolerating GC pauses, and where dev-team velocity and "just works" beats memory-control precision. esbuild achieves sub-100ms cold builds here.

Real system: esbuild (Evan Wallace, 2020–). The reference for this blueprint's module-group splitting algorithm and interop helpers.

Why bndl rejected it: Go's GC pauses are observable on graphs > 10k modules with large ASTs (multi-hundred MB live set). Go's interface{} + type switch for the AST loses exhaustive-match safety. Rust arenas give O(1) per-module free with no GC; enum + match gives exhaustive AST coverage; Send+Sync gives fearless parallelism. ADR-01.

Alt-B: Fully unbundled native-ESM dev-and-prod (no bundling step)

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Dev server + prod server                       │
│                                                 │
│  Browser requests ─▶ transform on demand ─▶ ESM │
│                        (no bundle graph)        │
│                                                 │
│  HTTP/2 push / preload for graph depth          │
│  No chunk graph. No tree-shaking.               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Regime where it wins: projects where every dependency is already ESM-published, target browsers support native ESM with HTTP/2, and the module count is small enough that waterfall depth is negligible (e.g., internal tools, modern Deno/browser apps with ≤200 modules).

Real system: Vite 5 dev mode (partial: pre-bundles CJS deps with esbuild, serves app code as native ESM). Snowpack (2020–2022, now defunct). Deno (no bundler by default).

Why bndl rejected it: (a) CJS modules cannot be served natively as ESM without transformation → pre-bundling is mandatory for any project with npm deps; (b) tree-shaking requires a graph; without it, every export of every dep is shipped; (c) HTTP/2 + 10k ESM requests still waterfall on slow connections; (d) bndl is designed to bundle for production, not just serve for dev. The dev-server component (bndl-server) does serve ESM on demand, but always behind a pre-bundling step for node_modules (ADR-21).

Alt-C: Plugin-first architecture (Webpack loader/plugin model)

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Core: module graph + chunk graph               │
│                                                 │
│  Everything else = loaders + plugins:           │
│  loader: Sass→CSS, TS→JS, WASM→ESM             │
│  plugin: HMR, terser, define, copy-plugin       │
│                                                 │
│  Core knows nothing about JS specifically       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Regime where it wins: large enterprise projects with highly heterogeneous asset types (SCSS, WASM, Worker, SVG, GraphQL schemas) where the bundler must be extended by many independent teams without coordinating on core changes. Webpack's ecosystem of 3,000+ loaders exists because of this.

Real system: Webpack 5. The foundational bundler for the React/Angular/Vue enterprise ecosystem.

Why bndl rejected it: (a) moving JS parsing and tree-shaking into the core (not loaders) is a correctness invariant: live-binding semantics and scope hoisting can only be preserved by a unified AST analysis, not by composing loader outputs; (b) Webpack's plugin surface is extremely complex and slows down its ability to optimize; bndl keeps the Plugin trait to four hooks (resolve/load/transform/render_chunk) and owns the JS analysis pipeline entirely (ADR-22); (c) fewer extension points = smaller attack surface for supply-chain plugin abuse (ADR-28).

Deferred Features Register

Every PhaseDeferred(...) used anywhere in this document (see 1.7) appears here; each anchor resolves to a real heading in this document.

Feature Anchor Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
WASM / native (.node) loaders FINAL_BLUEPRINT §16 (Phase 4 resolver) post-P13, dedicated loader phase a consumer needs compiled WASM/native modules as first-class graph nodes; resolver's Condition matching extends to .wasm/.node specifiers
CSS Modules (scoped class hashing) FINAL_BLUEPRINT §17 (Phase 5 module graph) post-P13, style-pipeline phase a consumer needs CSS-in-JS or .module.css class-name scoping; graph gains a non-JS ModuleKind::Css node
Non-JS asset pipelines (image/font transforms, inlining) FINAL_BLUEPRINT §17 (Phase 5 module graph) post-P13, asset-pipeline phase a consumer needs import img from './x.png' with transform/inlining; resolver + graph extend for binary asset nodes
Persistent distributed/remote cache FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25 (Phase 13 incremental cache) post-P13, CI-cache phase a CI fleet needs cross-machine cache sharing beyond the single-node content-addressed store built in Phase 13
Property-mangling beyond opt-in, advanced minifier passes FINAL_BLUEPRINT §23 (Phase 11 minification) Phase 11 extension benchmark data shows Phase 11's scope-aware mangle+DCE+fold leaves > 5% size on the table vs Terser-class advanced passes (cross-call inlining, constant propagation)

Glossary

Terms are defined precisely + implementation-relevantly: each entry ties the concept to a spine type, crate, invariant, or pitfall. Cross-references use → for "see also". code denotes a canonical type/field name.

Term Definition (implementation-relevant)
module specifier The raw string in import x from "<spec>" / require("<spec>") / import("<spec>"). Classified by bndl-resolver into Specifier::{Relative, Absolute, Bare}; fed to Resolver::resolve with active Condition set. Never a resolved path until → resolution runs.
relative specifier Specifier starting ./ or ../. Resolved against the importer's directory; no node_modules walk. Specifier::Relative(String). Subject to extension + index probing.
absolute specifier Specifier that is an absolute filesystem path (or file://). Specifier::Absolute(String). Bypasses package lookup; still extension/index-probed + realpath'd.
bare specifier Specifier not starting with .// (e.g. react, lodash/fp). Specifier::Bare{pkg, sub}. Triggers node_modules walk + package.json exports/main/module/browser resolution; pkg may be scoped (@scope/name). tsconfig paths matched before the walk.
conditional exports package.json exports/imports map keyed by Condition (import/require/browser/node/default/development/production/custom). First matching condition in declaration order wins; supports * pattern subpaths. PACKAGE_EXPORTS_RESOLVE. Mis-ordering → wrong file; failure → ResolveError::{NotExported, InvalidExports}.
module graph The DAG (with recorded back-edges for cycles) of all reachable modules. ModuleGraph{ modules: Vec<Module>, entries: Vec<ModuleId> }. Built by bndl-graph from entries via parallel resolve+load+scan; the central data structure consumed by shake/chunk/codegen.
module record One node of the graph: Module{ id, path, kind, ast, imports, exports, symbols, has_side_effects }. Immutable + Send+Sync after construction → enables fearless parallel processing. Identified by ModuleId(u32).
